Plus: the add list's variable tail is computed at Phase 0 from the manifest, the leg arithmetic is corrected to **22 across 22 rows**, the `campaign-lib.sh` extraction claim is corrected against the 63-line script it comes from, and both placeholders are resolved.

*(Rev 3 was the fix round on gate r2 — register `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r2.md`; prompt `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-234-round-2-2026-09-11.md`. Its ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 2 → rev 3** section.)*

> **Headline of rev 3.** Five blockers, and four of them are one modelling error: **rev 2's manifest was row→command, and the campaign is not row→command.** An EC row can have **more than one home** (EC-31 is device logic *and* a manual replay; EC-32c/d are two different families), and a browser **leg** can cover **more than one row** (R2 covers EC-1/2/3/4b; R4 covers EC-5/9/10). A row→command model forced rev 2 into an ad-hoc `also:` field for one row and would have made the runner execute R2's destructive leg **four times** against a single-use tenant. Rev 3 introduces two things: **`homes: [{lane, command, evidence}]`**, so a row can have several; and a **`legs:` block** with rows referencing a leg by id, so the runner **deduplicates by leg id and executes each leg once**, with `destructive: true` marking the ones that must never be replayed. The other two blockers are the same honesty problem in two places: the **command-level rerun did not target R0's tenant** — `permissions:sync-fleet` with no selector syncs the whole fleet and a default-connection `tinker` count reads the wrong database under db-per-tenant, so rev 3 pins both to `--tenants=<R0's uuid>` and to `tenants:run`; and the **staging replay did not execute the non-browser lanes against staging at all** — `--api`/`--web` only redirect HTTP, so the PHPUnit, Redis, shell, queue and database rows still ran locally while the checklist claimed a deploy was proven. Rev 3 makes `--staging-replay` **execute each lane against staging explicitly, per lane, or declare it local-only with the staging evidence it cannot provide**. Plus: `--leg` joins the parser so `--reuse`'s single-leg refusal is mechanically expressible; EC-32d's command resolves from its declared `cwd`; the **complete 57-row manifest** is supplied rather than promised; Task 4-2's wildcard add list is named out; the shared-library premise is corrected against what `campaign-onboarding.sh` actually contains; R4's RETIRED arm gets a database access path that works remotely; and the fixture registers **`parapharmacy`**, which is the vertical the onboarding campaign it claims to mirror actually uses.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Turn spec §7's edge-case register from a table a reader agrees with into a **campaign that runs**. Every row gets an executable home in a named lane, every browser row gets a scripted Playwright scenario on the repo's own harness, and every row's outcome lands in one committed evidence document with a run id, an environment, a count and a timestamp. **Exit is the 2026-08-31 empirical-evidence rule satisfied for every row** — a row is done when a command was run and its output recorded, never when a reviewer read the expectation and nodded.

**Architecture:** Nothing is designed. Wave 4 is a **harness plus a ledger**. The harness is the one this repository already has (`scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh` → `pnpm --dir apps/web campaign:onboarding` → a Playwright config with serial legs and a `ledger.json`), extended with an RBAC campaign that follows the same shape: a fresh tenant, a **second company**, a **second `pos_enabled` location**, serial legs, a ledger, and — because this is an authorization campaign — a **deny assertion in every leg that has an allow assertion**. The ledger is what makes the exit checkable: promotion reads the ledger, not the exit code.

**Tech Stack:** PHPUnit 11 (PG + SQLite lanes) / Redis 7 / Vitest (`apps/web`, `apps/pos`) / Playwright (`apps/web/e2e`) / Laravel 12 / React 19 / Tauri 2.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` (**rev 9.2** — ACCEPTED at rev 9 by Codex spec gate r9, register at `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r9.md`; **rev 9.2 = rev 9 plus editorial amendments A-1 (0a-4 → 0b-15) and A-2 (the three re-spelled keys), both already applied to the spec body** — the register is immutable and stays at rev 9). Wave 4's authoritative rows are **§7 in its entirety**, §6's wave-4 row, and §8's wave-4 row.

Its exit checklist is this plan's exit: *"a green campaign run **plus** a clean `tenant:census-day-one` — together the promotion precondition for the whole programme"*, and *"the campaign is re-run end to end on the same tenant, asserting the second pass creates no duplicate role, permission or membership row and that every counter in `permissions:sync-fleet` reports `ALREADY_CURRENT`"*.

## Entry condition

**One, and it is the whole programme.**

1. **Wave 3 merged into local `dev`.** The campaign asserts post-sweep behaviour: one enforcement style, no role-name idioms, the POS permission payload with the ladder gone, `MembershipRole` as a scope label only, and the retired keys absent (or present-but-deprecated, if wave 3's Task 3-7 deferred — the campaign must know which).
2. **Waves 1, 2 and 3 DEPLOYED to staging**, and wave 1's soak complete. The campaign runs against a **real stack**, not against unit fixtures; a leg that exercises `permissions:sync-fleet` needs the command deployed, and a leg that exercises a service token needs the `users` migration applied.

**Mechanical checks:**

```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp
git fetch --all --prune
for lane in rbac-w0a rbac-w0b rbac-w1 rbac-w2a rbac-w2b rbac-w3; do
  git merge-base --is-ancestor "lane/$lane" dev && echo "$lane MERGED" || echo "$lane NOT MERGED — STOP"
done
# staging facts, not repo facts:
#   cat /var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status            → permissions_sync=ok
#   GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health                     → permissions_sync.healthy = true
#   psql … -d tenant<uuid> -c "\d+ users"  | grep principal_kind    → present
#   psql … -d tenant<uuid> -c "\d+ roles"  | grep template_key      → present
```

**Wave 3's Task 3-7 disposition must be known before the campaign is written**, because two legs assert the deprecated keys' state and the assertion is the opposite in the two cases:
```
cd apps/api && php artisan permissions:report-dead      # 0 = retired; 22 = deferred
```

**Overlap.** Wave 4 writes almost no production code, so it collides with little — **but it consumes a tenant and a stack.** It must not run while a manual test day is using the same staging tenant, and the local legs must not run against a worktree another session is editing (vite HMR mid-spec resets a wizard and produces a **false** failure; sequence the editor out first, or build a static bundle with `vite build` + `vite preview` instead of the dev server).

Phase 0 confirms it — see **Q-w4-1**.
- [ ] **Both trailers on every commit**, as the last two lines, in this order:
      ```
      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```
- [ ] **Path-scoped commits, with an EXACT add list per commit.** `git add <explicit paths>`, never `git add -A`, never a glob or a wildcard. Every task below ends with a `git add` block naming each file it stages (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 3**). **The `R0..R9-*.spec.ts` and `tests/Feature/EdgeCases/Rbac/*` shorthands used in the File Structure tables are NOT add lists** — Task 4-3 and Task 4-2 each name their files individually at their commit sites.
- [ ] **A row is DONE when a command ran and its output is in the evidence doc.** Not when a test exists. Not when a reviewer agrees with the expectation. The 2026-08-31 empirical-evidence rule is this wave's exit criterion, and the evidence document is where it is discharged.
- [ ] **Every browser probe registers BOTH captures and asserts ZERO of each.** The **toast** and the **unchanged-row** assertions are **conditional**, because not every leg has a refusal surface and not every denial represents an attempted write (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M4-4**): rev 1 required both universally, which is unsatisfiable for a leg that asserts a nav entry's absence or a switcher's contents.
      | Assertion | When it applies |
      |---|---|
      | zero 5xx, zero console errors | **every** leg, unconditionally |
      | a refusal **toast asserted by text** | only where the leg drives a **UI refusal path** — a button the operator pressed and a message the product owes them. A leg that asserts an affordance is **absent** has no toast to assert, and demanding one would force a fake one to be built |
      | the target rows **unchanged** | only where the leg **attempted a mutation** that was rejected. A read denial, a hidden nav entry or an empty switcher has no row to compare |
      | the target rows **written and correct** | every allow path |
      **Each leg's spec names which of the four it asserts, in a comment at the top**, so a reviewer can tell a deliberate omission from a forgotten one. The capture block itself is unchanged:
      ```ts
      const serverErrors: string[] = []
      const consoleErrors: string[] = []
      page.on('response', (r) => { if (r.status() >= 500) serverErrors.push(`${r.status()} ${r.url()}`) })
      page.on('console', (m) => { if (m.type() === 'error') consoleErrors.push(m.text()) })
      // …leg…
      expect(serverErrors, 'zero 5xx').toEqual([])
      expect(consoleErrors, 'zero console errors').toEqual([])
      // The refusal string is the leg's OWN constant, declared at the top of its spec
      // and resolved from the shipped copy -- never a token in this plan (gate r3,
      // Wave 4 MINOR 1). Each leg's header comment carries its literal and the
      // translation key it came from, e.g. for R2:
      //
      //   // refusal copy: apps/web/src/locales/en/common.json -> errors.last_admin_role_removal
      //   const REFUSAL = /cannot remove the last admin/i
      //
      await expect(page.getByText(REFUSAL)).toBeVisible()
      ```
      **A 403 rendered as a blank screen is a failure, not a pass** — the whole point of a denial leg is that the operator is told.
      > **Phase 0 resolves every leg's `REFUSAL` before the legs are written**, from the copy the refusal codes actually render:
      > ```
      > cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4
      > grep -rn 'last_admin\|admin_permission_floor\|system_role\|service_account_use_dedicated' \
      >   apps/web/src/locales/en/common.json
      > ```
      > **A refusal code with no rendered copy is a FINDING** — it means the operator sees a raw code or a blank toast — and it is raised against the wave that shipped the code, not worked around here with a looser regex.
- [ ] **Every assertion is on DATA MEANING, never on HTTP status alone** (convention 09, `docs/conventions/09-SECOND-OF-EVERYTHING.md:49-50` — *"Assertions are on **data meaning** (the row company B sees, the balance after the second run), not on HTTP status or 'no exception thrown'"*. Rev 1 cited `:52`, which is the *architecture ratchet* heading and says nothing about assertions — plan gate r1, **minor m4-2**). A deny leg asserts the **row was not written**; an allow leg asserts the **row was written and says what it should**.
- [ ] **Locators are role, label or testid only.** A testid-or-label fallback locator takes **`.first()`** — strict mode trips the moment the testid ships.
- [ ] **Never the MCP browser.** Both MCP Playwright servers share **one** Chrome profile and it is routinely locked by another session ("Browser is already in use"). Use the scripted harness.
- [ ] **Playwright `outputDir` lives OUTSIDE the vite root** — point it at the session scratchpad. An `outputDir` inside `apps/web` makes vite full-page-reload the SPA every time a trace or screenshot is written mid-test, producing random resets, lost auth and phantom flakes.
- [ ] **PG lane env — both variables, always:**
      ```
      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 \
      DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w4 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w4_central \
      ./vendor/bin/phpunit <path>
      ```
      **Redis lane:** `REDIS_HOST=127.0.0.1 REDIS_PORT=6379 REDIS_DB=14 CACHE_STORE=redis` — DB 14 deliberately, never 0, and flushed only in `setUp`/`tearDown`.
- [ ] **PHPUnit by path only.** Never the full suite locally. The campaign's PG leg is the largest single run in this programme; it is run **by directory**, and its wall time is recorded.
- [ ] **The campaign is SINGLE-RUN-PER-FRESH-TENANT. `R0` re-registers on every run; `--reuse` is reserved for single-leg debugging.** Rev 1 declared the master plan's *"re-run end to end on the same tenant"* as a leg and supplied no reuse flag, no per-leg reset, no idempotent fixture algorithm and no skip contract — while `R0` always registers a fresh tenant and later legs rename, revoke, deactivate and **delete** state (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B4-2**). `docs/qa/ONBOARDING-CAMPAIGN.md`'s *Reuse mode* section says the same thing about its own campaign: *"reuse mode is for iterating on a single leg, not for a green run"*, and *"a tenant past L5 (locked) cannot re-run L1–L4 meaningfully"*.
      **This plan implements the campaign's declared contract, mirroring that section exactly:**
      | Mode | What it does | When |
      |---|---|---|
      | **default** | `R0` registers a **fresh** tenant, builds the fixture, and every later leg reads its ids from the ledger. One run = one tenant = one registration. | every green run, local and staging |
      | **`--reuse --leg <id>`** (`CAMPAIGN_REUSE_EMAIL` / `CAMPAIGN_REUSE_PASSWORD`) | logs into an existing campaign tenant instead of registering; `R0` skips its fixture census; the runner executes **exactly the one leg `--leg` names** and nothing else. | **single-leg debugging only** |

      **`--reuse` REQUIRES `--leg`, and `--leg` takes exactly one id.** Rev 2 promised a refusal it had no way to express (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B2**): its parser gained `--reuse`, `--lanes` and `--row`, and **no leg selector at all** — while the default browser run contains **nine** legs and `--row` selects **manifest rows**, which is a different axis (one row can reference a leg; one leg covers several rows). *"Refuse more than one leg"* over a flag set with no notion of a leg is not implementable. The parser therefore gains **`--leg <id>`**, validated against the manifest's `legs:` block, and the refusal becomes three mechanical checks:

      | Invocation | Outcome |
      |---|---|
      | `--reuse` with **no** `--leg` | **exit 2**, `reason=reuse_requires_leg` — the default leg set is nine, and reuse cannot run nine |
      | `--reuse --leg R4 --leg R8` | **exit 2**, `reason=reuse_is_single_leg`, naming both ids — `--leg` may be given once |
      | `--reuse --leg R4 --lanes pg` | **exit 2**, `reason=reuse_is_single_leg` — a lane selector adds non-browser commands to the run, which is more than one thing. **Rev 5's parser had no such arm and this invocation exited 0**, which the gate exercised (plan gate r5, Wave 4 **MINOR 1**); rev 6 adds the third `[[ -n "$LANES" ]]` check beside the other two |
      | `--reuse --leg NOPE` | **exit 2**, `reason=unknown_leg`, listing the manifest's declared ids |
      | `--reuse --leg R4` | runs **R4 only**, against the reused tenant |
      **This is where the campaign deliberately goes further than the onboarding one.** `docs/qa/ONBOARDING-CAMPAIGN.md`'s *Reuse mode* documents the caveat in prose and a tester can step past it; this one **exits 2**. A mutating leg cannot be replayed against a tenant it already mutated — and with `destructive: true` on eight of the nine legs, a campaign that lets you try produces a red that means nothing.
      > **`--leg` is also what makes the leg-dedup guard testable** (**B4**): `--leg R2` runs R2's command once and attributes its result to all four rows that reference it, which is the same attribution path the full run uses.
      **The master plan's exit is discharged at the COMMAND level, not the browser level**, and Task 4-5 step 2 is where: `permissions:sync-fleet` run twice more against **R0's tenant**, asserting no duplicate role, permission or membership row, every **mutations** counter zero with `ALREADY_CURRENT`, and `--dry-run` writing nothing. **Those three assertions are what the master plan's sentence actually measures** — it names row duplication and sync counters, both of which are command-level facts — and none of them needs a browser leg replayed. The narrowing is stated here rather than assumed, and it is recorded in the handback as a decision.
- [ ] **A red row is a FINDING, not a test to weaken.** If a leg fails, the fix is a production commit with its own reviewer gate, and the leg re-runs. **Never adjust the expectation to match the behaviour** — that is how a register becomes decoration.
- [ ] **No `origin` push.** Merge into **local** `dev`; the owner promotes.
- [ ] **Worktree:** `git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w4 -b lane/rbac-w4 dev`. Never edit or commit in the shared `dev` worktree.

Any production file, **except as a named fix for a red row**, each in its own commit with its own reviewer. > **`scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh` IS edited, and it is in the Modified table above** (gate r3, Wave 4 **MAJOR 2**). Rev 3's *"deliberately not touched"* paragraph named it while Task 4-3 Step 10 explicitly re-points it at `campaign-lib.sh` and stages it — two statements about one file that cannot both be true. **The edit is a re-point with no behaviour change**, and `CampaignLibParityTest` is what proves that across four cases. What this wave does **not** touch is the onboarding campaign's **specs, its config and its journey/selector modules** — those stay exactly as they are, and the RBAC campaign mirrors their shape rather than editing them.

## Decisions and open questions

Five. All are gaps this plan resolves; none is one of the spec's four OQs, all of which are settled or owner-owed by the time wave 4 runs.

### Q-w4-1 — the `Phase` ordinal

The programme's ordinals, **read out of the sibling plans rather than assumed**: 0a `Phase 0.1.<task>`, 0b `Phase 0.2.<task>`, **wave 1 `Phase 1.<task>`** — a two-segment subject outside the `0.x` series (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:150`, rev 6, used at `Phase 1.1` … `Phase 1.19`) — 2a `Phase 0.4.0` … `0.4.9`, 2b **`Phase 0.5.2` … `0.5.9`** with `0.5.1` unused (`…-wave-2.md` rev 3), wave 3 **`Phase 0.6.1` … `Phase 0.6.46`**, its matrix at **`0.6.47`** and a variable literal-conversion tail from **`0.6.48`** (`…-wave-3.md` rev 5, `:112-115`). **`Phase 0.3.*` is never taken**, and that gap is deliberate. Rev 1 of this plan listed wave 1 as `0.3` (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 1**). **This plan implements `Phase 0.7.<task>`**, which collides with nothing.

Phase 0 confirms it against `dev`'s history, matching **both** subject shapes:
```
git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp log dev --oneline --grep='^Phase [0-9]' -120 \
  | sed -E 's/^[0-9a-f]+ (Phase [0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?)\..*/\1/' | sort -u
```
**Expected:** `Phase 0.1`, `Phase 0.2`, `Phase 0.4`, `Phase 0.5`, `Phase 0.6`, `Phase 1` — and **not** `Phase 0.3`. If an earlier wave took a different ordinal, take the next free one and record the shift.

### Q-w4-2 — **RESOLVED: 58 retained register rows, 57 executable scenarios, 1 WITHDRAWN**

> **Rev 3 left this as an unreconciled discrepancy and then contradicted itself downstream** (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 2**): its YAML example **included** EC-22 while its "complete 57-row" table **omitted** it, and its verification simultaneously required `rows | length == 57` **and** EC-22 to be recorded. Those cannot both hold. **Rev 4 resolves it rather than deferring it, because the two dimensions are different quantities and conflating them is what produced the contradiction.**

**The authoritative counts, and each is measured rather than asserted:**

| Dimension | Count | How it is obtained |
|---|---:|---|
| **Retained register rows** — every number spec §7 has ever allocated and not reused | **58** | Spec §7's lead-in: *"Fifty-eight rows in rev 9"* (unchanged by amendments A-1/A-2) (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md:2095`) |
| **Executable scenarios** — rows with a behaviour to run | **57** | The enumerated ids in §7's own table |
| **WITHDRAWN** — a number retained so earlier cross-references do not silently repoint | **1** (EC-22) | `~~EC-22~~` is struck through in the table with *"WITHDRAWN in rev 2 — not a supported path, so there is nothing to test"* and *"The row number is retained rather than reused"* (`…-catalogue-design.md:2136`) |

**`58 = 57 + 1`, and the grep that returns 57 is correct — it is counting the executable ones.** The strike-through markup on EC-22's id cell (`| ~~EC-22~~ |`) does not match `^\| \*?\*?EC-[0-9]+[a-z]?`, so the mechanical enumeration below returns 57 and **excludes exactly the withdrawn row**. **Run on 2026-09-11 against the spec at `cbc95ecd8` it printed `57` and `1`:**
```
SPEC=docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md

# The register table runs from its header row to the start of section 8. Both bounds
# are DERIVED, never typed: rev 3 hard-coded NR>=2058 && NR<=2126, which is already
# stale against the spec at this SHA and would silently under-count after any edit.
START=$(grep -n '^| # | Scenario' "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)
END=$(grep -n '^## 8\.' "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)

awk -v s="$START" -v e="$END" 'NR>=s && NR<=e' "$SPEC" \
  | grep -oE '^\| \*?\*?EC-[0-9]+[a-z]?' | tr -d '|* ' | sort -u | wc -l      # 57 -- the EXECUTABLE ids
grep -c '^| ~~EC-' "$SPEC"                                                    # 1  -- the WITHDRAWN one
```
The 57 ids are EC-1..EC-21 (with 4a, 4b, 9a, 9b, 9c, 15a, 16a, 18a, 20a, 20b, 20c, 21a, 21b), then EC-23..EC-40 (with 32a, 32b, 32c, 32d, 37a, 37b). **EC-22 is the fifty-eighth, present and struck through.**

**What each artefact therefore contains, stated once so nothing downstream has to re-derive it:**

- **`rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml` carries 58 `rows:`**, one per retained number, **including EC-22 as a `manual` home with `result: WITHDRAWN` and no command.** A gap in a numbered list with no explanation is exactly what an auditor stops on, which is why the withdrawn row is **in** the file rather than absent from it.
- **`RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` asserts `count(rows) === 58`** and that the id set **equals** Phase 0's reconciled retained-id list — and, separately, that **exactly one** row's only home is `manual` with `result: WITHDRAWN`.
- **The green-run gate is over the 57 executable scenarios.** `WITHDRAWN` is a terminal state, not a pending one: it is neither a pass nor a red, and it is excluded from the pass/fail denominator. `NOT_RUN` remains never a valid state for any of the 57.
- **`NOT_SCRIPTABLE` is a different thing again** and is one of the 57: EC-31's operator half runs, as a documented manual device replay with a tester and a date.

**Phase 0 still re-reads §7 and produces the authoritative id list into the evidence document's first column** — but it is now reconciling against a stated expectation (58 retained / 57 executable / EC-22 withdrawn) rather than against an open question. **A campaign whose row count disagrees with its spec is a campaign nobody can audit**, so this is settled before, not during.

### Q-w4-3 — the campaign is a SECOND campaign, not legs appended to the onboarding one

`docs/qa/ONBOARDING-CAMPAIGN.md` is the fresh-tenant promotion gate, with legs L0a..L9 and its own known-red list; a red campaign blocks promotion and *"promotion reads the ledger, not the exit code"*.

**This plan implements a separate `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh` with its own config, ledger and runbook**, copying the onboarding campaign's shape verbatim. Three reasons: the onboarding campaign registers **one** tenant per run and is throttled (5 registrations per window per IP, ~576 migrations synchronously in-request, 20–60 s), so appending forty legs to it makes one run enormous and one red anywhere block both gates; its fixtures are **Tunisia/Parapharmacy-only** and this campaign needs a **second company and a second `pos_enabled` location**, which the onboarding fixtures do not create; and its known-red list is about product findings in the money path, which would drown an authorization finding.

**The bounded alternative** — one campaign, RBAC as legs L10+ — is a real option and costs less scaffolding; it is rejected because it couples two promotion gates whose failure modes are unrelated. **If the orchestrator prefers coupling, the edit is bounded**: move the nine browser legs into `apps/web/e2e/campaign/` as L10..L18, add their ledger definitions to the existing `journey.ts`, and drop `campaign-rbac.sh`. Nothing else changes.

**Separate JOURNEYS, one RUNNER.** Rev 1 duplicated the argument parsing, the preflights, the run-id generation and the ledger mechanics as well as the journey, which is a second implementation of the same campaign protocol (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M4-2**). Separate gates, ledgers and known-red lists are the point and stay separate; **the plumbing does not**. `scripts/campaign-lib.sh` is extracted from `dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh:3-63` and sourced by both, with `CampaignLibParityTest` proving the onboarding campaign's observable behaviour is unchanged by the extraction. Where an orchestrator would rather not touch a live promotion gate at all, the fallback is the **explicit version contract** described in the File Structure's *Modified* table — `CAMPAIGN_LIB_VERSION`, asserted equal by both — and a follow-up lane for the re-point. **What is not acceptable is two silently-diverging copies**, because the first thing to diverge is the exit-code contract, and *"promotion reads the ledger, not the exit code"* only works while both agree on what a ledger is.

### Q-w4-4 — the campaign's tenant is a **campaign tenant**, and there is no teardown

The onboarding campaign records this honestly and it applies identically here: **there is no teardown** — every run leaves its `tenant_<uuid>` database behind under db-per-tenant. `CAMPAIGN_KEEP_TENANT=1` only **prints** the credentials.

**This plan implements the same contract and states it in the runbook**: cleanup of accumulated campaign tenants is an **operator task** (`tenants:list` → delete the `rbac-campaign+…@test.otospex.dev` tenants), and the **push trigger stays INERT** — do not arm a push→dev trigger on a target you cannot clean. The bounded alternative — a teardown command — is genuinely useful and genuinely out of scope for a campaign wave; it is recorded as a residual.

**No teardown is also why the campaign is single-run-per-fresh-tenant rather than idempotent** (Global Constraints, **B4-2**). The two properties are the same fact seen from either end: a run that cannot clean up after itself must not be asked to run again on its own leavings. `--reuse` therefore refuses a multi-leg run outright — `exit 2`, `reason=reuse_is_single_leg` — rather than documenting a caveat a tester can step past, which is the one place this campaign deliberately goes further than the onboarding one.

### Q-w4-5 — which rows can be driven in a browser at all

Of the enumerated ids, only a minority have a **UI** that can express them. The rest are console, database-state or concurrency scenarios whose only honest home is a test lane.

**This plan implements the split by asking one question per row: *can an operator observe this in the product?*** If yes → a browser leg (and, where the underlying rule also needs a unit-level proof, **both**). If no → a lane test, and the evidence document records the **command and its output**, which is the same standard of proof. **What this plan refuses to do is invent a UI for a row so that it can be "campaigned"** — a screen that exists only to make an edge case observable is a second surface (convention 11) and a maintenance burden.

**A third answer exists and rev 1 did not use it: *observable, but not by this harness*.** `R9` was written as a Playwright leg driving *"the dev IziPOS build"*, but the declared config is `apps/web`'s, it preflights only the API and web, it has no `webServer` for a POS target, and **`apps/pos` has no Playwright dependency and no Playwright script at all** (`apps/pos/package.json` scripts are `"test": "vitest run"` and its siblings — plan gate r1, **MAJOR M4-1**). Standing up a Tauri driver is a piece of work in its own right and it is not this wave's.
**So `R9` is declared `NOT_SCRIPTABLE`, with a reason, and EC-31 is covered by two things instead**: `apps/pos/src/lib/auth/__tests__/offlineRevocation.test.ts` (the device logic, automated) and `docs/qa/RBAC-POS-DEVICE-REPLAY.md` (the operator-visible half, a **documented manual replay** whose observations land in the evidence document's own rows). `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` with a named reason and a manual replay is honest; a Playwright leg that cannot run is not. The lane assignments below are the spec's own, with the browser column added.

      grep -rln "EC-[0-9]" tests/ | sort

- [ ] **Stand up the local harness** and prove it before writing a leg:

      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4/apps/api
      nohup php artisan serve --host=127.0.0.1 --port=8011 > "$SCRATCH/api.log" 2>&1 & echo $! > "$SCRATCH/api.pid"

      curl -sS http://127.0.0.1:8011/api/v1/health

      Then register a fresh tenant and **create the second company and the second `pos_enabled` location** — the fixture every convention-09 axis needs:
      ```
      curl -sS -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8011/api/v1/auth/register \
        -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
        -d '{"name":"RBAC Campaign","email":"rbac-campaign+'"$RUN_ID"'@test.otospex.dev",
             "password":"'"$CAMPAIGN_PASSWORD"'","password_confirmation":"'"$CAMPAIGN_PASSWORD"'",
             "vertical":"parapharmacy","country_code":"TN"}'
      ```
      **The password is a generated variable, not an ellipsis** (plan gate r5, Wave 4 **MINOR 4**). Rev 5 left `"password":"…"` inside an executable `curl`, so the block was copy-paste-broken in the one place a reader is most likely to paste it. Generate it on the line above and export it, because `--reuse` needs the same value later:
      ```
      export CAMPAIGN_PASSWORD="Campaign!$(LC_ALL=C tr -dc 'A-Za-z0-9' </dev/urandom | head -c 16)"
      export CAMPAIGN_REUSE_EMAIL="rbac-campaign+${RUN_ID}@test.otospex.dev"
      export CAMPAIGN_REUSE_PASSWORD="$CAMPAIGN_PASSWORD"
      ```
      **Record the email in the handback and the password nowhere** — it is a disposable staging credential regenerated per run, and `--reuse` reads it from the environment of the shell that created the tenant.
      **`vertical` must be a case of `apps/api/app/Enums/Vertical.php`** — read it, do not guess. `Vertical.php` at `dev` declares twelve cases including **both** `Pharmacy = 'pharmacy'` (`:20`) and `Parapharmacy = 'parapharmacy'` (`:30`), so `pharmacy` — which rev 2 posted — is a *valid* value and the **wrong** one (plan gate r2, **minor 1**).
      **It must be `parapharmacy`, because that is the fixture this campaign claims to mirror.** `docs/qa/ONBOARDING-CAMPAIGN.md:22` states the onboarding campaign *"registers the **Parapharmacy** vertical (batch tracking on by default, which the expiry leg needs) with **Tunisia** fixtures"*, and its journey asserts it in code: `apps/web/e2e/campaign/selectors.ts:85` matches `/parapharmacy|parapharmacie/i` and `apps/web/e2e/campaign/journey.ts:317,325` requires it (`required_vertical: 'parapharmacy'`, *"Parapharmacy is required for the expiry campaign leg"*). Q-w4-3's whole argument for a second campaign is that it **copies the onboarding campaign's shape**; registering a different vertical would silently give the two gates different module sets — and **EC-29's module-gating leg (`R7`) depends on which modules the vertical enables**, so the difference is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.
- [ ] **Start Redis DB 14** for EC-28 and confirm it is empty (`redis-cli -n 14 DBSIZE` → 0); record `redis_version` (the atomic read-and-clear's `GETDEL` form needs 6.2+, though the Lua form works on any).
- [ ] **Confirm the lane cap (3)** and that **no manual test day overlaps** — the campaign consumes a staging tenant, and a tester on the same tenant invalidates both.

## Task 4-1: the evidence document, written FIRST

**Files:** `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-edge-campaign-evidence.md`, `docs/qa/RBAC-EDGE-CAMPAIGN.md`, `docs/qa/RBAC-POS-DEVICE-REPLAY.md`, `docs/handoff/PROMOTION-CHECKLIST-2026-08-26.md` — **every path resolved, no `20xx-xx` and no wildcard** (plan gate r1, **minor m4-1**)

**Why first.** The document is the exit criterion. Writing it last turns it into a summary of what happened; writing it first turns it into a **checklist that drives the work** and makes an unrun row visibly unrun rather than quietly absent.

> **The 58 rows are GENERATED, not stubbed** (plan gate r6, Wave 4 **MAJOR 1**). Rev 6 printed `EC-1` followed by `| … |` and told the implementer the table *"contains one row per retained id"* — but the other 57 row definitions are known **at plan time**: the manifest already names each id's lane, command and evidence. `<runId>`, `Result`, `Timestamp` and the evidence link are the only columns a run can fill, and only those are left blank. **`apps/api/tools/generate-evidence-rows.php` is supplied below**, its output was produced from this plan's own manifest on 2026-09-11 (**58 lines**, the six above and the three below verbatim from that run), and its `--check` mode is the coverage assertion: it exits **2** naming the drifted ids, which is how the evidence document and the manifest are prevented from disagreeing.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

/*
 * apps/api/tools/generate-evidence-rows.php
 *
 * Emits the `## Rows` table body of
 * docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-edge-campaign-evidence.md -- ONE ROW PER
 * REGISTER ID, in the manifest's declared order, with `Row`, `Lane` and `Home` filled
 * from the manifest and the run-time columns left blank for a real run to fill.
 *
 * Task 4-1 says the first column is GENERATED from the reconciled list; rev 6 printed
 * `EC-1` and then `| … |` (plan gate r6, Wave 4 MAJOR 1), which is a stub, not a
 * template: the other 57 row definitions are known at plan time and only <runId>,
 * Result and the asserted value are not.
 *
 *   php tools/generate-evidence-rows.php                 # prints the table body
 *   php tools/generate-evidence-rows.php --check FILE    # exits 2 if FILE's rows drift
 */

require __DIR__.'/../vendor/autoload.php';

use Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml;

$manifest = __DIR__.'/../tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml';
$m = Yaml::parseFile($manifest);

// Phase 0's reconciled register list. When it carries a one-line description per id the
// `Row` column is filled from it; otherwise the column is left blank for the run to fill.
// It is never INVENTED here -- the spec's §7 wording is the register's, not this tool's.
$idsFile = __DIR__.'/../tests/EdgeCases/rbac-register-ids.json';
/** @var array<string, string> $describe */
$describe = [];

if (is_file($idsFile)) {
    /** @var mixed $decoded */
    $decoded = json_decode((string) file_get_contents($idsFile), true);

    if (is_array($decoded)) {
        foreach ($decoded as $key => $value) {
            if (is_string($key) && is_string($value)) {
                $describe[$key] = $value;
            }
        }
    }
}

/** @var list<string> $lines */
$lines = [];
/** @var list<string> $ids */
$ids = [];

foreach ($m['rows'] as $row) {
    $ids[] = $row['id'];

    $lanes = [];
    $homes = [];

    foreach ($row['homes'] as $home) {
        if (isset($home['leg'])) {
            $lanes[] = 'browser';
            $homes[] = $home['leg'];

            continue;
        }

        $lane = $home['lane'] ?? '?';
        $lanes[] = $lane;

        if ($lane === 'manual') {
            $homes[] = $home['doc'] ?? ($home['result'] ?? 'manual');

            continue;
        }

        // The HOME is the file::test the command names, not the whole command line.
        $cmd = (string) ($home['command'] ?? '');
        $file = preg_match('#(\S+\.(?:php|ts|sh))#', $cmd, $mm) === 1 ? basename($mm[1]) : $cmd;
        $filter = preg_match('#--filter\s+(\S+)#', $cmd, $mf) === 1 ? '::'.$mf[1] : '';
        $homes[] = '`'.$file.$filter.'`';
    }

    $evidence = (string) ($row['homes'][0]['evidence'] ?? ($row['homes'][0]['result'] ?? ''));
    $replay = ($row['staging_replay'] ?? null) === 'required' ? ' **[staging replay]**' : '';

    $lines[] = sprintf(
        '| %s | %s | %s | %s | | PASS/FAIL/NOT_RUN | %s | | |',
        $row['id'],
        $describe[$row['id']] ?? '',
        implode(' + ', array_unique($lanes)).$replay,
        implode(' + ', $homes),
        $evidence,
    );
}

if (($argv[1] ?? '') === '--check') {
    $doc = (string) file_get_contents((string) $argv[2]);
    preg_match_all('/^\| (EC-[0-9a-z]+) \|/m', $doc, $found);
    $drift = array_merge(array_diff($ids, $found[1]), array_diff($found[1], $ids));

    if ($drift !== []) {
        fwrite(STDERR, 'evidence rows drift from the manifest: '.implode(', ', $drift)."\n");
        exit(2);
    }

    fwrite(STDOUT, 'evidence rows == manifest ids ('.count($ids)." rows)\n");
    exit(0);
}

echo implode("\n", $lines), "\n";
~~~

> **Exercised, both directions** (fake `apps/api` tree, the real `symfony/yaml` from `apps/api/vendor`, this plan's pasted manifest):
>
| Invocation | Result |
|---|---|
| `php tools/generate-evidence-rows.php` | **58 lines**, one per register id, in manifest order, EC-1 … EC-40 |
| `php tools/generate-evidence-rows.php --check <the generated document>` | exit **0**, `evidence rows == manifest ids (58 rows)` |
| the same, with the `EC-20b` line deleted | exit **2**, `evidence rows drift from the manifest: EC-20b` |
>
> **`Row` comes from `rbac-register-ids.json` when Phase 0's reconciled list carries a description, and is left BLANK otherwise** — the tool never invents the register's wording. **`Count / value asserted` is the manifest's own `evidence:` string**, which is already the data meaning the row is about, so the two artefacts cannot describe the same row differently.

**`Result` is one of PASS, FAIL, NOT_RUN, WITHDRAWN, or NOT_SCRIPTABLE with a stated reason.** `NOT_RUN` is never a pass. **`WITHDRAWN`** is EC-22 and only EC-22. **`NOT_SCRIPTABLE`** requires both a reason and, where a manual replay covers the row, that replay's document, tester and date — EC-31's operator half is the one such row this plan ships.
**`Home` for a row whose test waves 0a–3 already shipped is a POINTER, not a copy** — the file and the test method, exactly as `rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml` names them. **The manifest is the source**: `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` asserts the manifest's ids equal the register's, and this table's first column is generated from the same list, so the two cannot drift.
**`Count / value asserted` is the DATA MEANING** the row is about — a row count, a grant set, a suppressed_count, a status file's contents. Not "200 OK".

| # | Row | What happened | Fix commit | Re-run id | Result after fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Staging replay

| Row | Replayed on staging? | Run id | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — reconcile against Phase 0's coverage delta.** For every row that **already** has a named test from waves 0a–3, wave 4 writes **no code**: it records the file, the test method and the run output in the evidence document. **The temptation to re-write a covered row as a campaign leg is the main way this wave becomes six weeks long**; resist it, and let the evidence row point at the existing test.
- [ ] **Step 2 — write the missing homes only.** Each new test goes under `apps/api/tests/Feature/EdgeCases/Rbac/` (or the POS/web equivalent) named for its row, and each asserts **data meaning**. A row whose home would be a duplicate of an existing test **gets the pointer, not a copy**.
- [ ] **Step 3 — EC-22 is recorded as WITHDRAWN, not as missing.** Its number is retained by the spec; the evidence row says *"withdrawn in rev 2, number retained — no scenario"*. A gap in a numbered list with no explanation is exactly what an auditor stops on.
- [ ] **Step 4 — write `apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml`, the row → lane → command → evidence map.** Rev 1 said *"existing tests are referenced, not copied"* and then gave the runner a command that ran only the **new** directory, one Redis test and two broad frontend folders — so most existing EC homes had no command at all and the script's promise to run *"the PHPUnit lanes"* was unbacked (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B4-3**).
      **The manifest is the runner's input and the evidence document's spine.** One entry per enumerated register id; **nothing runs that is not in it, and nothing is in it that does not run**.
      > **Rev 3 changes the manifest's SHAPE, twice, because rev 2's row→command model cannot express the campaign** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKERs B3 and B4**).
      >
      > **B3 — a row can have SEVERAL homes.** Rev 2's schema had one scalar `lane` and one scalar `command`. But EC-5 is PG **and** vitest **and** a browser leg; EC-14 is vitest **and** playwright; EC-28 is Redis **and** PG; EC-31 is pos-vitest **and** a manual replay; EC-32c and EC-32d are two different families the lane table itself splits. Rev 2 noticed this for exactly one row and bolted on an ad-hoc `also:` field — which its own EC-31 entry then used **without** a `command`, so the second home ran nothing. **`homes:` is a LIST**, and the runner executes every element.
      >
      > **B4 — a LEG can cover several rows, and must run ONCE.** R2 covers EC-1, EC-2, EC-3 and EC-4b; R4 covers EC-5, EC-9 and EC-10; R8 covers EC-16, EC-18, EC-32b and EC-34. Under a row→command model with a runner that *"executes every row's command"*, **R2's spec would be invoked four times** — and R2 deletes and deactivates principals on a **single-use, no-teardown tenant** (Q-w4-4), so runs two through four would fail against state run one destroyed, producing three reds that mean nothing. **Legs are a first-class entity with their own ids**; a row references one by `leg:`; the runner **deduplicates by leg id** and executes each leg exactly once, in declared order; `destructive: true` marks the legs that can never be re-run.

      **The COMPLETE manifest, VALIDATED.** Rev 3's excerpt stopped at a prose ellipsis and was followed by a Markdown summary table rather than the remaining `rows:` objects — and the excerpt itself **did not parse**, because `playwright:{cwd: apps/web, env: []}` is a malformed flow mapping (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 1**). Rev 4 prints the whole file, and it was parsed before it was pasted:

      ```
      $ python3 -c "import yaml,sys; d=yaml.safe_load(open('rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml')); print(d['version'], len(d['lanes']), len(d['legs']), len(d['rows']))"
      2 8 9 58

      $ cd apps/api && M=<path> php -r 'require "vendor/autoload.php";
        $d = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["M"]);
        printf("symfony/yaml OK — version=%s lanes=%d legs=%d rows=%d\n", $d["version"], count($d["lanes"]), count($d["legs"]), count($d["rows"]));'
      symfony/yaml OK — version=2 lanes=8 legs=9 rows=58
      ```
      **Both parsers**, because `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` reads it with `symfony/yaml` (already in `apps/api/vendor`) while the runner and any ad-hoc inspection use Python's. A file that one accepts and the other rejects is a file nobody can trust. The invariants below were asserted mechanically on the parsed structure, not counted by eye:

      | Invariant | Asserted value |
      |---|---:|
      | `rows` | **58** |
      | rows with at least one non-`manual` home (**executable scenarios**) | **57** |
      | rows whose only home is `manual` with `result: WITHDRAWN` | **1** (EC-22) |
      | `legs` declared | **9** |
      | **leg references**, and the rows carrying them | **22 across 22 rows** |
      | per-leg reference counts | R2:4 · R3:3 · R4:6 · R5:2 · R6:2 · R7:1 · R8:4 · R0:0 · R1:0 |
      | `legs[].covers` equals the set of rows that actually reference it | **true for all nine** |
      | every row has ≥1 home; every `manual` home carries a `result` | **true** |
      | rows carrying `staging_replay: required` | **19** |

      ```yaml
      # apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml
      # Consumed by scripts/campaign-rbac.sh. Every `command` is relative to its lane's `cwd`.
      #
      # COUNTS, stated once so nothing downstream has to re-derive them (gate r3, W4 BLOCKER 2):
      #   58  register rows in this file   == spec 7's retained register (EC-22's number is retained)
      #   57  EXECUTABLE rows              == rows with at least one non-manual home
      #    1  WITHDRAWN row                == EC-22, `result: WITHDRAWN`, no command
      #    9  legs declared                == R0..R8
      #   22  leg references across 22 rows (NOT 31; gate r3, W4 MAJOR 3). R0 and R1 are
      #       referenced by no row and declare `covers: []`; the other seven are referenced
      #       by R2:4, R3:3, R4:6, R5:2, R6:2, R7:1, R8:4 rows.
      version: 2

      # LANE ENVIRONMENT -- `env:` is the LOCAL value set, `staging_env:` the per-key
      # overrides that apply under --staging-replay. THE RUNNER COMPOSES THE TWO
      # MECHANICALLY and emits the result as a shell-escaped assignment prefix on the
      # remote command; NOTHING is hand-written into a wrapper (gate r6, W4 BLOCKER 1).
      # Rev 6's wrappers hand-listed DB_DATABASE / DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE / REDIS_DB and
      # silently dropped DB_CONNECTION, the host, the port and CACHE_STORE -- and since
      # the generated command is an `ssh` command, the runner's own local `export` never
      # reached the remote shell at all. The composition is a pure function of this map,
      # so a key added here reaches staging without a code change.
      #
      # Every entry must match `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*=[A-Za-z0-9._:/-]*$`, asserted by
      # RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest rule 7: the composer splits on whitespace and
      # must never meet a glob, a space or a shell metacharacter in a VALUE.
      lanes:
        pg:
          cwd: apps/api
          env:
            - DB_CONNECTION=pgsql
            - DB_HOST=127.0.0.1
            - DB_PORT=5433
            - DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w4
            - DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w4_central
          # DB_CONNECTION is NOT overridden and therefore TRAVELS: `pgsql` reads
          # DB_DATABASE (dev:apps/api/config/database.php:87-99), so the isolated
          # database is the one the lane actually opens. Without it the deployed
          # DB_CONNECTION=central wins and the lane reads DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE instead
          # (:105-133), which is exactly how rev 6 could have run every PG row against
          # the deployed databases and reported `execution_target=staging:ssh`.
          staging_env:
            - DB_HOST=127.0.0.1
            - DB_PORT=5432
            - DB_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign
            - DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign_central
        sqlite:
          cwd: apps/api
          env: []
          # No staging arm: no `staging_replay: required` row has a sqlite home, and the
          # lane falls to the runner's `local` arm, which a replay run forces to FAIL.
          staging_env: []
        redis:
          cwd: apps/api
          env:
            - REDIS_HOST=127.0.0.1
            - REDIS_PORT=6379
            - REDIS_DB=14
            - CACHE_STORE=redis
          # The command executes ON the staging host inside the ssh session, so
          # 127.0.0.1:6379 is already correct and this lane overrides nothing. All four
          # keys travel -- including CACHE_STORE, which rev 6's hand-written `REDIS_DB=14`
          # wrapper dropped along with the host and the port.
          staging_env: []
        vitest:
          cwd: apps/web
          env: []
          staging_env: []
        posvitest:
          cwd: apps/pos
          env: []
          staging_env: []
        shell:
          # apps/api, not "." -- EC-32d's script is staged at apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/
          # and its command resolves from the lane's cwd (gate r2, MAJOR 1).
          cwd: apps/api
          env: []
          # This lane runs through `docker exec`, which does not inherit the ssh session's
          # environment. The composed map is emitted as `-e K=V` flags by the SAME
          # composer, so an env added here reaches the container with no code change.
          staging_env: []
        playwright:
          cwd: apps/web
          env: []
          staging_env: []
        manual:
          # No command. The evidence is a replay document or a stated WITHDRAWN result.
          cwd: .
          env: []
          staging_env: []

      # LEGS -- the browser journeys, declared ONCE with their own ids. A row points at a leg
      # with `leg:`; the runner dedups by leg id and runs each exactly once, in this order.
      legs:
        - id: R0
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R0-fixture.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: []
        - id: R1
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R1-second-company.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: false
          covers: []
        - id: R2
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R2-last-admin.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-1, EC-2, EC-3, EC-4b]
        - id: R3
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R3-role-management.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-11, EC-12, EC-13]
        - id: R4
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R4-template-drift.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-5, EC-9, EC-9a, EC-9b, EC-9c, EC-10]
        - id: R5
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R5-stale-cache-and-switch.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-14, EC-17]
        - id: R6
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R6-location-scope.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-15, EC-15a]
        - id: R7
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R7-module-gating.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-29]
        - id: R8
          command: pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.rbac.config.ts e2e/rbac-campaign/R8-service-accounts.spec.ts
          lane: playwright
          destructive: true
          covers: [EC-16, EC-18, EC-32b, EC-34]

      # ROWS -- one per retained register id, 58 of them, in register order.
      # `homes` is a LIST and every element runs. `--testdox` is in every PHPUnit command
      # deliberately: the evidence document quotes test names, and a testdox line is the
      # row's proof in a form a human can read.
      rows:
        - id: EC-1
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter removing_the_admin_role_from_the_last_admin_is_refused tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "model_has_roles rows for the admin role before=1 after=1"
            - leg: R2
              evidence: "refusal toast matched; the pivot row is still present after reload"
        - id: EC-2
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter deactivating_the_last_admin_is_refused tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "users.status unchanged"
            - leg: R2
              evidence: "users.status unchanged after the refusal; toast text matched"
        - id: EC-3
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter deleting_the_last_admin_is_refused tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "the users row still exists after the refusal"
            - leg: R2
              evidence: "the principal is still listed after reload"
        - id: EC-4
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_service_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "A = {} for a service-only admin population; deactivating the human admin is refused"
          note: "No leg. R2 drives a sole HUMAN admin's four refusals; it neither creates a service admin nor strips a membership (gate r1, M4-3)."
        - id: EC-4a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_membershipless_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "a membershipless admin is not counted in A; the removal is refused"
          note: "No leg, same reason as EC-4."
        - id: EC-4b
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter update_with_a_different_role_cannot_empty_the_admin_population tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "syncRoles replacing the only admin's role is refused; the pivot row is unchanged"
            - leg: R2
              evidence: "the role select refuses; the badge still reads admin after reload"
        - id: EC-5
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionsSyncTemplateDeltaTest.php
              evidence: "a customised role's non-template grant survives the delta apply"
            - lane: vitest
              command: pnpm test -- src/features/settings/__tests__/RolesPage.test.tsx
              evidence: "a deprecated grant renders struck through"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "BRANCHED -- Task 4-3 Step 4. The evidence row records WHICH arm ran (RETIRED or DEFERRED)."
        - id: EC-6
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionRenameTest.php
              evidence: "the renamed key's grants move; no role loses an ability"
        - id: EC-7
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter rename_target_already_exists_is_blocked tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionsSyncTest.php
              evidence: "exit non-zero, reason=rename_target_exists, zero rows written"
        - id: EC-8
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionCacheTenantScopingTest.php
              evidence: "tenant B's cached permission set is untouched by tenant A's sync"
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter the_cache_is_flushed_after_commit_not_before tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionsSyncTest.php
              evidence: "the flush is registered on afterCommit; a rolled-back sync flushes nothing"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-9
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionSyncAdoptionTest.php
              evidence: "a legacy role is adopted, keyed by template_key, exactly once"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "the 'N template updates not applied' banner renders with the real N"
        - id: EC-9a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_customised_role_is_not_silently_reset tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionSyncAdoptionTest.php
              evidence: "templates_skipped_customised gauge increments; the role's grants are unchanged"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "the banner names the customised role"
        - id: EC-9b
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter an_uncustomised_role_receives_the_template tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionSyncAdoptionTest.php
              evidence: "the pristine role's grants equal the template's after the run"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "the pristine role shows no drift badge"
        - id: EC-9c
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_tenant_that_never_received_the_lane_delta tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionSyncAdoptionTest.php
              evidence: "the pre-delta tenant adopts without a collision"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "no error surface on a pre-delta tenant"
        - id: EC-10
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/TemplateReapplyTest.php
              evidence: "re-apply on a pristine role is a no-op: zero mutations, customised_at unchanged"
            - leg: R4
              evidence: "Re-apply template on a pristine role reports no change"
        - id: EC-11
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_system_role_cannot_be_renamed tests/Feature/Identity/SystemRoleProtectionTest.php
              evidence: "422; roles.name unchanged"
            - leg: R3
              evidence: "the rename control is absent for a system role; the API refuses if forced"
        - id: EC-12
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_system_role_cannot_be_deleted tests/Feature/Identity/SystemRoleProtectionTest.php
              evidence: "422; the roles row still exists"
            - lane: vitest
              command: pnpm test -- src/features/settings/__tests__/RolesPage.test.tsx
              evidence: "the delete affordance is absent on a system role row"
            - leg: R3
              evidence: "the delete affordance is absent; the role survives a reload"
        - id: EC-13
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/AdminPermissionFloorTest.php
              evidence: "emptying the admin role's permissions is refused 422 and lands a RoleUpdateRefused audit row"
            - leg: R3
              evidence: "the refusal surfaces; the admin role's grant count is unchanged after reload"
        - id: EC-14
          homes:
            - lane: vitest
              command: pnpm test -- src/hooks/__tests__/usePermissions.companySwitch.test.tsx
              evidence: "the permission query key is re-keyed on company switch; the old company's set is not served"
            - leg: R5
              evidence: "switching company re-renders the sidebar against the new company's grants"
        - id: EC-15
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/GeneralManagerAssignmentTest.php
              evidence: "the assignment writes exactly one membership row with the location restriction"
            - leg: R6
              evidence: "the general_manager badge and the location chip both render"
        - id: EC-15a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/GeneralManagerLocationScopeTest.php
              evidence: "a write against location B is refused; location B's rows are unchanged"
            - leg: R6
              evidence: "location B is absent from the selector; the API refuses if forced"
        - id: EC-16
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountRevocationTest.php
              evidence: "removing the last membership deletes every personal_access_tokens row for the principal"
            - leg: R8
              evidence: "the curl with the issued token returns 401 on the next request"
        - id: EC-16a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter the_central_delete_failure_queues_a_retry tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountRevocationTest.php
              evidence: "a jobs row exists on the identity-revocation queue; audit row status=pending; the token row is still present; the next request is 403"
          staging_replay: required
          note: "No leg. A browser cannot induce a central-side delete failure or observe a queued retry (gate r1, M4-3)."
        - id: EC-17
          homes:
            - lane: vitest
              command: pnpm test -- src/hooks/__tests__/usePermissions.stale.test.tsx
              evidence: "a stale permission set still renders the affordance"
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_stale_client_write_is_still_refused tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialScopeTest.php
              evidence: "the write is 403 and the target row is unchanged"
            - leg: R5
              evidence: "the stale affordance is clicked and the refusal toast appears"
        - id: EC-18
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/TokenScopeNarrowingTest.php
              evidence: "effective set == owner grants INTERSECT token scope, never a superset"
            - leg: R8
              evidence: "the narrowed token is refused on an ability its owner holds"
        - id: EC-18a
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter per_site tests/Feature/Identity/TokenScopeNarrowingTest.php
              evidence: "each narrowing site intersects rather than replaces"
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter per_site_on_pg tests/Feature/Identity/TokenScopeNarrowingTest.php
              evidence: "the same intersection holds against a real tenant database"
        - id: EC-19
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter two_concurrent_removals_leave_exactly_one_admin tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php
              evidence: "after both connections commit, count(A) == 1"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-20
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter an_operator_edit_and_a_sync_serialise tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockRaceTest.php
              evidence: "the two transactions serialise; neither loses a grant"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-20a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter operator_edit_versus_sync_on_two_connections tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockRaceTest.php
              evidence: "the later writer blocks on the advisory lock and then reads the committed state"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-20b
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter ensure_versus_an_operator_edit tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockRaceTest.php
              evidence: "permissions:ensure serialises against the operator edit"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-20c
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/EnsurePermissionsRaceTest.php
              evidence: "the delta-first ordering makes matchesPreWave0b true; ensure grants the full batch"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-21
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter compatibility_mode tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionsSyncTest.php
              evidence: "permissions:sync --compat leaves the legacy key readable and the new key granted"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-21a
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_fleet_with_one_blocked_tenant tests/Feature/Identity/SyncPermissionsFleetTest.php
              evidence: "every remaining tenant is attempted; the aggregate marker names the failing tenant; exit non-zero"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-21b
          homes:
            - lane: pg
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter null_team_template_key_collision tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionSyncAdoptionTest.php
              evidence: "BLOCKED with reason=role_collision; NEITHER row adopted"
          staging_replay: required
        - id: EC-22
          homes:
            - lane: manual
              command: null
              result: WITHDRAWN
              evidence: "withdrawn in rev 2 of the spec, number retained -- no scenario. A central SuperAdmin has no tenant_id, so a central token on a tenant route is not a tenant authorization path at all."
        - id: EC-23
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter a_formrequest_returning_true_counts_as_uncovered tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php
              evidence: "the route appears in the uncovered baseline"
        - id: EC-24
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter anti_growth tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php
              evidence: "adding a route plus its baseline entry fails against the CI-pinned blob"
        - id: EC-25
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox --filter stale_entry tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php
              evidence: "the failure message names the entry to remove"
        - id: EC-26
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Architecture/PermissionRegistryLabelCoverageTest.php
              evidence: "the failure names every key missing an fr or ar label"
        - id: EC-27
          homes:
            - lane: sqlite
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Architecture/PermissionEnumManifestParityTest.php
              evidence: "parity holds in BOTH directions: no enum case without a manifest row and none the other way"
        - id: EC-28
          homes:
            - lane: redis
              command: ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialDedupTest.php
              evidence: "sum(suppressed_count) over two emissions == the suppressions actually performed"
            - lane: pg

        - id: EC-29

            - leg: R7

        - id: EC-30

        - id: EC-31
