      **Fifty-eight rows. Fifty-seven executable scenarios. One WITHDRAWN. Nine legs. TWENTY-TWO leg references across twenty-two rows** — which is why the runner dedups (**B4**): without it, R4 would run six times, R2 four, R8 four, R3 three, R5 twice and R6 twice, on a tenant that cannot be reset.
      > **Rev 3 said "thirty-one references across twelve rows" and both numbers were wrong** (gate r3, Wave 4 **MAJOR 3**). Counting the parsed structure gives 22 references, and they are spread across **22 distinct rows** rather than twelve — each referencing row carries exactly one leg. The dedup requirement is unchanged and the arithmetic is now the file's own; `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` asserts 22 rather than a number a reader has to trust.
      > **Phase 0's reconciliation is still the authority for the id list** (Q-w4-2). This file is written from the lane-assignment table above it and from spec §7 at plan-writing time; if Phase 0's enumeration differs by even one id, **the manifest follows Phase 0** and the difference is a finding in the handback, because `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` asserts the two are **equal**.

      **Six rules the manifest enforces, each with its own guard:**
      1. **`RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest`** — the manifest's id set **equals** Phase 0's reconciled register id list. A missing id fails; an unknown id fails. This is what makes *"every row has a home"* checkable rather than asserted.
      2. **Every `command` resolves, in its lane's `cwd`.** For a PHPUnit row, the named file exists **relative to `lanes.<lane>.cwd`** and the `--filter` matches at least one test (dry-run with `--list-tests`); for a vitest row, the file exists; for `shell`, the script exists **and is executable**, resolved the same way. **A row pointing at a test that waves 0a–3 renamed fails here**, not in the middle of a campaign run. **The cwd-relative resolution is what rev 2's EC-32d row got wrong** (**MAJOR 1**).
      3. **Every `leg:` reference names a declared leg**, and **every declared leg is referenced by at least one row or has `covers: []`** — so a leg cannot be orphaned and a row cannot point at a journey that does not exist. **B4's guard.**
      4. **`lane: manual` homes carry no command and a stated `result` or `doc`** — `WITHDRAWN` for EC-22, a replay document for EC-31's operator half. `NOT_RUN` is never a valid manifest state.
      5. **Every row has at least one home**, and a row whose only home is `manual` carries a `result`. **B3's guard**: a row with an empty `homes:` is what *"every row has an executable home"* is supposed to make impossible.
      6. **`staging_replay: required` drives Task 4-5 step 4's table**, so the replay list is generated from the manifest rather than hand-maintained beside it.

      **`apps/api/tests/Architecture/RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest.php`, the body** — rev 4 described six rules and shipped no implementation (rev 7 carries **eight**: rule 7 is the lane-env shape the runner's composer depends on, rule 8 the evidence-document equality), while the manifest itself intentionally names files Wave 4 is about to create (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 4**). Rule 2 therefore has to distinguish *"this file does not exist yet and this commit is why"* from *"a sibling wave renamed it"*, and that distinction is the guard's whole value.
      > **Rev 5's body was deterministically RED and rev 6 fixes three separate causes** (plan gate r5, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 1**). **(1) The repository root was one `dirname` short.** `base_path()` is `<repo>/apps/api`, so `dirname(base_path())` is `<repo>/apps`, and appending a lane `cwd` of `apps/api` gave `<repo>/apps/apps/api/…` — every rule-2 path was wrong, and a wrong path reads as *"a sibling wave renamed it"*, which is the one message this guard must never emit falsely. It is now `REPO_ROOT` if set, else `realpath(base_path('../..'))`, and the result is **asserted to contain `apps/api`** rather than trusted. **(2) Rule 3 asserted every manifest row id numbered 22** while rule 1, two methods above, requires **58**: the two could never both pass. The 22 is the number of rows that *carry a leg reference*, and that is now what is counted, with the 58 asserted alongside it. **(3) Rule 2 promised PHPUnit `--list-tests`/`--filter` resolution and performed only `file_exists` + `is_executable`.** A row naming a file that still exists but whose **method** a sibling wave renamed passed — and then ran nothing and reported green, because PHPUnit exits **0** on an empty filter selection. The PHPUnit arm now shells out through `Symfony\Component\Process\Process` in the lane's own `cwd` and asserts the listing is non-empty. `php -l` proves syntax and proved nothing about any of these three, which is why rev 5's M4-closure claim was false.
      ~~~php
      <?php

      declare(strict_types=1);

      namespace Tests\Architecture;

      use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\DataProvider;
      use Symfony\Component\Process\Process;
      use Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml;
      use Tests\TestCase;

      final class RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest extends TestCase
      {
          private const MANIFEST = 'tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml';

          /**
           * The reconciled retained-id list Phase 0 produces (Q-w4-2). It lives in the
           * manifest's sibling so the test has ONE oracle, and Phase 0 writes it there
           * before this test is written -- never the other way round.
           */
          private const REGISTER_IDS = 'tests/EdgeCases/rbac-register-ids.json';

          /**
           * Files this WAVE creates. A manifest command may name one before it exists --
           * that is the point of Task 4-2's coverage delta -- but the exemption is a CLOSED,
           * EXACTLY-EQUAL list, not a "does not exist yet, carry on".
           *
           * Phase 0 Step 5 produces it (the `MISSING` lines of the delta command) and the
           * commit that creates this test writes it here verbatim. A file that leaves the
           * list because the commit created it is REMOVED here in the same commit -- so the
           * exemption shrinks to empty by the end of Task 4-2 and rule 2 is then absolute.
           *
           * @var list<string>
           */
          private const WAVE_4_CREATES = [
              'tests/Feature/EdgeCases/Rbac/RetiredKeyAbsenceTest.php',
              'tests/Feature/Identity/GeneralManagerLocationScopeTest.php',
              'tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionRenameTest.php',
              'tests/Feature/Identity/ModuleGatingOrderTest.php',
          ];

          /** @return array<string, mixed> */
          private function manifest(): array
          {
              return Yaml::parseFile(base_path(self::MANIFEST));
          }

          public function test_rule_1_the_id_set_equals_the_reconciled_register(): void
          {
              $declared = array_column($this->manifest()['rows'], 'id');
              /** @var list<string> $register */
              $register = json_decode(
                  (string) file_get_contents(base_path(self::REGISTER_IDS)),
                  true,
                  flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
              );

              sort($declared);
              sort($register);

              self::assertSame(
                  $register,
                  $declared,
                  'the manifest and Phase 0\'s reconciled register id list have diverged; the '
                  .'manifest FOLLOWS Phase 0 and the difference is a handback finding',
              );
              self::assertCount(58, $declared, 'spec §7 retains 58 rows');
          }

          /**
           * THE REPOSITORY ROOT, and it is TWO levels above base_path(), not one
           * (plan gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1).
           *
           * base_path() is `<repo>/apps/api`. Rev 5's `dirname(base_path())` is therefore
           * `<repo>/apps`, and appending a lane cwd of `apps/api` produced
           * `<repo>/apps/apps/api/...` -- so rule 2 asserted the existence of files at a
           * path that cannot exist and was RED on every row that names one.
           *
           * The value is overridable by REPO_ROOT so a worktree or a container mount that
           * is not two levels up can state it, and the fallback is ASSERTED rather than
           * trusted: whatever it resolves to must actually contain `apps/api`.
           */
          private function repoRoot(): string
          {
              $root = getenv('REPO_ROOT');
              $root = is_string($root) && $root !== ''
                  ? (realpath($root) ?: $root)
                  : (realpath(base_path('../..')) ?: dirname(base_path(), 2));

              self::assertDirectoryExists(
                  $root.'/apps/api',
                  "REPO_ROOT resolved to {$root}, which does not contain apps/api; every rule-2 path "
                  .'built from it would be wrong, and a wrong path reads as "a sibling wave renamed it"',
              );

              return $root;
          }

          public function test_rule_2_every_command_resolves_in_its_lanes_cwd(): void
          {
              $m = $this->manifest();
              $repoRoot = $this->repoRoot();

              foreach ($m['rows'] as $row) {
                  foreach ($row['homes'] as $home) {
                      if (! isset($home['command'])) {
                          continue;                    // a leg reference or a manual home
                      }

                      $cwd = $m['lanes'][$home['lane']]['cwd'];

                      if (preg_match('#([\w./-]+\.(?:php|ts|sh))#', (string) $home['command'], $match) !== 1) {
                          continue;                    // a command that names no file (an artisan probe)
                      }

                      $relative = $match[1];
                      $absolute = $repoRoot.'/'.$cwd.'/'.$relative;

                      if (in_array($relative, self::WAVE_4_CREATES, true)) {
                          self::assertFileDoesNotExist(
                              $absolute,
                              "{$relative} is on WAVE_4_CREATES but already exists — remove it from the "
                              .'exemption list in the commit that created it, or rule 2 is permanently blind to it',
                          );

                          continue;
                      }

                      self::assertFileExists(
                          $absolute,
                          "{$row['id']} names {$relative} in lane {$home['lane']} (cwd {$cwd}) and it does not "
                          .'exist. Either a sibling wave renamed it — which is a STOP — or it belongs on '
                          .'WAVE_4_CREATES with a Phase 0 delta line to justify it.',
                      );

                      if ($home['lane'] === 'shell') {
                          self::assertTrue(is_executable($absolute), "{$relative} is not executable");

                          continue;
                      }

                      // RULE 2's SECOND HALF, which rev 5 promised and did not implement
                      // (plan gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1): for a PHPUnit home the --filter
                      // must MATCH AT LEAST ONE TEST. A row pointing at a file that still
                      // exists but whose method a sibling wave renamed is exactly the
                      // failure this rule exists to catch, and file existence is blind to it.
                      if (! str_contains((string) $home['command'], 'phpunit')) {
                          continue;                    // vitest/playwright: existence is the contract
                      }

                      if (preg_match('/--filter[= ]([^\s\'"]+)/', (string) $home['command'], $f) !== 1) {
                          continue;                    // a whole-file run needs no filter check
                      }

                      $listed = new Process(
                          ['vendor/bin/phpunit', '--list-tests', '--filter', $f[1], $relative],
                          $repoRoot.'/'.$cwd,
                          ['XDEBUG_MODE' => 'off'],
                          null,
                          120.0,
                      );
                      $listed->run();

                      self::assertTrue(
                          $listed->isSuccessful(),
                          "{$row['id']}: `phpunit --list-tests --filter {$f[1]} {$relative}` exited "
                          ."{$listed->getExitCode()} in {$cwd}: ".trim($listed->getErrorOutput()),
                      );
                      self::assertMatchesRegularExpression(
                          '/^\s+-\s/m',
                          $listed->getOutput(),
                          "{$row['id']}'s --filter {$f[1]} matches NO test in {$relative}. PHPUnit exits 0 on an "
                          .'empty selection, so without this assertion the row would run nothing and report green.',
                      );
                  }
              }
          }

          public function test_rule_3_leg_references_and_declared_legs_agree(): void
          {
              $m = $this->manifest();
              $declared = array_column($m['legs'], 'id');
              $referenced = [];
              $referencingRows = [];

              foreach ($m['rows'] as $row) {
                  foreach ($row['homes'] as $home) {
                      if (isset($home['leg'])) {
                          self::assertContains($home['leg'], $declared, "{$row['id']} references undeclared leg {$home['leg']}");
                          $referenced[] = $home['leg'];
                          $referencingRows[] = $row['id'];
                      }
                  }
              }

              self::assertCount(22, $referenced, 'the manifest carries 22 leg references');

              // ACROSS 22 DISTINCT *REFERENCING* ROWS. Rev 5 counted array_column($m['rows'],
              // 'id') -- EVERY row id -- and asserted it was 22, while rule 1 two methods
              // above requires 58. The two assertions could never both pass (plan gate r5,
              // Wave 4 BLOCKER 1). The quantity the arithmetic note claims is the number of
              // rows that CARRY a leg reference, and that is what is counted here.
              self::assertCount(
                  22,
                  array_unique($referencingRows),
                  'the 22 references are spread across 22 distinct rows -- each referencing row carries exactly one leg',
              );
              self::assertCount(58, array_unique(array_column($m['rows'], 'id')), 'spec §7 retains 58 rows');

              // `legs[].covers` EQUALS the set of rows that reference the leg -- asserted,
              // not eyeballed (plan gate r6, Wave 4 minor 4). Rev 6 checked only that an
              // UNREFERENCED leg declares `covers: []`, while the plan's own verification
              // claim said the equality held "for all nine". A leg whose covers drifts from
              // its referencing rows is how the evidence document ends up attributing a
              // green leg to a row the leg never exercised.
              $byLeg = [];
              foreach ($m['rows'] as $row) {
                  foreach ($row['homes'] as $home) {
                      if (isset($home['leg'])) {
                          $byLeg[$home['leg']][] = $row['id'];
                      }
                  }
              }

              foreach ($m['legs'] as $leg) {
                  $actual = $byLeg[$leg['id']] ?? [];
                  $declared = $leg['covers'] ?? null;

                  self::assertIsArray($declared, "leg {$leg['id']} declares no covers: key");

                  sort($actual);
                  sort($declared);

                  self::assertSame(
                      $actual,
                      $declared,
                      "leg {$leg['id']} declares covers ".json_encode($declared)." but the rows that "
                      .'reference it are '.json_encode($actual).'. An orphan leg declares covers: []; '
                      .'a referenced leg declares exactly its referencing rows.',
                  );
              }
          }

          public function test_rule_4_and_5_homes_and_manual_results(): void
          {
              $withdrawn = 0;

              foreach ($this->manifest()['rows'] as $row) {
                  self::assertNotEmpty($row['homes'], "{$row['id']} has no home");

                  $manualOnly = true;

                  foreach ($row['homes'] as $home) {
                      if (($home['lane'] ?? '') !== 'manual') {
                          $manualOnly = false;

                          continue;
                      }

                      self::assertArrayNotHasKey('command', $home, "{$row['id']}'s manual home carries a command");
                      self::assertTrue(
                          isset($home['result']) || isset($home['doc']),
                          "{$row['id']}'s manual home states neither a result nor a doc",
                      );
                      self::assertNotSame('NOT_RUN', $home['result'] ?? null, 'NOT_RUN is never a manifest state');
                  }

                  if ($manualOnly && ($row['homes'][0]['result'] ?? null) === 'WITHDRAWN') {
                      $withdrawn++;
                  }
              }

              self::assertSame(1, $withdrawn, 'exactly one row is WITHDRAWN, and it is EC-22');
          }

          public function test_rule_6_staging_replay_required_rows_name_a_replayable_lane(): void
          {
              $replayable = ['pg', 'redis', 'shell', 'manual'];

              foreach ($this->manifest()['rows'] as $row) {
                  if (($row['staging_replay'] ?? null) !== 'required') {
                      continue;
                  }

                  $lanes = array_filter(array_column($row['homes'], 'lane'));
                  self::assertNotEmpty(
                      array_intersect($lanes, $replayable),
                      "{$row['id']} declares staging_replay: required but none of its homes is on a lane the "
                      .'runner can replay (pg, redis, shell, manual)',
                  );

                  // A REPLAY ROW MUST NOT REFERENCE A BROWSER LEG. The playwright lane is
                  // `not replayed (declared)`, and the runner's generated replay plans no
                  // leg at all -- so a replay row with a leg home would carry a home that a
                  // replay silently never reaches (plan gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 3).
                  self::assertSame(
                      [],
                      array_values(array_filter(array_column($row['homes'], 'leg'))),
                      "{$row['id']} declares staging_replay: required AND references a browser leg; a "
                      .'generated replay plans no leg, so that home would never be replayed',
                  );
              }
          }

          /**
           * RULE 7 -- every lane env entry is a bare, split-safe K=V (plan gate r6, Wave 4
           * BLOCKER 1). `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh`'s env_merge()/env_quote() split these
           * lists on whitespace and emit each VALUE through shq(); a value containing a
           * space, a tab or a glob would silently become two assignments or match a file.
           * The shape is asserted here rather than defended in the shell, because the
           * manifest is the only place it can be wrong.
           */
          public function test_rule_7_every_lane_env_entry_is_a_split_safe_assignment(): void
          {
              foreach ($this->manifest()['lanes'] as $name => $lane) {
                  foreach (['env', 'staging_env'] as $key) {
                      self::assertArrayHasKey($key, $lane, "lane {$name} declares no {$key}: (use [] for none)");
                      self::assertIsArray($lane[$key], "lane {$name}'s {$key} is not a list");

                      foreach ($lane[$key] as $entry) {
                          self::assertMatchesRegularExpression(
                              '/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*=[A-Za-z0-9._:\/-]*$/',
                              (string) $entry,
                              "lane {$name}'s {$key} entry '{$entry}' is not a bare NAME=value the runner's "
                              .'env composer can split and quote safely',
                          );
                      }
                  }
              }
          }

          /**
           * RULE 8 -- the evidence document's row ids EQUAL the manifest's (plan gate r6,
           * Wave 4 MAJOR 1). Task 4-1 generates that table with
           * `tools/generate-evidence-rows.php`; this is the assertion that keeps it
           * generated. A row added to the manifest and not to the document, or the reverse,
           * is a RED here rather than a row nobody notices is missing at promotion.
           */
          public function test_rule_8_the_evidence_document_carries_every_manifest_row(): void
          {
              $doc = dirname(__DIR__, 3).'/docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-edge-campaign-evidence.md';
              self::assertFileExists($doc, 'the evidence document is written FIRST (Task 4-1)');

              preg_match_all('/^\| (EC-[0-9a-z]+) \|/m', (string) file_get_contents($doc), $found);

              $manifestIds = array_column($this->manifest()['rows'], 'id');
              $documentIds = $found[1];
              sort($manifestIds);
              sort($documentIds);

              self::assertSame(
                  $manifestIds,
                  $documentIds,
                  'the evidence document\'s rows and the manifest\'s rows have drifted; regenerate with '
                  .'`php tools/generate-evidence-rows.php` rather than hand-editing the first column',
              );
          }
      }
      ~~~
      > **`WAVE_4_CREATES` is the mechanism that makes Task 4-2's residual "coverage delta" a DISPATCH INPUT rather than a commit-time discovery** (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 4**). Phase 0 Step 5's delta command produces the list; the orchestrator pastes it into the dispatch brief **and** into this constant; each commit that creates one of those files deletes its line here, and the `assertFileDoesNotExist` arm is what forces that deletion rather than letting the exemption rot into a blanket. **The constant reaches empty before Task 4-2's last commit**, and the verification checklist asserts it.
      > **`rbac-register-ids.json` is Phase 0's output, not this plan's.** It is written in Task 4-2's commit, from the reconciled enumeration Q-w4-2 describes, and it is what makes rule 1 an equality rather than a count.

      **The runner's execution model, stated because B4 is about execution and not only about schema:**
      1. Collect every `homes[]` element across every row.
      2. **Partition into leg references and lane commands.**
      3. **Deduplicate the leg references by leg id**, preserving the `legs:` block's declared order. **Execute each leg exactly ONCE.** A leg's result is then attributed to **every** row that references it, in the ledger and in the evidence document.
      4. Deduplicate identical `(lane, command)` pairs — two rows naming the same `--filter` run it once, for the same reason.
      5. Execute, **accumulating rather than short-circuiting**: every command runs, every result lands in the ledger, and the script exits non-zero at the end if any is red — *"promotion reads the ledger, not the exit code"* is unusable if the first red stops the run.
      6. **A leg marked `destructive: true` is never executed twice in one run, and `--reuse` refuses it entirely** unless it is the single leg `--leg` names.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run each lane through the manifest and record the wall time:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4
      scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --lanes pg,sqlite,redis,vitest,posvitest,shell    # the non-browser rows
      ```
      and, for a single row while iterating:
      ```
      scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --row EC-16a
      ```
      **`--testdox` is in every PHPUnit command in the manifest, deliberately**: the evidence document quotes the test names, and a testdox line is the row's proof in a form a human can read.
- [ ] **Step 5a — THE DISPOSABLE-TENANT MARKER: a central column, a command that sets it, and a command that reads it** (plan gate r5, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 2**).

      > **The ruling, and why this is a column rather than an option on an existing command.** Rev 5's staging preflight invoked `php artisan rbac:row-census --tenant=<id> --field=is_disposable` and **neither option exists**: `rbac:row-census` takes no arguments, runs **under `tenants:run`**, and prints five table counts from the **tenant** database (Step 6's block below). `is_disposable` is a fact about the **central** `tenants` row — a different database — and `dev`'s `Tenant` model has no such fillable, cast or custom column (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Domain/Tenant.php:85-118,125-140,151-188`). So the fake-SSH happy path that rev 5 reported as *"exercised"* validated **mocked text**, not a command the repository can produce. **Conflating the two databases inside one command is the defect**, which is why the marker gets its own command instead of an option on the census.
      >
      > **`rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}` returns an EXIT CODE, not a line to grep.** A preflight that greps stdout is one log-format change away from silently passing; `exit 0` / `exit 1` cannot be misread. It reads the central connection directly and never boots tenancy.

      **(a) The central migration.** `apps/api/database/migrations/2026_09_12_090000_add_is_disposable_to_tenants_table.php` — the **central** migrations directory (`database/migrations/`), not `database/migrations/tenant/`, because `tenants` lives in `synerivia_central`:
      ~~~php
      <?php

      declare(strict_types=1);

      use Illuminate\Database\Migrations\Migration;
      use Illuminate\Database\Schema\Blueprint;
      use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;

      return new class extends Migration
      {
          public function up(): void
          {
              Schema::table('tenants', function (Blueprint $table): void {
                  // NOT NULL DEFAULT false: the safe value is "this is a real tenant".
                  // A nullable marker read as "unknown" is how a replay ends up mutating
                  // production, so there is no third state.
                  $table->boolean('is_disposable')->default(false);
                  $table->index('is_disposable');
              });
          }

          public function down(): void
          {
              Schema::table('tenants', function (Blueprint $table): void {
                  $table->dropIndex(['is_disposable']);
                  $table->dropColumn('is_disposable');
              });
          }
      };
      ~~~

      **(b) The model, in THREE places, because stancl needs all three.** `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Domain/Tenant.php` gains `'is_disposable'` to **`$fillable`** (beside `'is_sensitive'`, `dev:85-118`), `'is_disposable' => 'boolean'` to **`casts()`** (beside `'is_sensitive' => 'boolean'`, `dev:125-140`), **and `'is_disposable'` to `getCustomColumns()`** (`dev:151-188`). **The third is the one that is easy to miss and fatal to omit**: stancl's `HasDataColumn` stores any attribute *not* listed in `getCustomColumns()` inside the JSON `data` column, so without it the real column stays `false` forever while `$tenant->is_disposable` reads `true` — and the preflight, which queries the column, would refuse a tenant the operator had just marked. `is_sensitive` is in all three lists and is the precedent; `is_demo` is in only two and lives in `data`, which is exactly the shape to avoid here.

      **(c) The two commands**, registered in `apps/api/routes/console.php` beside the two probes of Step 6 (the file's existing idiom — `Artisan::command('inspire', …)` at `dev:apps/api/routes/console.php:26`):
      ~~~php
      // apps/api/routes/console.php

      // (3) MARK a tenant disposable. Central connection; an explicit, auditable act.
      Artisan::command('tenants:mark-disposable {id} {--unset}', function (string $id): int {
          $tenant = \App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant::query()->find($id);

          if ($tenant === null) {
              $this->error("no such tenant: {$id}");

              return 1;
          }

          $tenant->is_disposable = ! $this->option('unset');
          $tenant->save();

          $this->line(sprintf('%s is_disposable=%s', $id, $tenant->is_disposable ? 'true' : 'false'));

          return 0;
      })->purpose('Mark a tenant DISPOSABLE (or --unset). Only ever on a tenant you are willing to destroy.');

      // (4) READ the marker. EXIT CODE ONLY -- 0 disposable, 1 not (or unknown tenant).
      //     campaign-rbac.sh's staging_preflight() calls this and branches on $?, so there
      //     is no output format for a log change to break (gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 2).
      Artisan::command('rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}', function (string $id): int {
          $disposable = (bool) \Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB::connection(config('tenancy.database.central_connection', 'central'))
              ->table('tenants')
              ->where('id', $id)
              ->value('is_disposable');

          if (! $disposable) {
              $this->error("tenant {$id} is NOT marked disposable (or does not exist)");
          }

          return $disposable ? 0 : 1;
      })->purpose('Exit 0 when the tenant is marked disposable, 1 otherwise. Read by the campaign staging preflight.');
      ~~~
      > **`rbac:tenant-is-disposable` reads the table, not the model**, so a `getCustomColumns()` omission surfaces here as a refusal rather than as a false green — the two halves of (b) check each other.
      > **`TenantDisposableMarkerTest`** (`apps/api/tests/Feature/Tenant/TenantDisposableMarkerTest.php`): a fresh tenant's column is `false`; `tenants:mark-disposable` sets the **column** (asserted with `DB::connection(central)->table('tenants')->value('is_disposable')`, **not** through the model, which is what proves the custom-column registration); `rbac:tenant-is-disposable` exits **0** after marking, **1** before it and **1** for an unknown id.
      > **The staging tenant is marked ONCE, by hand, by the operator who created it** — `php artisan tenants:mark-disposable <uuid>` — and Phase 0 records the invocation in the handback beside the tenant id. Nothing in the runner marks a tenant; a runner that could would defeat the check.

              apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/rbac-register-ids.json \
              apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/entrypoint-boot-status.sh \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest.php \
              apps/api/database/migrations/2026_09_12_090000_add_is_disposable_to_tenants_table.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Domain/Tenant.php \

      > **`apps/api/routes/console.php` is in the list** because it carries **four** commands: the two tenant-aware probes Task 4-5 step 2 invokes — `rbac:row-census` and `rbac:dry-run-probe` — and Step 5a's `tenants:mark-disposable` / `rbac:tenant-is-disposable`. The migration, the `Tenant` model and `TenantDisposableMarkerTest` are in the list for the same reason the probes are: **`campaign-rbac.sh --staging-replay` refuses to start without the marker**, so the marker must exist before the runner does. They are **registered Artisan commands**, which is the only shape `tenants:run` can call (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 4**), and a command the re-run step needs must exist before that step runs.
      **Plus one file per row in the coverage delta — and the delta is COMPUTED AT PHASE 0, not at commit time** (plan gate r2 **MAJOR 3**; gate r3, Wave 4 **MAJOR 1** — rev 3 printed five fixed paths and then required a later `git status` output to be pasted, which is a list that does not exist at dispatch).
      **Phase 0 Step 5 computes it, and it is mechanical:** for each of the 57 executable ids, the manifest names a `command`; rule 2 of the six already requires every command to resolve in its lane's `cwd`. **A row whose command names a file that does not yet exist IS the delta.**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4/apps/api
      M=tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml php -r 'require "vendor/autoload.php";
        $d = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["M"]);
        foreach ($d["rows"] as $r) { foreach ($r["homes"] as $h) {
          if (! isset($h["command"]) || $h["command"] === null) { continue; }
          if (! preg_match("#(tests/\S+\.php)#", $h["command"], $m)) { continue; }
          if (! is_file($m[1])) { printf("%-8s MISSING  %s\n", $r["id"], $m[1]); }
        } }' | sort -u | tee "$SCRATCH/w4-coverage-delta.txt"
      ```
      **That output IS the add list's variable tail, and it is produced BEFORE Task 4-2 starts**, not after it. It is pasted into the dispatch brief, into this step's `git add` and into the commit body, so a reviewer can re-derive it with one command.
      > **The expected count is the delta's, not 57.** Step 1's whole point is that most rows already have a home from waves 0a–3 and get a **pointer**, not a file. **If the delta is empty, the fixed five paths above are the complete add list** — a legitimate and likely outcome, and the commit body says so rather than leaving a reader to wonder why the directory is absent.
      > **A `MISSING` line for a test a sibling wave is supposed to ship is a BLOCKER, not a file to write here.** `LastAdminFloorTest` is wave 2a's, `PermissionsSyncTest` is wave 1's; if Phase 0's delta names one of those, the entry condition *"waves 0a–3 merged"* is false and this lane does not open.
      Subject: **`Phase 0.7.2: Give every edge-case register row an executable home and a manifest entry naming its lane, command and evidence`**, both trailers.

## Task 4-3: the nine browser legs, the shared runner and the manifest-driven script

**Files:** `scripts/campaign-lib.sh`, `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh`, `scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh` (re-pointed), `apps/web/playwright.rbac.config.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/rbac-campaign/journey.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/rbac-campaign/selectors.ts`, and the **nine** named spec files `R0-fixture` · `R1-second-company` · `R2-last-admin` · `R3-role-management` · `R4-template-drift` · `R5-stale-cache-and-switch` · `R6-location-scope` · `R7-module-gating` · `R8-service-accounts`, plus `apps/web/package.json` and **`scripts/campaign-lib-parity-test.sh`** — **not `tests/CampaignLibParityTest.sh`**, which rev 5 named here and in Step 12's add list for a file that does not exist (plan gate r5, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 5**). **No `R9-pos.spec.ts`** (**M4-1**).

**Shape, sharing the onboarding campaign's runner rather than copying it** (**M4-2**). Legs run **serial**; each appends to a `ledger.json` under `test-results/campaign-<runId>/`; `journey.ts` holds the ledger definitions; `selectors.ts` holds every entry locator; the arg-parse **two-arg guard**, the two `curl --fail` preflights and run-id generation with its four env exports come from `scripts/campaign-lib.sh` — **and nothing else does** (gate r6, W4 **minor 1**). The **ledger accumulator**, the **unknown-argument arm** and the **final exit** are `campaign-rbac.sh`'s own; the library's detailed body has always said so and this sentence contradicted it. New assertions use real APIs and record product failures **without weakening the expectation**.

**Nine legs, not ten** — `R9`'s POS half is `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` (Step 9, **M4-1**).

**The fixture, built by R0 and used by every later leg:** a fresh tenant, **two companies**, **two `pos_enabled` locations** (one per company), one `admin`, one `manager`, one `cashier`, one **custom role** (`supervisor`), one **service account** with a membership restricted to location A, and one narrowed token.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 0 — `R0-fixture.spec.ts`.** Register the tenant, create company 2, create the second `pos_enabled` location, create the four principals and the custom role, and write the ids into the ledger. **Assert the fixture, don't assume it**: two companies present, two locations `pos_enabled`, the custom role `is_system = false`. Every later leg reads its ids from the ledger.
- [ ] **Step 1 — `R1-second-company.spec.ts` (convention-09 second-company axis).** A role granted in the tenant resolves **identically in both companies** while membership scope differs: as `manager`, open a permission-gated screen in company A (affordance present, write succeeds), switch to company B where the principal has **no membership**, and assert the company is **not offered in the switcher** — asserting the **rows** the switcher returns, not the absence of an error.
- [ ] **Step 2 — `R2-last-admin.spec.ts` (EC-1, EC-2, EC-3, EC-4b).** **EC-4 and EC-4a leave this leg** (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M4-3**): this leg drives the four refusals a **sole human admin** meets and neither creates a service admin nor strips a membership, so claiming their coverage here would be an over-claim. Both keep their `LastAdminFloorTest` homes and their manifest rows. As the sole `admin`, attempt: remove your own admin role; deactivate yourself; delete yourself; and change your role to `manager` via the user edit form (**EC-4b — the writer that goes through `syncRoles`, not `removeRole`**). Each expects the refusal **toast asserted by text**, and each is followed by a reload asserting the state is **unchanged**. Then create a second admin and assert one of the four now succeeds — **the leg must prove the floor is passable**, or it is indistinguishable from a broken screen.
- [ ] **Step 3 — `R3-role-management.spec.ts` (EC-11, EC-12, EC-13).** Rename a **custom** role (succeeds; assignments survive, asserted by re-opening a member's roles). Rename and delete a **system** role (refused, `SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED` toast, and the affordance is **disabled** — assert both, because a disabled button with a permissive API is the defect this pair catches). Attempt to empty **`admin`**'s permission set (refused, `ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR` toast) and assert **no `authz.denied` audit row was written** — EC-13's rev-2 note: the caller **is** authorized to reach the endpoint, so this is a floor refusal, not a denial.

Run `permissions:sync-fleet` out of band. Reload and assert the banner reads *"N template updates not applied"* with the customisation date. Click **Re-apply template** and assert the grant set becomes the template's **exactly** (asserting the rendered matrix, not a success toast), the banner clears, and a `RoleSyncedV1` audit row exists with `cause: template_reapply` and a **non-null actor**. **Those three are unconditional.**
      **The deprecated-grant assertion is not.** Rev 1 ended the leg by asserting a **custom** role's deprecated grant *"survived the same sync and is rendered struck through"* — unconditionally. **After a RETIRED Task 3-7 that is impossible**: the key is gone from `PermissionRegistry`, `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` has removed the row, and **`R0` registers a fresh tenant**, so there is no path by which a role in this campaign's tenant can hold or render it (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B4-1**). A leg that asserts an unreachable state is a leg that is red for the wrong reason.
      **The branch, read once from the disposition Phase 0 already recorded** (`php artisan permissions:report-dead` → `0` = RETIRED, `22` = DEFERRED):
      | Disposition | R4's final assertion | EC-5's preservation evidence |
      |---|---|---|
      | **DEFERRED** | As rev 1 wrote it: give a **custom** role the deprecated grant, run the sync, assert the grant **survived** and is rendered **struck through** in the Roles matrix | this leg |
      | **RETIRED** | **Absence, in the TWO places a browser can read**: the key is **not** in `GET /api/v1/permissions`' payload, and the Roles matrix renders **no** struck-through entry for it. **The `permissions`-row absence is NOT in this leg** — it is `RetiredKeyAbsenceTest`, a `pg` home on EC-5, for the reason two paragraphs down (gate r3, Wave 4 **MINOR 2**: rev 3's cell said *"in all three places"* and then moved the third away, so the contract was stated twice and differently) | **wave 1's own evidence, pre-retirement** — `PermissionsSyncTemplateDeltaTest`'s deprecation case and its handback, cited by path and test name in the evidence row. EC-5's behaviour was real and was proven when the key existed; it cannot be re-proven on a tree where it does not, and pretending otherwise is what makes a register decorative |
      **The middle assertion needs a DATABASE read, and a browser leg has no database** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 5**). Rev 2 said *"asserted by a direct query"* and named no access path; the leg is otherwise entirely HTTP-driven, and a **local** `psql`/`tinker` from the runner would inspect the **local** database while the leg drove **staging** — the campaign runs against both. **The row-absence assertion is therefore NOT a browser assertion. It moves to EC-5's `pg` home**, where it belongs:
      ```
      # apps/api/tests/Feature/EdgeCases/Rbac/RetiredKeyAbsenceTest.php -- the pg home.
      # Runs in the lane, on the lane's connection, which under --staging-replay is the
      # DEPLOYED database reached over ssh (Task 4-5 step 4's pg row) rather than a local one.
      $this->assertDatabaseMissing('permissions', ['name' => $retiredKey]);
      ```
      **R4 keeps the two assertions a browser CAN make** — the key is absent from `GET /api/v1/permissions`' payload (an HTTP read the leg already performs) and the Roles matrix renders no struck-through entry (a DOM assertion) — and the manifest's EC-5 row gains the `pg` home above for the third. **A leg that claims a database fact it cannot read is the same defect as a leg that claims a queue fact it cannot observe (EC-16a, M4-3)**, and it is fixed the same way: the assertion goes to the lane that can make it.
      **The evidence row records WHICH arm ran**, so a later reader can tell a skipped assertion from a passed one. The leg reads the disposition from the ledger (R0 writes it) rather than re-deriving it, so both arms cannot run and neither can be silently skipped.
      **`R4` is the only leg with a disposition branch.** The `pos_orders.*`, `audit.view` and other retired-key legs mentioned in the entry condition are manifest rows, not browser legs, and their expected values come from the same ledger field.
- [ ] **Step 5 — `R5-stale-cache-and-switch.spec.ts` (EC-14, EC-17).** With a tab open as `manager`, revoke a permission out of band; **without reloading**, assert the affordance is still rendered (the accepted 5-minute UX exposure) **and** that clicking it produces a **403 with a visible error**, not a silent no-op or a 500. Both keep their PG homes and their manifest rows. **EC-18a** stays PG too, per the lane table. Create a service account with a location-A-restricted membership and the `viewer` role. Issue a token narrowed to a **subset** of `viewer`'s keys; assert the plaintext shows **once**. Then, out of band with `curl`, the MCP-shaped probe:
      ```
      curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "X-Company-Id: $COMPANY_A" $API/api/v1/auth/me | jq '.data.token_scope'
      #   granted = the narrowed subset; unknown = []
      # The narrowed-out route is `GET /api/v1/roles`, and that is a RESOLVED choice, not a
      # placeholder: `viewer` holds `roles.view` (wave 1's templateGrants), the token above is
      # narrowed to a SUBSET of viewer's keys that EXCLUDES it, and the route is gated
      # `can:roles.view` (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:59). Rev 4 left

      # The BODY is asserted, not only the status: `-o /dev/null` cannot tell token narrowing
      # apart from any other 403, and §"assert the data meaning, never the status code" is
      # this campaign's own rule.
      curl -sS -w '\n%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "X-Company-Id: $COMPANY_A" \
        $API/api/v1/roles | tee "$EVIDENCE/ec-18-narrowed-denial.txt"
      #   403 — NOT 500 — and the body's error.code is FORBIDDEN with error.ability = "roles.view",

      #   the same call on an UNSCOPED token for the same principal returns 200, and the leg

      curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" $API/api/v1/products
      #   400 COMPANY_CONTEXT_REQUIRED
      curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "X-Company-Id: $COMPANY_B" $API/api/v1/products
      #   403 SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_NO_MEMBERSHIP

EC-31 is covered by a vitest plus a documented manual replay.** Rev 1 wrote *"drive the dev IziPOS build"* against `apps/web`'s Playwright config, which preflights only the API and web, has no `webServer` for a POS target, and cannot reach a Tauri app; **`apps/pos` has no Playwright dependency and no Playwright script** (`apps/pos/package.json` — `"test": "vitest run"`). Standing up a POS driver is its own piece of work (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M4-1**).
      **What ships instead, and it is two things:**
      1. **`apps/pos/src/lib/auth/__tests__/offlineRevocation.test.ts`** (pos-vitest, automated): against a cached operator set, a **revoked** operator's PIN is absent from the holder set the device will accept; an operator whose cached row carries `authority_stale: true` does **not** get manager access; and an operator with no cached permissions and `roles: ['admin']` gets **nothing** — the ladder is gone. **Assertions are on the cached rows**, never on `navigator.onLine`, which a mock makes trivially true.
      2. **`docs/qa/RBAC-POS-DEVICE-REPLAY.md`** (manual, documented): the operator-visible half, written as a numbered replay a tester performs on a real device — sign in by cashier PIN, complete a **real sale**; manager PIN step-up approves an override; deactivate the manager server-side and confirm their PIN stops being offered on all three surfaces (`verifyPin`, `pinData`, `hasPins` — they share `pinHolders()`); **physically disconnect the network** and confirm the device continues on its last synced payload. Each step states its expected observation and where the tester writes it, and the evidence document's EC-31 row quotes those observations with the tester's name and the date.
      **The evidence row is `NOT_SCRIPTABLE`, with the reason and the replay's run date.** That is an honest state; a Playwright leg that cannot execute is not, and `NOT_RUN` never passes.
      **When a POS driver does exist**, this becomes a leg without any other change: the manifest row's `lane` moves from `manual` to `playwright` and its `command` is filled in. That is recorded as **R-w4-8**.
