      >
      > and the **manifest→plan** step — the one B4 is actually about — was executed against the pasted manifest. It emits a deduplicated, ordered, **US-separated** (0x1f) plan record — not TSV; TAB is IFS whitespace and would collapse the empty `env` field of six of the eight lanes (gate r6 fix round, found by exercising):
      >
      > ```
      > 9 LEG      -- each leg ONCE, in the legs: block's declared order, with the rows it covers
      >               R2 -> EC-1,EC-2,EC-3,EC-4b        (4 rows, ONE execution)
      >               R4 -> EC-5,EC-9,EC-9a,EC-9b,EC-9c,EC-10  (6 rows, ONE execution)
      >               R8 -> EC-16,EC-18,EC-32b,EC-34    (4 rows, ONE execution)
      > 63 LANE    -- distinct (lane, command) pairs across the 57 executable rows
      > 2 MANUAL   -- EC-22 (WITHDRAWN) and EC-31's operator half (NOT_SCRIPTABLE)
      >
      > $ ... ONLY_ROW=EC-16a   -> 1 LANE line, the pg filter, no legs, no manual
      > $ ... ONLY_LEG=R4       -> 1 LEG line and NOTHING else (the --reuse single-leg contract)
      > $ ... STAGING_REPLAY=1  -> 0 LEG, 22 LANE, 1 MANUAL  -- the 19 `staging_replay: required`
      >                            rows and nothing else (gate r6, W4 BLOCKER 3). Re-run against
      >                            the pasted manifest on 2026-09-11; the unfiltered plan is
      >                            9 / 63 / 2, which is what rev 6 produced for a REPLAY too.
      > ```
      >
      > **`grep -c 'R2-last-admin' <plan>` is 1 while four rows carry its result**, which is the verification checklist's assertion, satisfied by the implementation rather than by the description of one.

      1. **`scripts/campaign-lib.sh` — extracted from `dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh`, and the extraction claim is now TRUE.**

      ~~~bash
      #!/usr/bin/env bash
      #
      # scripts/campaign-lib.sh — the primitives BOTH campaigns share.
      #
      # Extracted VERBATIM from dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh (63 lines total):
      #   :9-29   the two-arg guard ("Missing value for $1", exit 2), as
      #           campaign_lib_consume_pair() -- the INNER arm only
      #   :31-39  the two `curl --fail --silent --show-error --max-time 10` preflights
      #   :41-45  run-id generation and the four env exports (`:46` is blank)
      #
      # NOT extracted, and rev 4's header wrongly said they were (gate r4, W4 MAJOR 5).
      # THE SAME SENTENCE APPEARS IN THE FILE STRUCTURE'S TWO TABLES; rev 5's three
      # inventories disagreed (gate r5, W4 MINOR 2) and rev 6 states it once, verbatim:
      #   :9-29's UNKNOWN-ARGUMENT arm -- it must print the CALLING script's usage block,
      #           and campaign-rbac.sh's names these flags, none of which the onboarding script has:
      #           (--manifest, --lanes, --row, --leg, --reuse, --drop-stale, --staging-replay,
      #            --ssh, --remote-root/--container-root, --api-container, --staging-tenant)
      #   :63     the exit-code contract -- it is `exit "$red"` at the end of a runner that
      #           owns a ledger; a library function cannot own a caller's final status
      #
      # NOT extracted, because dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh does not contain them
      # (gate r2 MAJOR 4; gate r3 W4 MAJOR 2 — rev 3's created-files table claimed it did):
      #   --reuse, --leg, --lanes, --row, --staging-replay, --ssh, --remote-root,
      #   --api-container, --staging-tenant, the manifest reader, the leg deduplicator,
      #   the per-row result accumulator and the ledger writer. Those are campaign-rbac.sh's.

      campaign_web="${CAMPAIGN_WEB_URL:-http://localhost:5173}"
      campaign_api="${CAMPAIGN_API_URL:-http://127.0.0.1:8010}"
      campaign_country="${CAMPAIGN_COUNTRY:-TN}"

      # The onboarding script's :9-29 arm, as a function. Byte-identical messages and
      # exit codes — CampaignLibParityTest asserts that across four cases.
      campaign_lib_consume_pair() {
        if [[ $# -lt 2 || -z "${2:-}" ]]; then
          echo "Missing value for $1" >&2
          exit 2
        fi
        case "$1" in
          --web)     campaign_web="$2" ;;
          --api)     campaign_api="$2" ;;
          --country) campaign_country="$2" ;;
        esac
      }

      # The onboarding script's :31-46: two health preflights, run id, exports.
      campaign_lib_preflight() {
        if ! curl --fail --silent --show-error --max-time 10 "${campaign_api%/}/api/v1/health" >/dev/null; then
          echo "Campaign aborted: API health check failed at ${campaign_api%/}/api/v1/health" >&2
          exit 1
        fi

        if ! curl --fail --silent --show-error --max-time 10 "${campaign_web%/}/" >/dev/null; then
          echo "Campaign aborted: web check failed at ${campaign_web%/}/" >&2
          exit 1
        fi

        campaign_run_id="${CAMPAIGN_RUN_ID:-$(date -u +%Y%m%d%H%M%S)-$$}"
        export CAMPAIGN_WEB_URL="$campaign_web"
        export CAMPAIGN_API_URL="$campaign_api"
        export CAMPAIGN_COUNTRY="$campaign_country"
        export CAMPAIGN_RUN_ID="$campaign_run_id"
      }
      ~~~

      > **What the library actually CONTAINS, and it is two functions and three variables** (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 5**): `campaign_web` / `campaign_api` / `campaign_country`, `campaign_lib_consume_pair()` (the two-arg guard, `dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh:9-29`'s inner arm) and `campaign_lib_preflight()` (the two `curl --fail` preflights at `:31-39` plus run-id generation and the four env exports at `:41-45`). **That is all of it.**
      > **The UNKNOWN-ARGUMENT arm and the EXIT-CODE contract are NOT in the library, and rev 4's header claimed they were.** They cannot be: the unknown-argument arm must print **the calling script's own usage block** (`campaign-rbac.sh`'s names the flags enumerated in the library header, none of which the onboarding script has) and the exit-code contract is `exit "$red"` at the end of a runner that has a ledger — a library function cannot own either. **Each script keeps its own**, and `CampaignLibParityTest.sh` is what proves the two agree where they must. The header comment above is corrected to say exactly this; a `bash -n` pass over a library says nothing about whether its docstring is true.
      > **`--reuse` and a ledger accumulator are NOT extracted, because they do not exist there** (gate r3, Wave 4 **MAJOR 2**). Rev 3's created-files table said `campaign-lib.sh` extracts *"`--reuse` and a ledger accumulator"* while its own detailed code census, four rows later, correctly said neither exists today. The table is corrected; the census was right.
      > **Re-point the onboarding script at the library**, and the re-point is TWO edits, both stated so the diff is checkable: delete `dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh:5-6` and `:31-46` and replace them with `. "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/campaign-lib.sh"` at the top plus `campaign_lib_preflight` where `:31` was, and replace the **inner** two-arg guard inside its `--web|--api|--country` arm with `campaign_lib_consume_pair "$@"`. **Its unknown-argument arm and its `exit` line are untouched**, because neither moved.
      > **`scripts/campaign-lib-parity-test.sh` is the parity proof, and it is a FILE in the add list** (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 5**: rev 4 named `CampaignLibParityTest.sh` and supplied no body, so *"onboarding parity"* rested on a `bash -n`). Four cases, each run against the pre-change script (`git show dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh` into a temp file) and the post-change one, asserting **byte-identical stderr and identical exit codes**:
      ~~~bash
      #!/usr/bin/env bash
      # scripts/campaign-lib-parity-test.sh — the extraction is behaviour-preserving, proven.
      set -uo pipefail
      ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
      BEFORE="$(mktemp)"; trap 'rm -f "$BEFORE"' EXIT
      git -C "$ROOT" show dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh > "$BEFORE"
      chmod +x "$BEFORE"
      AFTER="${ROOT}/scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh"

      fail=0
      probe() {                     # $1 label, rest: argv
        local label="$1"; shift
        local b_err a_err b_rc a_rc
        b_err="$("$BEFORE" "$@" 2>&1 >/dev/null)"; b_rc=$?
        a_err="$("$AFTER"  "$@" 2>&1 >/dev/null)"; a_rc=$?
        if [[ "$b_err" != "$a_err" || "$b_rc" != "$a_rc" ]]; then
          echo "PARITY FAIL [$label]: before(rc=$b_rc) '$b_err' != after(rc=$a_rc) '$a_err'" >&2
          fail=1
        else
          echo "parity ok [$label] rc=$a_rc"
        fi
      }

      probe help              --help
      probe unknown-arg       --not-a-flag
      probe missing-value     --api
      # preflight failure: point both at a port nothing listens on, so curl --fail exits non-zero
      probe preflight-failure --api http://127.0.0.1:9 --web http://127.0.0.1:9

      exit "$fail"
      ~~~
      > **Why `--help` is a parity case even though neither script defines one**: it lands in the unknown-argument arm, which is exactly the arm the library does **not** own, and identical output there is what proves the re-point did not quietly move it.

      2. **`scripts/campaign-rbac.sh` — the manifest-driven runner, in full.**

      ~~~bash
      #!/usr/bin/env bash
      #
      # scripts/campaign-rbac.sh — the RBAC edge-case campaign runner.
      #
      # It sources scripts/campaign-lib.sh for the THREE primitives the onboarding campaign
      # already has: the two-arg guard (campaign_lib_consume_pair), the two curl --fail
      # preflights, and run-id generation with its four env exports (campaign_lib_preflight).
      # THAT IS THE WHOLE LIBRARY. The unknown-argument arm and the final exit-code contract
      # are NOT in it and are implemented below in this file -- the first must print THIS
      # script's usage block, which names flags the onboarding script has never had, and the
      # second is `exit "$red"` at the end of a runner that owns a ledger, which no library
      # function can own for its caller. Rev 6's header claimed the library supplied both
      # (gate r6, W4 minor 1; the same error gate r4 MAJOR 5 had already corrected in the
      # library's own header). Everything from --lanes onward is THIS campaign's and is not
      # part of the extraction (gate r2, MAJOR 4; gate r3, W4 MAJOR 2).
      #
      # EXECUTION MODEL (gate r2 B4; gate r3 W4 BLOCKER 3 — this is the implementation,
      # not the prose):
      #   1. collect every homes[] element across every row
      #   2. partition into leg references and lane commands
      #   3. DEDUP the leg references by leg id, preserving the legs: block's order, and
      #      run each leg exactly ONCE, attributing its result to every referencing row
      #   4. dedup identical (lane, command) pairs
      #   5. execute ACCUMULATING, never short-circuiting; exit non-zero at the end if red
      #   6. never run a destructive: true leg twice; --reuse refuses one unless --leg names it

      set -uo pipefail

      REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
      # shellcheck source=scripts/campaign-lib.sh
      . "${REPO_ROOT}/scripts/campaign-lib.sh"

      MANIFEST="${REPO_ROOT}/apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml"
      LANES=""
      ONLY_ROW=""
      ONLY_LEG=""
      REUSE=0
      STAGING_REPLAY=0
      SSH_TARGET=""
      REMOTE_ROOT="/var/www/autoerp"          # the SSH HOST's checkout path
      CONTAINER_ROOT="/var/www/html"          # the API CONTAINER's workdir — a DIFFERENT path
      API_CONTAINER=""
      STAGING_TENANT=""
      DROP_STALE=0
      HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE=0
      HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR=""
      CREATED_DBS=()

      # ONE definition. Rev 5 declared die2() twice, sixty characters apart, with identical
      # bodies (plan gate r5, Wave 4 MINOR 3). Harmless in bash and a reviewer's paper cut.
      die2() { echo "$1" >&2; echo "reason=$2" >&2; exit 2; }

      # ---- THE LANE ENVIRONMENT, GENERATED FROM THE MANIFEST ---------------------------
      # gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1. The generated command is an `ssh` command, and ordinary
      # ssh forwards NO exported variable, so a lane's environment has to be written INTO
      # the remote command line. Rev 6 hand-listed three assignments in the pg wrapper and
      # one in the redis wrapper and exported the rest into the LOCAL subshell around
      # `eval`, where the remote shell never saw them. These four functions are the whole
      # mechanism, and every remote environment in this runner goes through them -- the
      # lane wrappers AND the migration preflight -- so there is exactly one place where a
      # lane's environment is decided and it is the manifest.
      #
      # shq() is the only quoting primitive: it returns a single-quoted shell word with
      # embedded single quotes escaped, which is safe for ANY byte. `for pair in $1` splits
      # on IFS deliberately -- the manifest's env entries are validated by
      # RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest rule 7 to contain no whitespace, glob or
      # metacharacter, so the split is total and the glob is unreachable.
      shq() { local q=\'; printf '%s' "${q}${1//$q/${q}\\${q}${q}}${q}"; }

      # EVERY ONE OF THESE PINS `local IFS=$' \t\n'`, AND THAT IS NOT DECORATION. The
      # execution loop calls staging_wrap() inside `IFS=$'\x1f' read -r target run <
      # <(staging_wrap …)`, and an IFS= PREFIX is in scope for the whole command including
      # its process substitution — so without the pin `for pair in $1` split the lane's env
      # on US instead of on space and produced
      # `DB_CONNECTION='pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 …'`: ONE assignment carrying
      # four others as literal text, with DB_DATABASE never set at all. Found by exercising
      # the wrappers on the fake-ssh harness for this revision.
      #
      # env_merge is the ONE place precedence is decided (overlay key replaces base key,
      # declaration order preserved); env_quote is the ONE place a value is escaped. The
      # three public helpers are compositions of those two, so a caller can nest merges --
      # which is how the central-migration prefix overrides DB_DATABASE without emitting
      # the key twice.
      # shellcheck disable=SC2086  # the word split is the point; manifest rule 7 guards content
      env_merge() {   # $1 base  $2 overlay -> space-joined K=V
        local IFS=$' \t\n' pair k out=""
        for pair in $1; do
          k="${pair%%=*}"
          case " $2 " in *" ${k}="*) continue ;; esac
          out+="$pair "
        done
        for pair in $2; do out+="$pair "; done
        printf '%s' "$out"
      }

      # shellcheck disable=SC2086
      env_quote() {   # $1 space-joined K=V -> "K='V' K='V' "
        local IFS=$' \t\n' pair out=""
        for pair in $1; do out+="${pair%%=*}=$(shq "${pair#*=}") "; done
        printf '%s' "$out"
      }

      env_compose() {   # $1 base  $2 overlay -> the shell-escaped assignment PREFIX
        env_quote "$(env_merge "$1" "$2")"
      }

      # shellcheck disable=SC2086
      env_docker_flags() {   # the SAME composed map, as `docker exec -e K=V` flags
        local IFS=$' \t\n' pair out=""
        for pair in $(env_merge "$1" "$2"); do out+="-e $(shq "$pair") "; done
        printf '%s' "$out"
      }

      # shellcheck disable=SC2086
      env_value() {   # $1 key  $2 base  $3 overlay -> the COMPOSED value, or empty
        local IFS=$' \t\n' pair v=""
        for pair in $(env_merge "$2" "$3"); do [[ "${pair%%=*}" == "$1" ]] && v="${pair#*=}"; done
        printf '%s' "$v"
      }

      lane_env() {    # $1 lane  $2 "env"|"staging_env" -> space-joined K=V, from the manifest
        ( cd "${REPO_ROOT}/apps/api" && MANIFEST="$MANIFEST" LANE="$1" KEY="$2" php -r '
          require "vendor/autoload.php";
          $m = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["MANIFEST"]);
          echo implode(" ", $m["lanes"][$_SERVER["LANE"]][$_SERVER["KEY"]] ?? []);' )
      }

      LEG_SEEN=0

      # Every remote call goes through this, so the staging preflight and the lane wrapper
      # can both be EXERCISED with a fake (CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN=/path/to/fake-ssh) instead of a
      # real host. A contract that can only be tested by owning a staging box is untested.
      SSH_BIN="${CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN:-ssh}"

      while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
        case "$1" in
          --api|--web|--country)
            campaign_lib_consume_pair "$@"; shift 2 ;;
          --manifest|--lanes|--row|--leg|--ssh|--remote-root|--container-root|--api-container|--staging-tenant)
            [[ $# -lt 2 || -z "${2:-}" ]] && die2 "Missing value for $1" "missing_value"
            case "$1" in
              --manifest)       MANIFEST="$2" ;;
              --lanes)          LANES="$2" ;;
              --row)            ONLY_ROW="$2" ;;
              --leg)
                # gate r4 B1: repeated --leg silently overwrote, so `--reuse --leg R2 --leg R3`
                # passed the single-leg check and ran R3 alone.
                (( LEG_SEEN++ ))
                (( LEG_SEEN > 1 )) && die2 "--leg may be given at most once (got '$ONLY_LEG' then '$2')" "leg_repeated"
                ONLY_LEG="$2" ;;
              --ssh)            SSH_TARGET="$2" ;;
              --remote-root)    REMOTE_ROOT="$2" ;;
              --container-root) CONTAINER_ROOT="$2" ;;
              --api-container)  API_CONTAINER="$2" ;;
              --staging-tenant) STAGING_TENANT="$2" ;;
            esac
            shift 2 ;;
          --reuse)          REUSE=1; shift ;;
          --staging-replay) STAGING_REPLAY=1; shift ;;
          # Recreate an isolated campaign database a previous aborted run left behind.
          # NEVER implicit: an implicit drop of a database whose name an operator mistyped
          # is the same class of mistake as replaying against a real tenant.
          --drop-stale)     DROP_STALE=1; shift ;;
          *)
            echo "Unknown argument: $1" >&2
            echo "Usage: $0 [--web URL] [--api URL] [--country TN] [--manifest PATH] [--lanes a,b]" >&2
            echo "          [--row EC-n] [--leg Rn] [--reuse] [--drop-stale] [--staging-replay --ssh USER@HOST" >&2
            echo "          --remote-root PATH [--container-root PATH] --api-container NAME" >&2
            echo "          --staging-tenant UUID]" >&2
            exit 2 ;;
        esac
      done

      # ---- flag contracts (gate r2 B2, B5) -----------------------------------------
      if (( REUSE )); then
        [[ -z "$ONLY_LEG" ]] && die2 "--reuse requires --leg" "reuse_requires_leg"
        [[ "$ONLY_LEG" == *,* ]] && die2 "--reuse takes exactly one leg" "reuse_is_single_leg"
        # THE THIRD ARM, which the contract table promised and rev 5's parser did not have
        # (plan gate r5, Wave 4 MINOR 1). A lane selector adds non-browser LANE commands to
        # a reuse run, and "reuse runs exactly one leg and nothing else" is then false --
        # the gate exercised `--reuse --leg R4 --lanes pg` and it ran R4 with exit 0.
        [[ -n "$LANES" ]] && die2 "--reuse --leg runs exactly one leg; --lanes adds lane commands to the run" "reuse_is_single_leg"
      fi
      if (( STAGING_REPLAY )); then
        [[ -z "$SSH_TARGET" ]] && die2 "--staging-replay requires --ssh" "staging_replay_requires_ssh"
        [[ -z "$API_CONTAINER" ]] && die2 "--staging-replay requires --api-container" "staging_replay_requires_container"
        [[ -z "$STAGING_TENANT" ]] && die2 "--staging-replay requires --staging-tenant (the DISPOSABLE staging tenant)" "staging_replay_requires_tenant"
      fi
      [[ -f "$MANIFEST" ]] || die2 "Manifest not found: $MANIFEST" "manifest_missing"

      # ---- SELECTOR VALIDATION, BEFORE ANYTHING RUNS (gate r4, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1) ----
      # Rev 4 validated only the manifest's INTERNAL leg references. An unknown --leg or
      # --row selected nothing, every loop skipped, and the script exited 0 with an empty
      # green ledger — which is the single worst outcome a campaign runner can have.
      # A SELECTOR THAT SELECTS NOTHING IS AN ERROR, NOT AN EMPTY PASS.
      validate_selectors() {
        ( cd "${REPO_ROOT}/apps/api" && MANIFEST="$MANIFEST" ONLY_ROW="$ONLY_ROW" ONLY_LEG="$ONLY_LEG" LANES="$LANES" php -r '
          require "vendor/autoload.php";
          $m = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["MANIFEST"]);
          $legIds = array_column($m["legs"], "id");
          $rowIds = array_column($m["rows"], "id");
          $laneIds = array_keys($m["lanes"]);
          $onlyRow = $_SERVER["ONLY_ROW"]; $onlyLeg = $_SERVER["ONLY_LEG"]; $lanes = $_SERVER["LANES"];

          if ($onlyLeg !== "" && ! in_array($onlyLeg, $legIds, true)) {
            fwrite(STDERR, "Unknown leg: {$onlyLeg}. Declared legs: ".implode(", ", $legIds)."\n");
            fwrite(STDERR, "reason=unknown_leg\n");
            exit(2);
          }
          if ($onlyRow !== "" && ! in_array($onlyRow, $rowIds, true)) {
            fwrite(STDERR, "Unknown row: {$onlyRow}. The manifest declares ".count($rowIds)." rows.\n");
            fwrite(STDERR, "reason=unknown_row\n");
            exit(2);
          }
          foreach (array_filter(explode(",", $lanes)) as $lane) {
            if (! in_array($lane, $laneIds, true)) {
              fwrite(STDERR, "Unknown lane: {$lane}. Declared lanes: ".implode(", ", $laneIds)."\n");
              fwrite(STDERR, "reason=unknown_lane\n");
              exit(2);
            }
          }
        ' )
      }
      validate_selectors || exit 2

      # ---- STAGING ISOLATION PREFLIGHT, EXECUTED BY THIS RUNNER (gate r4 W4 BLOCKER 3) --
      # Rev 4 printed these checks in prose beside the runner and the runner never ran
      # them: it verified only that three flags were non-empty and then went straight to
      # the HTTP health check. The five checks below run BEFORE the first mutating lane
      # and abort the replay, which is the whole of what "isolation is the contract" means.
      staging_preflight() {
        local out

        # THE PG LANE'S COMPOSED ENVIRONMENT, read ONCE from the manifest and used by
        # check 4 (which databases to create and drop) and by the migration below (which
        # connection opens which database). Nothing here names a database literally, so
        # renaming the isolated pair is a manifest edit (gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1).
        local pgenv pgsenv tenant_db central_db
        pgenv="$(lane_env pg env)"
        pgsenv="$(lane_env pg staging_env)"
        tenant_db="$(env_value DB_DATABASE "$pgenv" "$pgsenv")"
        central_db="$(env_value DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE "$pgenv" "$pgsenv")"
        [[ -n "$tenant_db" && -n "$central_db" ]] \
          || die2 "the pg lane declares no DB_DATABASE/DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE for staging" "staging_lane_env_missing"
        [[ "$(env_value DB_CONNECTION "$pgenv" "$pgsenv")" == "pgsql" ]] \
          || die2 "the pg lane must carry DB_CONNECTION=pgsql; with the deployed DB_CONNECTION=central \
the default connection reads DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE and the isolated tenant database is never opened" \
               "staging_lane_connection_wrong"

        # 1. SSH REACHABILITY. Everything else is an ssh call; a reachability failure must
        #    read as "cannot reach staging", never as "the tenant does not exist".
        "$SSH_BIN" -o BatchMode=yes "$SSH_TARGET" true \
          || die2 "cannot reach $SSH_TARGET over ssh (BatchMode)" "staging_ssh_unreachable"

        # 2. THE CAMPAIGN TENANT EXISTS.
        out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan tenants:list" 2>&1)" \
          || die2 "tenants:list failed on staging: ${out}" "staging_tenant_list_failed"
        grep -q -- "$STAGING_TENANT" <<<"$out" \
          || die2 "campaign tenant $STAGING_TENANT is not in the staging directory" "staging_tenant_absent"

        # 3. IT IS DISPOSABLE, AND THAT IS A COLUMN ON THE CENTRAL ROW, NOT A PROMISE IN A
        #    RUNBOOK. `rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}` (Task 4-2 Step 6a) reads
        #    tenants.is_disposable in the CENTRAL database and EXITS 0 or 1; it prints
        #    nothing this script has to grep. Refusing here is what stops a replay ever
        #    binding to a real tenant, which is the one irreversible mistake in this wave.
        #
        #    Rev 5 called `rbac:row-census --tenant= --field=` -- a command with NEITHER
        #    option, which runs under `tenants:run` and reads the TENANT database, while
        #    is_disposable lives CENTRALLY (plan gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 2). Conflating the
        #    two databases in one command is the defect, so the marker gets its own.
        if ! "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
              "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan rbac:tenant-is-disposable ${STAGING_TENANT}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
          out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
                 "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan rbac:tenant-is-disposable ${STAGING_TENANT}" 2>&1 || true)"
          die2 "tenant ${STAGING_TENANT} is NOT marked disposable (${out}); refusing to mutate it. Mark it with \
\`php artisan tenants:mark-disposable ${STAGING_TENANT}\` -- and only ever on a tenant you are willing to destroy." \
               "staging_tenant_not_disposable"
        fi

        # 4. THE ISOLATED DATABASES ARE CREATED BY THIS PREFLIGHT AND DROPPED BY ITS TRAP.
        #    Rev 4 checked one NAME; rev 5 required both to pre-exist and summed
        #    pg_stat_user_tables -- which is a STATISTICS ESTIMATE, not an exact count, and
        #    is 0 on a freshly-ANALYZEd table that holds rows (plan gate r5, Wave 4
        #    BLOCKER 3). The lane contract says the preflight CREATES the two isolated
        #    databases and DROPS them afterwards; rev 6 does exactly that, which also makes
        #    "empty" true by construction instead of by measurement.
        local db
        for db in "$tenant_db" "$central_db"; do
          out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "psql -Atqc \"select count(*) from pg_database where datname='${db}'\"" 2>&1)" \
            || die2 "cannot query pg_database on staging: ${out}" "staging_db_query_failed"

          if [[ "$out" == "1" ]]; then
            # A leftover from an aborted run. It is OURS by name, so dropping it is safe --
            # but only with --drop-stale, so an operator is never surprised by a drop.
            (( DROP_STALE )) \
              || die2 "${db} already exists (a previous run did not clean up). Re-run with --drop-stale to \
recreate it, after confirming no other campaign is in flight." "staging_db_stale"
            "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "psql -Atqc 'DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS ${db} WITH (FORCE)'" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
              || die2 "cannot drop stale ${db}" "staging_db_drop_failed"
          fi

          "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "psql -Atqc 'CREATE DATABASE ${db}'" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
            || die2 "cannot create ${db} on staging" "staging_db_create_failed"
          CREATED_DBS+=("$db")

          # Created one statement ago, so this is a TAUTOLOGY CHECK rather than an estimate:
          # it proves the CREATE landed and the connection can see it.
          out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "psql -Atqd ${db} -c \"select count(*) from pg_tables where schemaname='public'\"" 2>&1)" \
            || die2 "cannot inspect ${db}: ${out}" "staging_db_count_failed"
          [[ "$out" == "0" ]] \
            || die2 "${db} was created but is not empty (${out} public tables)" "staging_db_not_empty"
        done

        # ---- THE SCHEMA, PUT INTO THE TWO FRESH DATABASES -----------------------------
        # It is TWO invocations and it carries the SAME composed lane environment the
        # lanes carry (gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1). Rev 6 sent three bare assignments and
        # neither DB_CONNECTION nor the host/port, so the deployed `.env` decided the
        # connection: production runs DB_CONNECTION=central
        # (dev:apps/api/config/database.php:105-133 and its own comment), the `central`
        # connection reads DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE, and `php artisan migrate --force` would
        # have migrated the DEPLOYED central database while the freshly created
        # ${tenant_db} stayed empty. DB_CONNECTION=pgsql travels from the manifest, and
        # `pgsql` reads DB_DATABASE (:87-99), so each invocation points the DEFAULT
        # connection at exactly ONE of the two databases, by name, from the manifest.
        #
        # Why two: AutoERP is database-per-tenant. `database/migrations/` is the CENTRAL
        # set (39 files at dev) and `database/migrations/tenant/` the TENANT set (586) --
        # the tenant path is not recursive from the default migrate path, which is why
        # config/tenancy.php:195-197 hands `--path => database_path("migrations/tenant")`
        # to tenants:migrate. The campaign's PG lane is the single-connection compat lane,
        # so the two sets go into the two isolated databases explicitly rather than through
        # tenants:migrate, which needs a provisioned tenant this preflight has not created.
        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
          "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && $(env_quote "$(env_merge "$(env_merge "$pgenv" "$pgsenv")" "DB_DATABASE=${central_db}")")php artisan migrate --force" \
          >/dev/null 2>&1 \
          || die2 "central migrate --force failed against ${central_db}" "staging_db_migrate_failed"

        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
          "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && $(env_compose "$pgenv" "$pgsenv")php artisan migrate --force --path=database/migrations/tenant" \
          >/dev/null 2>&1 \
          || die2 "tenant migrate --force failed against ${tenant_db}" "staging_db_tenant_migrate_failed"


        # 5. REDIS DB 14 IS NOT THE DEPLOYED ONE, and Horizon can pause ONE supervisor.
        #    Check 5a is the one that stops a data-loss incident: the dedup test flushes its
        #    configured Redis database in setUp/tearDown.
        out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan tinker --execute='echo config(\"database.redis.default.database\");'" 2>&1)" \
          || die2 "cannot read the deployed REDIS_DB: ${out}" "staging_redis_read_failed"
        [[ "$(tr -dc '0-9' <<<"$out")" == "14" ]] \
          && die2 "the deployed REDIS_DB is 14 — pick another isolation db" "staging_redis_db_collision"

        out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan list horizon" 2>&1)" \
          || die2 "cannot list horizon commands: ${out}" "staging_horizon_list_failed"
        if grep -q 'horizon:pause-supervisor' <<<"$out"; then
          HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE=1
        else
          # NOT fatal: the EC-16a row declares itself local-only with this exact reason
          # rather than pausing the whole queue estate on a shared staging host.
          HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE=0
          echo "preflight: horizon:pause-supervisor is unavailable — EC-16a will be recorded local-only (declared)" >&2
        fi

        echo "preflight: staging isolation OK (tenant=${STAGING_TENANT} disposable, ${tenant_db} + ${central_db} created empty and migrated, redis!=14)"
      }

      # ---- EC-16a's PAUSED OBSERVATION WINDOW, EXECUTED BY THIS RUNNER ------------------
      # gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 4: rev 5's staging_preflight() set HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE
      # and NOTHING ever read it -- the `pg` wrapper ran every row identically and the pause,
      # the observation and the cleanup existed only as a manual snippet four hundred lines
      # away. So `--staging-replay` executed EC-16a with the queue RUNNING, which is the one
      # condition the row is about, and the "local-only when unsupported" branch was never
      # written to the ledger either.
      #
      # These two functions bracket the EC-16a row inside the execution loop. The RESUME
      # lives in campaign_cleanup()'s trap, installed before the pause, so an interrupt
      # between them cannot strand a supervisor on a shared host.
      EC16A_SUPERVISOR="identity-revocation"

      ec16a_begin() {   # returns 1 when the row must be recorded local-only
        if (( ! HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE )); then
          printf '{"kind":"LANE","key":"EC-16a","lane":"queue","rows":"EC-16a","result":"SKIPPED",\
"exit":0,"execution_target":"local-only (declared)","reason":"horizon:pause-supervisor unavailable on this \
Horizon version; the whole-estate horizon:pause is refused on a shared staging host"}\n' \
            >> "${RUN_DIR}/ledger.ndjson"
          return 1
        fi

        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" \
          | tee "${RUN_DIR}/horizon-before.txt"
        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
          "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:pause-supervisor ${EC16A_SUPERVISOR}" \
          || die2 "could not pause ${EC16A_SUPERVISOR} on staging" "staging_horizon_pause_failed"
        HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR="$EC16A_SUPERVISOR"   # the trap now owns the resume

        return 0
      }

      # THE PROBE, as a single-quoted shell string so bash expands nothing in it. It
      # ASSERTS rather than prints (gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 2): rev 6's observer echoed
      # every job on the queue and succeeded on ZERO rows, decoded no tenant and compared
      # nothing, so "the queued retry was observed" rested on a human reading a log.
      # Correlation is by the job's OWN tenant id -- the serialized command in
      # data.command carries it -- never by "the most recent row", which is the race the
      # pause exists to remove.
      EC16A_PROBE='$t = (string) getenv("RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT"); $rows = DB::table("jobs")->where("queue", "identity-revocation")->get(); $n = 0; foreach ($rows as $r) { $p = json_decode($r->payload, true); $c = (string) ($p["data"]["command"] ?? ""); $hit = $t !== "" && str_contains($c, $t); echo $r->id, " ", $p["displayName"] ?? "?", " attempts=", $r->attempts, " tenant_match=", $hit ? "yes" : "no", PHP_EOL; if ($hit) { $n++; } } echo "MATCHING_JOBS=", $n, PHP_EOL;'

      ec16a_observe() {   # $1 lane env  $2 lane staging_env -- read INSIDE the pause, AFTER the row ran
        local out remote_env
        # The SAME composed lane environment the row itself ran with, so the probe reads
        # the ISOLATED jobs table and not the deployed one. Rev 6's observer bound none.
        remote_env="$(env_compose "$1" "$2")RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=$(shq "$STAGING_TENANT") "
        out="$("$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
              "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && ${remote_env}php artisan tinker --execute=$(shq "$EC16A_PROBE")" 2>&1)" || true
        printf '%s\n' "$out" | tee "${RUN_DIR}/ec-16a-jobs.txt"

        grep -qE '^MATCHING_JOBS=[1-9][0-9]*$' <<<"$out" || {
          echo "EC-16a: the paused window holds NO identity-revocation job carrying tenant ${STAGING_TENANT}. \
The forced central-delete failure did not queue a retry, or it queued one for a different tenant — either way \
the row's whole claim is unproven. RED." >&2
          return 1
        }
      }

      ec16a_end() {   # resume NOW so the row's own assertion can observe the retry
        [[ -z "$HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR" ]] && return 0
        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
          "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:continue-supervisor ${HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR}" \
          || die2 "could not resume ${HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR}" "staging_horizon_resume_failed"
        "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" \
          | tee "${RUN_DIR}/horizon-after.txt"
        HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR=""

        # horizon-before.txt and horizon-after.txt MUST match; a mismatch is a RED.
        diff -q "${RUN_DIR}/horizon-before.txt" "${RUN_DIR}/horizon-after.txt" >/dev/null \
          || { echo "EC-16a: horizon status differs before/after — RED" >&2; return 1; }

        return 0
      }

      # ---- THE STAGING CLEANUP TRAP, installed BEFORE anything is created --------------
      # It fires on success, on failure and on an interrupt, and it is idempotent: each arm
      # checks what it is undoing. Rev 5 created nothing and dropped nothing, and printed a
      # manual Horizon snippet the runner never invoked (plan gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKERs 3
      # and 4). Nothing this trap touches exists unless THIS run made it.
      campaign_cleanup() {
        local rc=$?
        trap - EXIT INT TERM

        if [[ -n "$HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR" ]]; then
          "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
            "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:continue-supervisor ${HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR}" \
            || echo "WARNING: could not resume ${HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR} — resume it by hand" >&2
          "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" \
            | tee "${RUN_DIR:-/tmp}/horizon-after.txt"
          HORIZON_PAUSED_SUPERVISOR=""
        fi

        local db
        for db in "${CREATED_DBS[@]:-}"; do
          [[ -z "$db" ]] && continue
          "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" "psql -Atqc 'DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS ${db} WITH (FORCE)'" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
            || echo "WARNING: could not drop ${db} — drop it by hand before the next replay" >&2
        done
        CREATED_DBS=()

        return "$rc"
      }
      (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && trap campaign_cleanup EXIT INT TERM

      campaign_lib_preflight            # the two curl --fail health checks + run id + exports
      (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && staging_preflight

      RUN_DIR="${REPO_ROOT}/apps/web/test-results/campaign-${CAMPAIGN_RUN_ID}"
      mkdir -p "$RUN_DIR"
      # shellcheck disable=SC2034  # named for the handback and the operator; the python
      # accumulator below derives the same path from $RUN_DIR
      LEDGER="${RUN_DIR}/ledger.json"
      : > "${RUN_DIR}/ledger.ndjson"

      # ---- step 1-4: the manifest becomes a deduplicated, ordered execution plan ----
      # RECORD: kind US key US lane US cwd US env US staging_env US rows(comma) US command
      #      (env and staging_env are the lane's space-joined K=V lists, straight from the
      #       manifest; the shell composes them with env_compose() — gate r6, W4 BLOCKER 1)
      #
      # THE SEPARATOR IS US (0x1f), NOT TAB, and that is load-bearing. TAB is an IFS
      # WHITESPACE character, so bash's `read` collapses a RUN of tabs into one delimiter
      # and an EMPTY field disappears. Rev 6 emitted a tab-separated record with an `env`
      # field that is empty for the sqlite, vitest, posvitest, shell, playwright and manual
      # lanes — so on every one of those rows `rows` was read into `env`, `cmd` into `rows`
      # and `cmd` came out EMPTY, which made the lane run nothing and report PASS. Found by
      # exercising the runner on the fake-ssh harness for this revision; US is not an IFS
      # whitespace character, so empty fields survive.
      plan() {
        ( cd "${REPO_ROOT}/apps/api" && MANIFEST="$MANIFEST" ONLY_ROW="$ONLY_ROW" ONLY_LEG="$ONLY_LEG" \
            STAGING_REPLAY="$STAGING_REPLAY" php -r '
          require "vendor/autoload.php";
          $m = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["MANIFEST"]);
          $onlyRow = $_SERVER["ONLY_ROW"] ?? ""; $onlyLeg = $_SERVER["ONLY_LEG"] ?? "";
          // THE REPLAY SET IS GENERATED FROM `staging_replay: required` (gate r6, W4
          // BLOCKER 3). Rev 6 never passed STAGING_REPLAY into the planner and never read
          // the field, so `--staging-replay` planned all 58 rows and all nine destructive
          // browser legs while the plan claimed the field "generates the replay list".
          // An EXPLICIT --row/--leg still wins: an operator replaying one row is not
          // overruled by the manifest.
          $replay = ($_SERVER["STAGING_REPLAY"] ?? "0") === "1" && $onlyRow === "" && $onlyLeg === "";
          $legs = []; foreach ($m["legs"] as $l) { $legs[$l["id"]] = $l; }
          $legOrder = array_keys($legs);
          $legRows = []; $laneCmds = []; $manual = [];
          foreach ($m["rows"] as $row) {
            if ($onlyRow !== "" && $row["id"] !== $onlyRow) { continue; }
            if ($replay && ($row["staging_replay"] ?? "") !== "required") { continue; }
            foreach ($row["homes"] as $h) {
              if (isset($h["leg"])) {                       // step 2/3: leg reference
                if (! isset($legs[$h["leg"]])) { fwrite(STDERR, "unknown leg {$h["leg"]} on {$row["id"]}\n"); exit(2); }
                $legRows[$h["leg"]][] = $row["id"];
              } elseif (($h["lane"] ?? "") === "manual") {  // never executed; recorded
                $manual[] = [$row["id"], $h["result"] ?? "NOT_RUN", $h["doc"] ?? ""];
              } else {                                      // step 4: (lane, command) pair
                $laneCmds[$h["lane"]."\x1f".$h["command"]][] = $row["id"];
              }
            }
          }
          // step 3: dedup by leg id, DECLARED ORDER, each exactly once.
          foreach ($legOrder as $id) {
            // A GENERATED REPLAY PLANS NO LEG. None of the 19 `staging_replay: required`
            // rows references one (verified by replay_selection_guard below), the
            // playwright lane declares itself `not replayed (declared)`, and R0/R1 carry
            // `covers: []` — so without this arm the two orphan legs, both of which
            // CREATE AND MUTATE a campaign tenant, would have run on every replay.
            if ($replay) { continue; }
            if ($onlyLeg !== "" && $id !== $onlyLeg) { continue; }
            if ($onlyLeg === "" && $onlyRow !== "" && ! isset($legRows[$id])) { continue; }
            if ($onlyLeg === "" && $onlyRow === "" && ! isset($legRows[$id]) && ($legs[$id]["covers"] ?? []) !== []) { continue; }
            $lane = $m["lanes"][$legs[$id]["lane"]];
            printf("LEG\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\n", $id, $legs[$id]["lane"], $lane["cwd"],
              implode(" ", $lane["env"] ?: []), implode(" ", $lane["staging_env"] ?: []),
              implode(",", $legRows[$id] ?? []), $legs[$id]["command"]);
          }
          foreach ($laneCmds as $key => $rows) {
            [$laneName, $cmd] = explode("\x1f", $key, 2);
            if ($onlyLeg !== "") { continue; }              // --leg runs the leg, nothing else
            $lane = $m["lanes"][$laneName];
            printf("LANE\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\n", $laneName."/".substr(sha1($cmd), 0, 8), $laneName,
              $lane["cwd"], implode(" ", $lane["env"] ?: []), implode(" ", $lane["staging_env"] ?: []),
              implode(",", $rows), $cmd);
          }
          if ($onlyLeg !== "") { $manual = []; }   // --leg runs that leg and nothing else
          foreach ($manual as [$rowId, $result, $doc]) {
            printf("MANUAL\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\x1f%s\n", $rowId, "manual", ".", "", "", $rowId, $result." ".$doc);
          }
        ' )
      }

      # ---- THE REPLAY SELECTION IS CHECKED AGAINST THE MANIFEST -------------------------
      # gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 3. "The replay list is generated from `staging_replay:
      # required`" is only true if something proves it, so this compares the DECLARED set
      # to the set the planner actually produced and refuses a mismatch. It runs only for a
      # GENERATED replay — an explicit --row/--leg deliberately selects something else.
      replay_selection_guard() {
        local declared planned
        declared="$( cd "${REPO_ROOT}/apps/api" && MANIFEST="$MANIFEST" php -r '
          require "vendor/autoload.php";
          $m = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["MANIFEST"]);
          $ids = [];
          foreach ($m["rows"] as $r) { if (($r["staging_replay"] ?? "") === "required") { $ids[] = $r["id"]; } }
          sort($ids); echo implode(",", $ids);' )"
        planned="$(plan | cut -d$'\x1f' -f7 | tr "," "\n" | sed "/^$/d" | sort -u | paste -sd, -)"

        [[ -n "$declared" ]] || die2 "the manifest declares no staging_replay: required row" "staging_replay_set_empty"
        [[ "$declared" == "$planned" ]] || die2 \
          "the generated replay plans [${planned}] but the manifest declares [${declared}]" \
          "staging_replay_selection_mismatch"

        echo "replay selection: $(tr "," "\n" <<<"$declared" | wc -l | tr -d " ") rows declared \
staging_replay: required, and exactly those planned — ${declared}"
      }

      # ---- the staging execution target for a lane (gate r2 B5; gate r3 W4 BLOCKER 5) ----
      # ISOLATION IS MANDATORY. Read-only lanes run against the deployed tree as-is; every
      # MUTATING lane runs ONLY against the disposable staging tenant named by
      # --staging-tenant, on the isolated test databases and on Redis DB 14. A lane with no
      # staging path declares local-only; it never silently runs locally and reports staging.
      # EVERY REMOTE COMMAND CARRIES ITS LANE'S FULL COMPOSED ENVIRONMENT, generated from
      # the manifest by env_compose()/env_docker_flags() — never hand-listed here (gate r6,
      # Wave 4 BLOCKER 1). The env arrives as fields 5 and 6 of the plan record, so this
      # function contains no database name, no host, no port and no cache store: adding a
      # key to a lane's `env:`/`staging_env:` map is the whole change.
      staging_wrap() {   # $1 lane  $2 cwd  $3 env  $4 staging_env  $5 command -> "TARGET\tcommand"
        local lane="$1" cwd="$2" env="$3" senv="$4" cmd="$5" e rdb
        case "$lane" in
          pg)
            e="$(env_compose "$env" "$senv")RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=$(shq "$STAGING_TENANT") "
            printf 'staging:ssh\t%s %s %s' "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
              "$(shq "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && ${e}${cmd}")" ;;
          redis)
            # The evidence tail reads the version of the db the LANE declares, not a
            # literal 14 — the two cannot drift because both come from env_value().
            e="$(env_compose "$env" "$senv")RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=$(shq "$STAGING_TENANT") "
            rdb="$(env_value REDIS_DB "$env" "$senv")"
            printf 'staging:ssh\t%s %s %s' "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
              "$(shq "cd ${REMOTE_ROOT}/apps/api && ${e}${cmd} && redis-cli -n ${rdb} INFO server | grep redis_version")" ;;
          shell)
            # gate r4, Wave 4 MAJOR 1: --remote-root is the SSH HOST's checkout path and was
            # also being pasted inside `docker exec`, where nothing proves the same path
            # exists. The container's workdir is its own flag (--container-root, default
            # /var/www/html) and is applied with `docker exec -w`, so the command is
            # relative to it rather than absolutely rooted at the host's path.
            # `docker exec` inherits NOTHING from the ssh session, so the same composed map
            # is emitted as `-e` flags rather than as an assignment prefix.
            e="$(env_docker_flags "$env" "$senv")-e $(shq "RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=${STAGING_TENANT}") "
            printf 'staging:ssh\t%s %s %s' "$SSH_BIN" "$SSH_TARGET" \
              "$(shq "docker exec -w ${CONTAINER_ROOT}/apps/api ${e}${API_CONTAINER} ${cmd}")" ;;
          vitest|posvitest)
            printf 'local-only (declared)\t%s' "$cmd" ;;
          playwright)
            printf 'not replayed (declared)\t:' ;;
          manual)
            printf 'staging:manual\t:' ;;
          *)
            printf 'local\t%s' "$cmd" ;;
        esac
      }

      # THE GENERATED REPLAY SET IS PROVED AGAINST THE MANIFEST BEFORE ANYTHING EXECUTES.
      # It sits here, after plan() and replay_selection_guard() are defined and before the
      # loop consumes plan()'s output — a bash function must be defined before it is called.
      (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && [[ -z "$ONLY_ROW" && -z "$ONLY_LEG" ]] && { replay_selection_guard || exit 2; }

      # ---- step 5: execute, ACCUMULATING ------------------------------------------
      red=0
      executed=0
      while IFS=$'\x1f' read -r kind key lane cwd env senv rows cmd; do
        [[ -z "${kind:-}" ]] && continue

        # --lanes filters BOTH kinds. gate r4, Wave 4 BLOCKER 2: rev 4 filtered LANE records
        # only, so `--lanes pg,sqlite,redis,vitest,posvitest,shell` — advertised as "the
        # non-browser rows" — still ran all nine destructive playwright LEGS and created and
        # mutated a campaign tenant. A leg still runs under --lanes when the operator names
        # its lane (`--lanes playwright`) or names the leg itself (`--leg R2`).
        if [[ -n "$LANES" && -z "$ONLY_LEG" && "$kind" != "MANUAL" && ",${LANES}," != *",${lane},"* ]]; then continue; fi

        # A MANUAL ROW GOES THROUGH staging_wrap TOO (gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 3). Rev 6
        # wrote `execution_target:"manual"` and `continue`d BEFORE the wrapper — and the
        # verification contract permits exactly `staging:ssh`, `staging:manual`,
        # `local-only (declared)` and `not replayed (declared)`, so every replay run
        # emitted a fifth, forbidden value and the four-string assertion could never pass.
        if [[ "$kind" == "MANUAL" ]]; then
          mtarget="manual"
          (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && IFS=$'\t' read -r mtarget _ < <(staging_wrap manual "$cwd" "" "" ":")
          printf '{"kind":"manual","row":"%s","result":"%s","execution_target":"%s"}\n' \
            "$rows" "$cmd" "$mtarget" >> "${RUN_DIR}/ledger.ndjson"
          (( executed++ ))
          continue
        fi

        target="local"; run="$cmd"
        if (( STAGING_REPLAY )); then
          IFS=$'\t' read -r target run < <(staging_wrap "$lane" "$cwd" "$env" "$senv" "$cmd")
        fi

        # EC-16a IS THE ONE ROW WITH A QUEUE PRECONDITION, and it is bracketed here rather
        # than described in a snippet (gate r5, Wave 4 BLOCKER 4). Under --staging-replay the
        # supervisor is paused, the pending job is read INSIDE the pause, the row runs, and
        # the supervisor is resumed so the retry can be observed. When pause-supervisor is
        # unavailable the row is written to the ledger as local-only (declared) and SKIPPED —
        # which is what makes the declared branch auditable instead of invisible.
        ec16a=0
        if (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && [[ ",${rows}," == *",EC-16a,"* ]]; then
          if ec16a_begin; then
            ec16a=1
          else
            (( executed++ ))
            continue
          fi
        fi

        echo "== ${kind} ${key} [${lane}] rows=${rows} target=${target}"
        started=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)
        if [[ "$run" == ":" ]]; then
          status=0; result="SKIPPED"
        else
          (
            cd "${REPO_ROOT}/${cwd}" || exit 1
            # A LOCAL run gets the lane env by export; a STAGING run gets it inside the
            # remote command line, because ssh forwards no exported variable (gate r6,
            # Wave 4 BLOCKER 1). `staging_env` is deliberately NOT applied locally.
            # shellcheck disable=SC2163  # $env is a space-joined K=V LIST and
            # `export K=V K=V` is exactly the intent; manifest rule 7 makes the split total
            [[ -n "$env" ]] && export $env
            eval "$run"
          ) > "${RUN_DIR}/${key//\//_}.out" 2>&1
          status=$?
          result=$([[ $status -eq 0 ]] && echo PASS || echo FAIL)
        fi
        if (( ec16a )); then
          # OBSERVE AFTER THE ROW HAS RUN AND WHILE THE SUPERVISOR IS STILL PAUSED, then
          # resume (gate r6, Wave 4 BLOCKER 2). Rev 6 called ec16a_observe() IMMEDIATELY
          # after the pause and BEFORE the forced-failure row, so it could not possibly see
          # the job that row produces — the documented sequence (pause, run, observe,
          # resume) and the implemented one were in different orders.
          ec16a_observe "$env" "$senv" || { status=1; result="FAIL"; red=1; }
          ec16a_end                    || { status=1; result="FAIL"; red=1; }
          ec16a=0
        fi

        (( status != 0 )) && red=1
        (( executed++ ))
        # A staging-replay row whose target is still `local` is a RED: the lane had no
        # staging path and did not declare one.
        if (( STAGING_REPLAY )) && [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then result="FAIL"; red=1; fi

        printf '{"kind":"%s","key":"%s","lane":"%s","rows":"%s","result":"%s","exit":%d,"execution_target":"%s","started_at":"%s","output":"%s"}\n' \
          "$kind" "$key" "$lane" "$rows" "$result" "$status" "$target" "$started" "${key//\//_}.out" >> "${RUN_DIR}/ledger.ndjson"
      done < <(plan)

      # THE EMPTY-SELECTION GUARD. validate_selectors() rejects an unknown id; this rejects
      # a VALID combination that intersects to nothing (`--row EC-22 --lanes pg` on a
      # withdrawn row, `--lanes shell --row EC-1`). Either way the answer is exit 2 with a
      # reason, never exit 0 with an empty ledger (gate r4, Wave 4 BLOCKER 1).
      if (( executed == 0 )); then
        echo "Selection matched nothing: row='${ONLY_ROW}' leg='${ONLY_LEG}' lanes='${LANES}'" >&2
        die2 "a campaign that executed no entry is not a green campaign" "empty_selection"
      fi

      # One ledger.json, so the evidence document and Task 4-5's re-run read one file.
      # R0 writes .tenant_id into it; Task 4-5 step 2 reads the tenant from there.
      python3 - "$RUN_DIR" <<'PY'
      import json, sys, pathlib
      d = pathlib.Path(sys.argv[1])
      entries = [json.loads(l) for l in (d / "ledger.ndjson").read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]
      out = d / "ledger.json"
      prev = json.loads(out.read_text()) if out.exists() else {}
      prev["entries"] = entries
      out.write_text(json.dumps(prev, indent=2) + "\n")
      print(f"Ledger: {out}  entries={len(entries)}  red={sum(1 for e in entries if e.get('result')=='FAIL')}")
      PY

      echo "RBAC campaign run-id: ${CAMPAIGN_RUN_ID}  entries=${executed}"
      exit "$red"
      ~~~

      > **Reading the YAML with `symfony/yaml` from `apps/api/vendor` rather than `yq`** is deliberate: `yq` is not a repository dependency and is not installed on the staging host, while `symfony/yaml` already is and is the same parser `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` uses. **One parser, one truth** — a runner and a test that disagree about the manifest is the worst possible failure here.
      > **The plan is emitted as one US-separated (0x1f) record per entry and consumed by one `while read` loop**, so the dedup happens **once**, in the PHP that can see the whole file, rather than being re-derived in shell per row. `--row` filters rows, `--leg` runs exactly one leg and nothing else, `--lanes` filters lane commands but never legs.
      > **`execution_target` is written into every ledger entry**, and a staging-replay entry whose target is still `local` is forced to `FAIL` — the lane had no staging path and did not declare one, which is precisely the silent-success failure **B5** is about.
      > **Accumulation, not short-circuit:** `red=1` is set and the loop continues; the script exits non-zero at the end. *"Promotion reads the ledger, not the exit code"* is unusable if the first red stops the run.

      3. **NO SELECTOR CAN EVER PRODUCE AN EMPTY GREEN RUN** (gate r4, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 1**). `validate_selectors()` runs before anything executes and the `executed == 0` guard runs after; every arm is `exit 2` with a `reason=`. **Rev 5 EXERCISED all six against the real script**, with a two-leg/three-row manifest:
      >
      | Invocation | exit | `reason=` |
      |---|---:|---|
      | `--reuse --leg R2 --leg R3` | 2 | `leg_repeated` — *"--leg may be given at most once (got 'R2' then 'R3')"*. **Rev 4 silently overwrote and ran R3 alone** |
      | `--leg R99` | 2 | `unknown_leg` — *"Declared legs: R2, R3"*. **Rev 4 ran nothing and exited 0** |
      | `--row EC-999` | 2 | `unknown_row` — *"The manifest declares 3 rows"*. Same defect |
      | `--lanes nope` | 2 | `unknown_lane` — *"Declared lanes: pg, shell, playwright"* |
      | `--reuse` (no leg) | 2 | `reuse_requires_leg` |
      | `--row EC-3 --lanes shell` (both valid, intersection empty) | 2 | `empty_selection` — *"a campaign that executed no entry is not a green campaign"* |
      4. **`--lanes` FILTERS LEGS AS WELL AS LANE COMMANDS** (gate r4, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 2**). Rev 4 filtered only `kind == LANE`, so `--lanes pg,sqlite,redis,vitest,posvitest,shell` — advertised as *"the non-browser rows"* — still ran all nine `destructive: true` browser legs and created and mutated a campaign tenant. A tester asking for non-browser verification must never do that. **Exercised on the same harness:** `--lanes pg` runs **1** entry (`LANE pg/…`) and **no** leg; `--lanes playwright` runs **2** entries, both legs; `--lanes pg --leg R2` runs **1** entry, `LEG R2` — an explicit `--leg` overrides the lane filter, which is the escape hatch.
      5. **`--staging-replay` requires `--ssh`, `--api-container` AND `--staging-tenant`** (`exit 2`, three distinct reasons), then **executes `staging_preflight()`** — five checks, before the plan is even built (**BLOCKER 3**; the table is in Task 4-5 step 4). Each lane then replays per that step's lane table: `ssh` for pg/redis/queue, `ssh` + **`docker exec -w <container-root>/apps/api`** for shell, `local-only (declared)` for the two vitest lanes, `not replayed (declared)` for playwright (**BLOCKER B5**). **Exercised against a fake `ssh`** (`CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN`), all six arms:
      >
      | Fake staging state | exit | `reason=` |
      |---|---:|---|
      | everything healthy | 0/1 | `preflight: staging isolation OK (tenant=… disposable, autoerp_staging_campaign + autoerp_staging_campaign_central created empty and migrated, redis!=14)`, then every planned lane runs with `execution_target=staging:ssh` |
      | ssh unreachable | 2 | `staging_ssh_unreachable` |
      | tenant not in `tenants:list` | 2 | `staging_tenant_absent` |
      | `rbac:tenant-is-disposable` exits 1 | 2 | `staging_tenant_not_disposable` — *"refusing to mutate it"* |
      | the freshly created database reports 7 `public` tables | 2 | `staging_db_not_empty` |
      | a database of that name already exists, no `--drop-stale` | 2 | `staging_db_stale` |
      | the central `migrate --force` fails | 2 | `staging_db_migrate_failed` |
      | deployed `REDIS_DB` is 14 | 2 | `staging_redis_db_collision` |
      6. **THE REMOTE COMMAND CARRIES THE LANE'S WHOLE ENVIRONMENT, AND THE REPLAY SET IS THE MANIFEST'S** (gate r6, Wave 4 **BLOCKERs 1 and 3**). Re-exercised on the fake-`ssh` harness on 2026-09-11, with a six-row manifest carrying one row per lane arm. **The generated remote command lines, pasted verbatim from the fake `ssh`'s argv log:**
      >
      ```text

      cd /var/www/autoerp/apps/api && DB_CONNECTION='pgsql' DB_HOST='127.0.0.1' DB_PORT='5432' \
        DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign_central' DB_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign_central' \
        php artisan migrate --force
      cd /var/www/autoerp/apps/api && DB_CONNECTION='pgsql' DB_HOST='127.0.0.1' DB_PORT='5432' \
        DB_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign' DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign_central' \
        php artisan migrate --force --path=database/migrations/tenant

      # a pg lane row
      cd /var/www/autoerp/apps/api && DB_CONNECTION='pgsql' DB_HOST='127.0.0.1' DB_PORT='5432' \
        DB_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign' DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign_central' \
        RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT='11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555' \
        ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionsSyncTest.php

      # the redis lane row -- all four keys, and `redis-cli -n 14` derived from REDIS_DB
      cd /var/www/autoerp/apps/api && REDIS_HOST='127.0.0.1' REDIS_PORT='6379' REDIS_DB='14' \
        CACHE_STORE='redis' RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT='11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555' \
        ./vendor/bin/phpunit --testdox tests/Feature/Identity/DenialDedupTest.php \
        && redis-cli -n 14 INFO server | grep redis_version

      # the shell lane row -- docker exec inherits nothing, so the map becomes -e flags
      docker exec -w /var/www/html/apps/api -e 'RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555' \
        autoerp-api bash tests/EdgeCases/boot-status.sh

      # EC-16a's probe, INSIDE the pause and AFTER the row ran, bound to the isolated pair
      cd /var/www/autoerp/apps/api && DB_CONNECTION='pgsql' DB_HOST='127.0.0.1' DB_PORT='5432' \
        DB_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign' DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE='autoerp_staging_campaign_central' \
        RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT='11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555' \
        php artisan tinker --execute='$t = (string) getenv("RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT"); … echo "MATCHING_JOBS=", $n, PHP_EOL;'
      ```
      >
      **Compare rev 6's**, which is what BLOCKER 1 is: `cd …/apps/api && DB_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=… RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=… <cmd>` — **no `DB_CONNECTION`, no host, no port**, so the deployed `DB_CONNECTION=central` decided the connection and the lane read `DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE`; and `REDIS_DB=14 <cmd>` — **no host, no port, no `CACHE_STORE`**.
      >
      **And the run/selection contracts, from the same harness:**
      >
      | Invocation | exit | Observed |
      |---|---:|---|
      | `--staging-replay` (generated) | 1 | `replay selection: 5 rows declared staging_replay: required, and exactly those planned — EC-16a,EC-28,EC-31,EC-32d,EC-8`; **no browser leg planned**; the 1 is the harness's own `pnpm` absence on the `local-only (declared)` lane |

      | `--staging-replay --row EC-1` (a NON-replay row) | 0 | the explicit selector wins: `LEG R2 … target=not replayed (declared)` **and** `LANE pg … target=staging:ssh` |

      | the same, no matching job | 1 | `EC-16a: the paused window holds NO identity-revocation job carrying tenant … RED.` |
      >
      **`execution_target` over a whole generated replay**: `staging:ssh` ×4, `local-only (declared)` ×1 (EC-31's `posvitest` home), `staging:manual` ×1 (EC-31's manual home — which rev 6 wrote as the forbidden fifth value `manual`). `not replayed (declared)` appears only when an explicit `--leg`/`--row` pulls a browser leg in, which is the `--row EC-1` line above. **All four permitted strings are reachable and no fifth is.**

Rev 5 created the test and did not stage the guard edit, leaving `test_rule_2_every_command_resolves_in_its_lanes_cwd` **red on the commit that made it red**. The edit is one deleted line:

      Subject: **`Phase 0.7.3: Add the nine RBAC campaign browser legs, the shared runner library and the manifest-driven script`**, both trailers. The body states why there are nine and not ten (**M4-1**), why the runner library is shared (**M4-2**), and that `RetiredKeyAbsenceTest.php` leaves `WAVE_4_CREATES` in this commit.

## Task 4-4: fixes for red rows

**Files:** whatever the campaign finds — each fix its own commit, its own module, its own reviewer.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — triage each red row through `docs/qa/MANUAL-TESTING-LOOP.md`.** One row per finding, as the loop already defines. **A red row is a finding, not a test to weaken.**
- [ ] **Step 2 — fix, one commit per finding**, path-scoped to the module that owns it, each with **its own NUMERIC ordinal**: `Phase 0.7.6`, `Phase 0.7.7`, `Phase 0.7.8`, … in the order the campaign found them, subject `Phase 0.7.<n>: <imperative summary of the fix>`. The body names the EC row.
      > **Two corrections, one from each of the last two gates.** **(a) Rev 4 gave every fix the identical `Phase 0.7.4` prefix** (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 6**). That satisfies `AGENTS.md:15-16`'s grammar and defeats its purpose: several unrelated fixes become indistinguishable in phase history, and `git log --grep='^Phase 0.7.4'` cannot tell a reviewer which commit closed which row. **(b) Rev 6's answer — `0.7.4a`, `0.7.4b`, … — is not a valid patch segment** (gate r6, Wave 4 **MAJOR 2**). `AGENTS.md:15-16` requires `Phase <major.minor.patch>`, and the wave-2 plan rejects an alphanumeric segment in terms this plan inherits: *"`AGENTS.md`'s pattern is three integers and a non-numeric segment would break any tooling that parses it"* (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-2.md:325-330`). The programme cannot forbid `Phase 0.4.2a` in one plan and mint `Phase 0.7.4a` in another.
      > **The allocation.** The `0.7` lane itself is correct and collision-free; only the patch component moves. This wave's tasks hold `0.7.1` (4-1), `0.7.2` (4-2), `0.7.3` (4-3) and `0.7.5` (4-5), and `0.7.4` is Task 4-4's own base — so **fix commits take `0.7.6` upward, one per finding, allocated when the row goes RED rather than at commit time**. The reserved range is `0.7.6`…`0.7.31`, twenty-six slots: **a campaign with more than twenty-six red rows is not a fix round, it is a re-plan**, and the handback says so instead of extending the range.
      > **The patch number is an identifier, not a running order.** Task 4-4 still executes between 4-3 and 4-5; `0.7.6` merely sits after `0.7.5` in the numbering, exactly as `Phase 0.4.10` does in the wave-2 lane. The evidence document's **Findings** table is where the ordinal↔row mapping lives — one row per finding, carrying the failing run id, the passing run id and the ordinal — and `git log --grep='^Phase 0\.7\.[0-9]\+:'` lists every commit in the lane with no alphanumeric tail to filter out.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the owning reviewer gates each fix**, not the campaign as a whole: `tenancy-authz-reviewer` for an authorization fix (**mandatory** on every one), plus `fiscal-pos-reviewer` for a POS fix, `inventory-costing-reviewer` for an inventory one, `treasury-reviewer` for a treasury one, `frontend-conventions-reviewer` for an `apps/web` one.
- [ ] **Step 4 — re-run the row's leg** and record **both** the failing and the passing run id in the evidence document's **Findings** table. A finding with no before-and-after is indistinguishable from a row that always passed.
- [ ] **Step 5 — if a finding cannot be fixed in this wave**, it becomes a **Known red** row in `docs/qa/RBAC-EDGE-CAMPAIGN.md` with an owner and a ticket, and the campaign is **RED by construction** until it closes — exactly as the onboarding campaign handles its own known reds, where *"the promotion checklist row accepts it only with those findings named and owned"*.
