## Task 4-5: the green run, the re-run, and the staging replay

**Files:** the evidence document; `docs/qa/RBAC-EDGE-CAMPAIGN.md`'s Known-red section

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the green local run.** `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh` end to end, every leg PASS or a named NOT_SCRIPTABLE, every lane test green. Fill the evidence document's **Rows** table: run id, result, the **value asserted**, the timestamp, the evidence excerpt.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the same-tenant RE-RUN, at the COMMAND level** (master plan's exit, and convention-09's re-run axis). **The browser legs are NOT replayed** — the campaign is single-run-per-fresh-tenant (Global Constraints, **B4-2**), and by this point R2 has deleted and deactivated principals, R3 has renamed roles and R8 has revoked memberships, so a second pass over them would assert against state the first pass destroyed. **What the master plan's sentence measures is row duplication and sync counters, and both are command-level facts**, so they are measured directly against R0's tenant:
      - **no duplicate role, permission or membership row** — asserted by row counts before and after, per table, recorded in the evidence document;
      - **every `permissions:sync-fleet` counter reports `ALREADY_CURRENT`** with every `mutations` counter zero (the two **gauges** — `orphans`, `templates_skipped_customised` — report whatever is true of the tenant and are **unconstrained**; asserting a gauge at zero on a re-run is a misreading of the marker and will produce a false red);
      - `permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` exits **0** and writes **zero rows**, proven by a query-log count.
      > **Rev 3 scopes both commands to R0's tenant. Rev 2's did not, and under db-per-tenant that meant they measured the wrong thing entirely** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B1**). A bare `permissions:sync-fleet` syncs **every tenant in the directory** — on a shared local or staging database that is dozens of tenants, and *"every counter reports `ALREADY_CURRENT`"* then says nothing about R0's. Worse, the `tinker` row counts ran on the **default connection**, which in a database-per-tenant system is the **central** database (`synerivia_central`) — where `roles`, `role_has_permissions` and `user_company_memberships` **do not exist as the tenant's**. The three assertions the master plan names were being taken against a database the campaign never touched.

      **R0 writes its tenant uuid into the ledger** (`ledger.tenant_id`), and both commands read it from there:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4/apps/api

      # R0's tenant, from the ledger this run wrote -- never typed, never guessed.
      TENANT=$(jq -r '.tenant_id' "../web/test-results/campaign-${CAMPAIGN_RUN_ID}/ledger.json")
      test -n "$TENANT" && test "$TENANT" != "null" || { echo "no tenant_id in the ledger — R0 did not complete"; exit 1; }

      # --tenants=<uuid> is wave 0b's fleet SELECTOR contract, and 4.3.3 step 1a requires
      # each selector to resolve to EXACTLY ONE tenant. Three invocations, all scoped.
      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"
      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet           --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"
      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet           --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"   # expect ALREADY_CURRENT, mutations all zero
      ```
      **Plus the row-count comparison the first bullet names**, taken before and after the second and third invocations and pasted into the evidence document. **It runs through TWO COMMITTED ARTISAN COMMANDS invoked under `tenants:run`, because the rev-3 form is not a valid `tenants:run` invocation** (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 4**).

      > **What rev 3 wrote, and why it cannot run.** `php artisan tenants:run --tenants="$TENANT" 'tinker --execute=…'` passes a single quoted string as `tenants:run`'s **`{commandname}` argument**, and Stancl then calls that exact string as a command name. The installed signature is:
      > ```
      > tenants:run {commandname : The command's name.}
      >             {--tenants=* : The tenant(s) to run the command for. Default: all}
      >             {--argument=* : The arguments to pass to the command. Default: none}
      >             {--option=*   : The options to pass to the command. Default: none}
      > ```
      > (`/Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/apps/api/vendor/stancl/tenancy/src/Commands/Run.php:23-26`), and its `handle()` reduces `--argument`/`--option` into an array before `$this->call($this->argument('commandname'), …)` (`:35-54`). **Options go through `--option="key=value"`, never inline with the command name.** Rev 3's offered fallback — *"a standalone PHP file `tenants:run` invokes by path"* — is also invalid: `tenants:run` calls an **Artisan command name**, not a file path.
      > **The repository already does this correctly and is the precedent to copy:** `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Product/Presentation/Console/CensusProductBarcodeTwinsCommand.php:28` prints *"Run via: php artisan tenants:run products:census-barcode-twins"*, and `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Commands/CloseOrphanedShiftCommand.php:628-630` documents `Stancl\Tenancy\Commands\Run` turning `apply=1` into `['--apply' => '1']`. **A tenant-aware probe is a registered command, invoked by name.**

      **The two probes, registered as closure commands in `apps/api/routes/console.php`** (the file already carries `Artisan::command('inspire', …)` at `dev:apps/api/routes/console.php:26`, so this is the file's existing idiom, and it is staged in Task 4-2 Step 6's add list):
      ```php
      // apps/api/routes/console.php

      // (1) The row census. Runs INSIDE tenancy, so DB::table() is the TENANT database.
      Artisan::command('rbac:row-census', function (): void {
          foreach (['roles', 'permissions', 'role_has_permissions', 'model_has_roles', 'user_company_memberships'] as $t) {
              $this->line(str_pad($t, 28).DB::table($t)->count());
          }
      })->purpose('Print the RBAC row counts for the CURRENTLY BOUND tenant (run under tenants:run).');

      // (2) The dry-run WRITE probe. This one does NOT run under tenants:run: it invokes
      //     the fleet command in-process and listens on every connection the runner opens,
      //     which is the only way to observe what the dry run actually executed.
      Artisan::command('rbac:dry-run-probe {--tenants=}', function (): int {
          $writes = [];
          DB::listen(function ($q) use (&$writes): void {
              if (preg_match('/^\s*(insert|update|delete)\b/i', $q->sql) === 1) {
                  $writes[] = $q->sql;
              }
          });

          $exit = $this->call('permissions:sync-fleet', [
              '--dry-run' => true,
              '--tenants' => [(string) $this->option('tenants')],
          ]);

          $this->line('write statements: '.count($writes));

          foreach ($writes as $sql) {
              $this->line('  '.$sql);
          }

          return $writes === [] && $exit === 0 ? 0 : 1;
      })->purpose('Prove permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run executes ZERO write statements for one tenant.');
      ```
      **and the invocations, both valid against the installed `tenants:run`:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4/apps/api

      # BEFORE the two write invocations
      php artisan tenants:run rbac:row-census --tenants="$TENANT" | tee "$EVIDENCE/census-before.txt"

      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"
      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"   # expect ALREADY_CURRENT, mutations all zero

      # AFTER -- the two files must be IDENTICAL, which is the "no duplicate row" assertion
      php artisan tenants:run rbac:row-census --tenants="$TENANT" | tee "$EVIDENCE/census-after.txt"
      diff "$EVIDENCE/census-before.txt" "$EVIDENCE/census-after.txt" && echo "no duplicate rows"

      # The dry run's "writes zero rows", proven by INSTRUMENTING the command itself
      php artisan rbac:dry-run-probe --tenants="$TENANT" ; echo "exit=$?"       # expect "write statements: 0", exit 0
      ```
      > **`diff` of the before/after census IS the assertion**, not a human comparing two printouts — it exits non-zero on any difference and the evidence document quotes both files.
      > **The dry-run probe instruments the command rather than wrapping it from a second process**, which is what gate r3 asked for and what rev 3 could not express: a `tenants:run` child process cannot install a query listener around a **fleet** command that opens its own per-tenant connections. Running it in-process with `DB::listen` catches statements on **every** connection the runner resolves, tenant connections included. **An exit of 0 from `--dry-run` says it did not fail, not that it did not write**; this probe says the second thing.
      **The narrowing is recorded as a decision in the handback**, with this justification, so a later reader does not read *"re-run end to end"* as a promise this wave quietly dropped. **The bounded alternative** — per-leg fixture resets making every browser leg idempotent — was considered and rejected: it needs a reset contract per mutating leg (five of the nine), it would be the largest single piece of new machinery in a wave that ships almost no production code, and the onboarding campaign this one copies makes the same choice for the same reasons (`docs/qa/ONBOARDING-CAMPAIGN.md`, *Reuse mode*: *"reuse mode is for iterating on a single leg, not for a green run"*).
- [ ] **Step 3 — `tenant:census-day-one` clean**, on the campaign tenant. Record its output verbatim. **Together with the green run this is the promotion precondition for the whole programme.**
- [ ] **Step 4 — the staging replay, driven by the manifest, and EXECUTED AGAINST STAGING PER LANE.**

      > **Rev 2's `--staging-replay` did not replay anything against staging** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B5**). `--api` and `--web` only redirect **HTTP targets** — that is all `campaign-onboarding.sh` uses them for (`dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh:5-6`, exported as `CAMPAIGN_API_URL` / `CAMPAIGN_WEB_URL`). Every non-browser row's command is a **local process**: `./vendor/bin/phpunit` against `autoerp_test_w4` on `127.0.0.1:5433`, `pnpm test` in the local worktree, `tests/EdgeCases/entrypoint-boot-status.sh` against the local filesystem. Under rev 2, `--staging-replay` would have re-run **the same local tests** and filled a table headed *"Replayed on staging?"* with *"yes"* — and the rows that carry the flag are precisely the ones whose **whole point** is that a local run cannot prove them: a real queue (EC-16a), a real Redis at its deployed version (EC-28), real two-connection concurrency (EC-19/20/20a/b/c), the container's own filesystem (EC-32c/d), the real tenant directory (EC-37a/b, EC-38, EC-39, EC-40).
      >
      > **The ruling: each lane either executes against staging explicitly, or declares itself local-only and states the staging evidence it cannot provide.** No lane gets to be silent.

      **ISOLATION IS THE CONTRACT, and rev 3 had none** (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 5**). Rev 3 said *"`ssh <staging> php artisan test`"* against the deployed tree with no isolated database, no tenant selector and no fixture lifecycle, while claiming it exercised *"the deployed PG"* and *"the real tenant directory"*; and its Redis row said to use the deployed `REDIS_*` while `AuthorizationDenialDedupTest` **flushes its configured Redis database in `setUp` and `tearDown`** (`…rbac-wave-2.md`, Task 2a-7 Step 4). Pointed at the application's Redis, that replay would **delete live cache and queue state**. The Wave 4 global rule already requires isolated DB 14; the replay ignored it.

      **The three isolation preconditions, and `--staging-replay` refuses to start without them:**

      | Precondition | Flag | Why it cannot be defaulted |
      |---|---|---|
      | A **disposable staging tenant**, registered for this replay and never a real one | `--staging-tenant <uuid>` | every mutating lane binds to it and nothing else; without it a fleet command touches every tenant on staging |
      | The **api container name** | `--api-container <name>` | the shell lane is `docker exec`; `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status` exists nowhere else |
      | The **container's** checkout path, separately from the host's | `--container-root <path>` (default `/var/www/html`) | gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 1**: rev 4 reused `--remote-root` — an **SSH-host** path — inside `docker exec`, with nothing proving `/var/www/autoerp` is also the container's path. The shell lane now runs `docker exec -w <container-root>/apps/api`, so the two roots cannot be conflated |
      | The **ssh host** | `--ssh user@host` | `--api`/`--web` only redirect HTTP and cannot reach a lane that is not HTTP |

      **How each lane replays, READ-ONLY unless it is named mutating, and this table is the contract `--staging-replay` implements:**

      | Lane | Mutating? | Replays against staging by | Isolation | Evidence it produces |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | **pg** | **yes** | `ssh <host> 'cd <root>/apps/api && DB_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign_central RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=<uuid> php artisan test --testdox <path> --filter <f>'` | **a DEDICATED pair of databases on the staging PG instance**, never `autoerp` / `synerivia_central`, created by the replay's own preflight and dropped after; plus the campaign tenant as the only tenant any test binds | the remote `--testdox` output, verbatim, and the two database names it ran against |
      | **redis** | **yes** | the same `ssh`, with **`REDIS_DB=14` forced**, never the deployed `REDIS_DB` | **DB 14 only**, because the test flushes its configured database in `setUp`/`tearDown`. The runner **exports `REDIS_DB=14` explicitly on the remote command line** so the deployed value cannot leak in | the remote output **plus `redis-cli -n 14 INFO server \| grep redis_version`** — EC-28's whole point is the deployed version's `GETDEL`/Lua behaviour |
      | **shell** | **no** | `ssh <host> 'docker exec <api-container> <root>/apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/entrypoint-boot-status.sh'` | **read-only**: the script reads `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status` on both arms and writes only inside the container's own tmp | the file's contents on both arms |
      | **queue-dependent pg** (EC-16a) | **yes** | the same `ssh` as **pg**, with `QUEUE_CONNECTION` left at the deployed value and **Horizon's `identity-revocation` supervisor paused around the observation window** — the full sequence is below | the campaign tenant's own queue rows only; the job carries `tenantId` and the assertions filter on it | `horizon:status` and the paused supervisor before/after, the `jobs` row with its decoded `tenantId`, its retry, and the token row's disappearance |
      | **vitest / posvitest** | no | **LOCAL-ONLY, declared.** Pure unit lanes over `apps/web` and `apps/pos` source; staging runs a **built bundle**, not a test runner | n/a | the evidence row says **`local-only (declared)`** and names what staging cannot show: *"the device's cached-row logic is the same code in the bundle; a staging run would re-execute the same assertions against the same source"* |
      | **playwright** | — | **NOT replayed.** The campaign is single-run-per-fresh-tenant (**B2**) and the legs are `destructive: true`; a staging replay of R2 would need a second staging tenant and a second registration against the 5-per-window throttle | n/a | the ledger's `execution_target` is the byte-exact **`not replayed (declared)`** — the reason (a `destructive: true` leg under a single-run contract) lives in this cell, never in the ledger value (gate r4, Wave 4 **MINOR 3**); the staging assurance for those rows comes from their **pg** homes, which do replay |
      | **manual** | **yes, on a real device** | **the replay IS the staging run** — `docs/qa/RBAC-POS-DEVICE-REPLAY.md` performed against staging | the device signs in **as the campaign tenant's operator**, never a live tenant's | tester, date, and the observations quoted |

      **The replay's preflight is `staging_preflight()` IN `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh`** (Task 4-3 Step 10 item 2), called immediately after `campaign_lib_preflight` and **before the plan is built**, so no lane — mutating or not — runs until it passes.
      > **Rev 4 printed these checks here, as prose, beside a runner that never ran them** (gate r4, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 3**). The runner checked only that `--ssh`, `--api-container` and `--staging-tenant` were non-empty and then went to the HTTP health check: it never listed the tenant, never proved it disposable, never inspected either database, and never asked Horizon what it supports. **Rev 5 moves all of it into the runner and adds the two checks rev 4's prose did not have.** The five, with the `reason=` each exits 2 with:
      >
      | # | Check | `reason=` on failure | Why it is not optional |
      |---:|---|---|---|
      | 1 | **SSH reachability** (`ssh -o BatchMode=yes … true`) | `staging_ssh_unreachable` | everything below is an ssh call; without this, an unreachable host reads as *"the tenant does not exist"* |
      | 2 | the campaign tenant is **in the staging directory** (`tenants:list`) | `staging_tenant_absent` | a typo'd `--staging-tenant` would otherwise bind every lane to nothing and pass |
      | 3 | the tenant is **marked disposable** — **`php artisan rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}`, which EXITS 0/1** and prints nothing to grep | `staging_tenant_not_disposable` | **this is the check that cannot be skipped.** Binding a mutating replay to a real tenant is the one irreversible mistake this wave makes available, and *"it is a disposable tenant"* must be a **marker on the row**, not a promise in a runbook. *(Rev 5 named `rbac:row-census --field=is_disposable`; that command has neither option, runs under `tenants:run` and reads the TENANT database, while `is_disposable` is a CENTRAL column — plan gate r5 W4 BLOCKER 2. Rev 6 replaced it in the implementation and this table kept the old name until gate r6, **W4 minor 3**.)* |
      | 4 | the two isolated databases are **CREATED BY THIS PREFLIGHT** (dropped by its trap), each verified to hold **zero `public` tables** one statement after its `CREATE DATABASE`, and then **migrated** | `staging_db_stale` / `staging_db_create_failed` / `staging_db_not_empty` / `staging_db_migrate_failed` / `staging_db_tenant_migrate_failed` | rev 4 checked that **one** database **name** existed and claimed *"the databases are empty"*; rev 5 then required both to pre-exist and summed `pg_stat_user_tables`, which is a **statistics estimate** and reads 0 on a freshly-`ANALYZE`d table that holds rows (gate r5 W4 BLOCKER 3). **Creating them makes "empty" true by construction rather than by measurement**, and a leftover from an aborted run is refused unless `--drop-stale` names it. The database NAMES come from the pg lane's `env:`/`staging_env:` map, not from a literal in the script |
      | 5 | the **deployed** `REDIS_DB` is **not** 14, and `horizon:pause-supervisor` exists | `staging_redis_db_collision` / *(the Horizon arm is non-fatal)* | the dedup test flushes its configured Redis database in `setUp`/`tearDown`; if the application is on 14, *"flush DB 14"* is *"flush production cache"* |
      >
      > **It is EXERCISED, not asserted.** Every remote call goes through `SSH_BIN="${CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN:-ssh}"`, so the whole preflight runs against a fake `ssh` with no staging host at all — which is how rev 5 verified all five arms plus the happy path. A contract that can only be tested by owning a staging box is an untested contract.
      **The EC-16a staging sequence, because "assert the job appears" is race-prone against a live worker** (gate r3, Wave 4 **MAJOR 4**). A running Horizon supervisor can consume the `jobs` row before the test observes the pending state, and the test would then fail for a reason that is not the product's. **This is the sequence the runner IMPLEMENTS — `ec16a_begin` before the row, `ec16a_observe` after it and inside the pause, `ec16a_end` to resume — and rev 6's runner called `ec16a_observe` immediately after the pause and BEFORE the row, so it could not see the job the row produces** (gate r6, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 2**). The observer also ASSERTS now: it decodes each pending job's serialized command, compares the tenant id, prints `MATCHING_JOBS=n` and **returns non-zero on zero matches**, where rev 6's printed every row and succeeded on none. The snippet below is the sequence in readable form; the executable version is Task 4-3 Step 10 item 2:
      ```
      # 1. PAUSE only the queue this test writes to. Never `horizon:pause` (global) --
      #    that stops every tenant's fiscal projections for the duration.
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" | tee "$EVIDENCE/horizon-before.txt"
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:pause-supervisor identity-revocation"

      # 2. Run the forced-failure case against the ISOLATED databases and the campaign tenant.
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && DB_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign \
        DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_staging_campaign_central RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT=$STAGING_TENANT \
        php artisan test --testdox --filter the_central_delete_failure_queues_a_retry \
        tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountRevocationTest.php" | tee "$EVIDENCE/ec-16a-staging.txt"

      # 3. Observe the PENDING window while the supervisor is paused -- correlated by
      #    the job's own tenantId, never by "the most recent row".
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan tinker --execute='
        \$rows = DB::table(\"jobs\")->where(\"queue\",\"identity-revocation\")->get();
        foreach (\$rows as \$r) { \$p = json_decode(\$r->payload, true);
          echo \$r->id, \" \", \$p[\"displayName\"] ?? \"?\", \" attempts=\", \$r->attempts, PHP_EOL; }'" \
        | tee "$EVIDENCE/ec-16a-jobs.txt"

      # 4. RESUME, then observe the retry consuming it and the token row disappearing.
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:continue-supervisor identity-revocation"
      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" | tee "$EVIDENCE/horizon-after.txt"
      ```
      **The resume is a `trap`, not a last line** (gate r4, Wave 4 **MAJOR 2**). Steps 2 and 3 are fallible `ssh` calls; rev 4 resumed only after them, so a failed test, a dropped connection or a `Ctrl-C` stranded `identity-revocation` paused on a shared staging host — while the prose asserted the supervisor is always resumed. The pause is therefore installed with its own cleanup **before** the pause itself:
      ```
      # Installed BEFORE the pause, so it fires on success, on failure and on an interrupt.
      resume_identity_revocation() {
        local rc=$?
        ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:continue-supervisor identity-revocation" \
          || echo "WARNING: could not resume identity-revocation — resume it by hand" >&2
        ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:status" | tee "$EVIDENCE/horizon-after.txt"
        trap - EXIT INT TERM
        return "$rc"
      }
      trap resume_identity_revocation EXIT INT TERM

      ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan horizon:pause-supervisor identity-revocation"
      ```
      With the trap in place step 4's two explicit lines are **deleted** — running them and the trap would resume twice and write `horizon-after.txt` twice. **`horizon-before.txt` and `horizon-after.txt` must still match, and a mismatch is a RED**; the trap is what makes that assertion checkable rather than aspirational.
      **Four things this pins that rev 3 left open:** *which* supervisor is paused (not the whole of Horizon); *when* the pending state is read (inside the pause, not racing a worker); *how* the job is correlated (its decoded `tenantId`, not recency); and *what cleanup* runs (the supervisor is resumed, and `horizon-before.txt`/`horizon-after.txt` must match — a replay that leaves a supervisor paused is a **red**, not a pass).
      > **`horizon:pause-supervisor` / `continue-supervisor` are resolved at Phase 0** against the deployed Horizon version (`ssh "$SSH" "cd $ROOT/apps/api && php artisan list horizon"`); if this version exposes only the global `horizon:pause`, the replay **declares EC-16a `local-only` with that reason** rather than pausing the whole queue estate on a shared staging host.

      > **Check 3 is the one that stops a data-loss incident.** If the deployed application happens to be configured on DB 14, then "flush DB 14" is "flush production cache", and the replay must refuse rather than proceed.
      > **No lane gets to be silent.** Each either executes against staging with the isolation above, or declares itself `local-only` and states the staging evidence it cannot provide.

      **The invocation, and it takes a target that is a HOST, not only a URL:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4

      # Phase 0 RESOLVES these three and records them in the handback. They are not
      # placeholders in the dispatch brief (gate r3, Wave 4 MINOR 1 -- rev 3 left
      # "<the api container name>" in an executable-looking example):
      #   SSH_USER        from docs/deploy/ -- the staging runbook's operator account
      #   API_CONTAINER   ssh "$SSH" 'docker ps --format "{{.Names}}" | grep -i api'
      #   STAGING_TENANT  registered BY THIS REPLAY, disposable, recorded in the ledger
      SSH="${SSH_USER}@157.180.71.252"
      API_CONTAINER=$(ssh "$SSH" 'docker ps --format "{{.Names}}" | grep -i "api" | head -1')
      test -n "$API_CONTAINER" || { echo "could not resolve the api container"; exit 1; }

      scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --staging-replay \
        --api https://api.erp.otospex.dev \
        --web https://erp.otospex.dev \
        --ssh "$SSH" \
        --remote-root /var/www/autoerp \
        --api-container "$API_CONTAINER" \
        --staging-tenant "$STAGING_TENANT"
      ```
      **`--ssh`, `--remote-root`, `--api-container` and `--staging-tenant` exist because `--api`/`--web` cannot reach a lane that is not HTTP.** Without `--ssh` the runner exits 2 with `reason=staging_replay_requires_ssh`; without `--api-container`, `reason=staging_replay_requires_container`; **without `--staging-tenant`, `reason=staging_replay_requires_tenant`** — the last is what makes "mutating lanes touch only a disposable tenant" mechanical rather than an instruction. **None of the three silently defaults**, because the failure mode of a default here is a mutating test run against a real tenant.
      > **The runner writes `execution_target` into every ledger entry** — `local`, `staging:ssh`, `staging:http`, or `local-only (declared)` — and the evidence document's **Staging replay** table renders that field rather than a hand-typed *"yes"*. **A row whose `execution_target` is `local` in a staging-replay run is a RED**, not a pass: it means the lane had no staging path and did not declare one.

      **The replay list is generated** — every manifest row carrying `staging_replay: required` — rather than hand-maintained beside the manifest, so the two cannot disagree. **And rev 7 is where that became true** (gate r6, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 3**): rev 6's `plan()` was never passed `STAGING_REPLAY` and never read the field, so `--staging-replay` planned all 58 rows **and all nine `destructive: true` browser legs**. The planner now filters on the field, plans **no** leg for a generated replay — none of the nineteen rows references one, and `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` rule 6 makes that an invariant rather than a coincidence — and **`replay_selection_guard()` compares the planned row set to the declared one before anything executes**, exiting 2 with `reason=staging_replay_selection_mismatch` if they differ. An explicit `--row`/`--leg` still overrides the generated set, which is how a single row is replayed. The rows that carry the flag, and why each does:
      | Row | Why it must be replayed on staging |
      |---|---|
      | EC-8 | the cache flush is `DB::afterCommit` under a **real** queue and a real Redis, not a sync driver |
      | EC-16a | the central-side token delete failing and its **queued** retry — a sync queue runs the job inline and never proves the retry |
      | EC-19, EC-20, EC-20a/b/c | real two-connection concurrency against a real PG, not a single-connection local |
      | EC-21, EC-21a, EC-21b | compatibility mode and the fleet's `BLOCKED` aggregate line in the **container log** |
      | EC-28 | the denial dedup against the deployed Redis, at its actual version |
      | EC-32c | wave 0b's `EnsurePermissions*` family against the **real tenant directory** — a marked tenant only exists on a deployed fleet |
      | EC-32d | the entrypoint's boot-status file on both arms — a **shell-level** fact that only exists in the container, reached with `docker exec` per the lane table above |
      | EC-37a, EC-37b, EC-38, EC-39, EC-40 | the fleet runner against the real tenant directory, with a real not-ready tenant |
      | EC-31 | a POS device that has actually been offline, not a mocked `navigator.onLine` — **and this row's replay IS the manual one** (`docs/qa/RBAC-POS-DEVICE-REPLAY.md`), performed on a real device against staging, with the tester and date recorded |
      Fill the **Staging replay** table. **A row that cannot be replayed on staging says so and why** — that is a NOT_SCRIPTABLE with a reason, not an omission.

---

## Convention-09 obligations (spec §6, wave-4 row)

The campaign **is** the second-company/second-location journey; the axes are legs, not separate tests.

| Axis | Leg | Assertion (on **data meaning**, never on status) |
|---|---|---|
| **Second company** | `R0-fixture` + `R1-second-company` | A fresh tenant with **two companies**; a role granted in the tenant resolves **identically in both** while membership scope differs — asserted on the **rows** each company returns and on the switcher's contents |
| **Second location** | `R0-fixture` + `R6-location-scope` + the **manual POS replay** | A **second `pos_enabled` location in the second company**; an approval permission is honoured at the **selected** terminal's location and refused at the other. The terminal half is the documented device replay, not a Playwright leg (**M4-1**) |
| **Re-run / idempotency** | Task 4-5 step 2 | The **command-level** re-run against R0's tenant (**B4-2**): no duplicate role, permission or membership row — asserted by per-table counts before and after — and every `permissions:sync-fleet` **mutations** counter zero with `ALREADY_CURRENT`. The two **gauges** (`orphans`, `templates_skipped_customised`) report whatever is true of the tenant and are **unconstrained**; asserting a gauge at zero on a re-run is a misreading of the marker and produces a false red |

- [ ] **Every** id has a row in the evidence document with a `Result` that is **not** `NOT_RUN` — `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` entries each carry a stated reason
- [ ] **EC-22** is recorded as **withdrawn, number retained** — not as missing

- [ ] SQLite lane: the same directory's fast-lane half — green
- [ ] **Redis lane** (`REDIS_DB=14`): `AuthorizationDenialDedupTest` — green, including the concurrent-increment **outcome** assertion

- [ ] `apps/api/tests/EdgeCases/rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml` exists; `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` green — the manifest's id set **equals** the reconciled register list, every `command` resolves, every `lane: manual` row carries a `result` or a `doc`
- [ ] `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh` — **all nine browser legs PASS** plus every manifest row's command, ledger written, run id recorded; the runner **accumulated** rather than stopping at the first red
- [ ] `scripts/campaign-lib.sh` exists and **both** campaigns source it; `CampaignLibParityTest` green — the onboarding campaign's `--help` and preflight-failure output are byte-identical to before the extraction
- [ ] `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --reuse` with **no** `--leg` — **exits 2**, `reason=reuse_requires_leg`; with **two** `--leg` — **exits 2**, `reason=reuse_is_single_leg`; with an unknown id — **exits 2**, `reason=unknown_leg`; with **one** valid `--leg` — runs that leg and nothing else (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B2**)
- [ ] **The runner executes each leg ONCE.** `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh` end to end, then `grep -c 'R2-last-admin.spec.ts' "$LEDGER"` — **exactly 1**, while **four** rows (EC-1, EC-2, EC-3, EC-4b) carry its result. A leg executed once per referencing row would delete and deactivate principals four times on a single-use tenant (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B4**)
- [ ] **Every manifest row has at least one `homes[]` entry**, every `leg:` reference names a declared leg, every declared leg is referenced or carries `covers: []`, and every `command` resolves **relative to its lane's `cwd`** — `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest`'s six rules (plan gate r2, **BLOCKERs B3, B4**; **MAJOR 1**)
- [ ] **`campaign-rbac.sh` and `campaign-lib.sh` EXIST as shell, not as prose** — `bash -n` clean on both, `shellcheck -S error` clean on the runner, and all four flag contracts exit 2 with their stated `reason=` (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 3**)
- [ ] **The tenant-scoped rerun commands are VALID against the installed `tenants:run`** — `php artisan tenants:run rbac:row-census --tenants="$TENANT"` names a **registered** command, and `rbac:dry-run-probe` reports `write statements: 0` with exit 0. Neither is a quoted `tinker --execute=…` string passed as `{commandname}`, which cannot run (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 4**)
- [ ] **`--staging-replay` refuses without all three isolation flags** — `reason=staging_replay_requires_ssh` / `_container` / `_tenant` — and its preflight aborts if the campaign tenant is absent, the isolated databases are missing, or **the deployed application is itself on Redis DB 14** (gate r3, Wave 4 **BLOCKER 5**)

The file **parses**, and all four numbers come from the parsed structure, never from a grep:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w4/apps/api

          if (isset($h["leg"])) { $refs++; $rows[$r["id"]] = true; }
        } }
        printf("rows=%d withdrawn=%d executable=%d legs=%d legrefs=%d over %d rows\n",

## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the campaign and on every fix.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w4` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9
> **§7 in its entirety**, §6's wave-4 row and §8's wave-4 row, and against this
> plan and the committed evidence document. Cite `file:line` for every claim;
> verify against the tree and against the ledger, never from the plan alone.
>
> 1. **Is every register row accounted for?** Take §7's id list, take the
>    evidence document's first column, and diff them. Name every id in one and
>    not the other. `NOT_RUN` is a gap; `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` without a reason is a
>    gap.
> 2. **Does each row's home actually assert what the row says?** Sample fifteen
>    across all lanes. For each, read the test and say whether its assertion is
>    the row's expected behaviour or something adjacent. A test named for a row
>    that asserts a 200 is the failure mode to hunt.
> 3. **Is any assertion on status alone?** Convention 09 forbids it. Name every
>    one you find.
> 4. **Do the deny legs prove denial?** For each browser leg with a refusal, find
>    the assertion that the row was **not** written, and the toast asserted by
>    text. A blank screen is not a pass.
> 5. **Do the allow paths exist?** A campaign that only proves refusals cannot
>    distinguish a correct floor from a broken screen. `R2` in particular must
>    show one of the four operations succeeding once a second admin exists.
> 6. **Is the fixture real?** Confirm two companies and two `pos_enabled`
>    locations, and that later legs read their ids from the ledger rather than
>    hard-coding.
>  7. **Is the re-run assertion right, and is its narrowing declared?** Confirm
>    the **mutations** counters are asserted zero and the two **gauges** are NOT
>    — asserting a gauge at zero is a misreading of the marker and produces a
>    false red. Then confirm the re-run is **command-level** by design, that the
>    per-table row counts were recorded before and after, and that the handback
>    states the narrowing rather than leaving *"re-run end to end"* to be read as
>    a kept promise.
>  7a. **Does every register row have a runnable command?** Open
>    `rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml`, take its id set, diff it against the register
>    and against the evidence document's first column: all three must be equal.
>    Then pick five rows whose test an earlier wave shipped and confirm the
>    manifest's `command` actually resolves to it — a row pointing at a renamed
>    test is a row that never ran.
>  7b. **Does R4 branch?** Read Task 4-3 Step 4 and the evidence row. On a
>    RETIRED disposition it must assert registry, row and UI **absence** and
>    point EC-5's preservation evidence at wave 1's pre-retirement test. A leg
>    asserting a struck-through deprecated grant on a pruned tree is red for the
>    wrong reason.
>  7c. **Is there one runner or two?** Find the arg parsing, the preflights and
>    the ledger accumulator. If they exist twice, that is a finding — separate
>    journeys and separate gates are the design; separate plumbing is not.
> 8. **Is the staging replay honest?** For each row in the replay list, say
>    whether it was actually replayed or merely locally run. The queued retry
>    (EC-16a), the entrypoint status file (EC-32c/d) and the two-connection races
>    cannot be proven on a `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` single-connection harness.
> 9. **Were any expectations changed to make a leg green?** Diff the evidence
>    document against the spec's expected-behaviour column for every row. Any
>    softened expectation is a BLOCKER.
> 10. **Are the fixes each reviewed?** For every finding, name the fix commit and
>     the reviewer verdict. A fix merged on the campaign's own gate is not
>     reviewed.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`fiscal-pos-reviewer` — mandatory** for EC-31 — the `offlineRevocation.test.ts` vitest **and** the manual replay document. Ask: is the replay's sale a **real** sale through the device's own chain, or a synthetic API call? Does the replay **physically disconnect**, and does the vitest assert on **cached rows** rather than mocking `navigator.onLine`? Does a revoked operator's PIN stop being returned by **all three** surfaces? Does an `authority_stale` operator lose manager access? **And: is `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` honest here** — is there really no Playwright harness in `apps/pos`, or was one available and unused?

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory** for the nine legs. Ask: are all locators role/label/testid, with `.first()` on any fallback? Is `outputDir` outside the vite root? Are both captures registered on **every** leg? Does any leg assert a CSS class rather than rendered text?

**`inventory-costing-reviewer` / `treasury-reviewer` — mandatory only if a fix touches their modules.** The campaign itself touches neither.

## Handback

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w4-<ISO date>.md`.

It carries: the lane base and tip SHAs; the `Phase` ordinal used; **the reconciled register id list and count** (Q-w4-2) with the explanation for the 58-vs-57 difference; the local and staging run ids; the ledger paths; the per-lane wall times; the `tenant:census-day-one` output; every finding with its fix commit and before/after run ids; the Known-red list with owners; and the residuals below.

**And — because this is the programme's last wave — a one-page programme close-out**: the two ratchet ceilings' journey from wave 0a to now, the SoD baseline's final number, whether OQ-1..OQ-4 were ever ruled, and which residuals from waves 0a–3 are still open.

### Residuals

| # | Residual | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **R-w4-1** | **No campaign teardown.** Every run leaves its `tenant_<uuid>` database behind (db-per-tenant); cleanup is an operator task (`tenants:list` → delete the `rbac-campaign+…` tenants). The **push trigger stays INERT** until a teardown exists. | ops |
| **R-w4-2** | **Fixtures are Tunisia/pharmacy-shaped**, inherited from the onboarding campaign's constraints. A country- or vertical-specific authorization defect would not be caught. Parameterising the fixtures is a real, bounded piece of work. | QA |
| **R-w4-3** | Rows marked **NOT_SCRIPTABLE** — each with its stated reason. These are the register's honest edge: a scenario nobody can drive is a scenario nobody can regress-test, and naming them is better than a green tick. | QA |
| **R-w4-4** | Any **Known red** carried into promotion, named and owned, per the onboarding campaign's own convention. | owner |
| **R-w4-5** | The campaign is **on-demand**, not on push. Arming it needs the teardown (R-w4-1) and a target that can absorb one registration per run against a 5-per-window throttle. | CI owner |
| **R-w4-6** | **EC-17's 5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** is proven, not closed — `R5` asserts the exposure exists and that every mutating request is still refused. Closing it costs a websocket or a poll and is out of scope by spec §10. | out of scope |
| **R-w4-7** | Any wave-3 residual the campaign **re-confirms** rather than closes — in particular the batch-write location gap and, if wave 3 deferred it, the 22-key retirement. The campaign records their current state so the next reader does not re-derive it. | as filed |
| **R-w4-8** | **EC-31's operator-visible half is a manual replay, not a leg**, because `apps/pos` has no Playwright harness (**M4-1**). When a Tauri/POS driver exists, the manifest row's `lane` moves from `manual` to `playwright` and its `command` is filled in — **no other change is needed**, which is the point of the manifest. Until then the row is `NOT_SCRIPTABLE` with a named reason and a dated replay. | POS / QA |
| **R-w4-9** | **The same-tenant re-run is command-level, not end-to-end** (**B4-2**). Making the five mutating browser legs idempotent would need a per-leg reset contract and is real work; it is recorded rather than pretended. The master plan's three assertions — no duplicate rows, `ALREADY_CURRENT`, dry-run writes nothing — are all discharged. | QA |
| **R-w4-10** | **`scripts/campaign-lib.sh` touches a live promotion gate.** The extraction re-points `campaign-onboarding.sh`, and `CampaignLibParityTest` is what makes that safe. If the orchestrator prefers not to touch it at all, the fallback is the `CAMPAIGN_LIB_VERSION` contract with a follow-up lane for the re-point — **but two silently-diverging copies are not an option**, because the first thing to diverge is the exit-code contract. | orchestrator |

## Deploy

**Wave 4 deploys nothing of its own.** It adds a campaign script, a Playwright config, tests and two documents; no migration, no permission, no route.

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| — | **Fixes only** | If Task 4-4 produced production fixes, they deploy on the normal sequence: `permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` → `tenants:migrate-rolling` → `permissions:sync-fleet` → `permission:cache-reset` → `permissions:export-frontend-map`, with the standard health-check row. A fix that adds no permission and no migration needs only the code deploy. |
| — | **The campaign is run, not deployed** | On demand, locally or against staging via `scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --web … --api … --country TN`. **Do not arm a push trigger** (R-w4-1). |

**Promotion.** *A green RBAC edge campaign (read the **ledger**, not the exit code) plus a clean `tenant:census-day-one` is the promotion precondition for the whole roles-and-permissions programme.* A run is RED by construction while any Known red is open, and the promotion-checklist row accepts it **only** with those findings named and owned.

---

## Rollback

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 4-5 (evidence) | the ledger commit disappears; the promotion row points at nothing | the promotion-checklist row, in the same revert |
| Task 4-4 (a fix) | that fix's behaviour returns; **its EC row goes red again** | mark the row red in the evidence document in the same commit — an evidence document that says PASS for a reverted fix is worse than no document |
| Task 4-3 (browser legs) | the campaign script has no legs | Task 4-1's runbook's Legs table, in the same revert; **and `scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh`'s re-point**, or the onboarding campaign sources a library that no longer exists and **both** promotion gates go red |
| Task 4-2 (lane homes) | the new tests disappear; **the tests waves 0a–3 shipped are untouched** and keep their coverage | — |
| Task 4-1 (templates) | the evidence template and runbook disappear | everything above first |

**Reverting wave 4 removes evidence, not behaviour.** Nothing in this wave changes what the product does — except a Task 4-4 fix, which is why each of those is its own commit with its own reviewer and its own revert row. **The two exceptions to "no behaviour":** `scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh`'s re-point at the shared library (behaviour-preserving by construction, and `CampaignLibParityTest` is what proves it — revert them together), and the promotion-checklist row. **The one thing a revert cannot undo** is a campaign tenant: it stays on the target until an operator deletes it (R-w4-1).

## Change log rev 6 → rev 7

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r6** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r6.md`). Wave 4: **3 BLOCKER, 2 MAJOR, 5 minor**. All ten applied; nothing rejected. **Two further defects were found by EXERCISING the corrected runner** and are recorded at the end — both were in rev 6 and no gate had reached them.

| Gate id | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **W4-B1** | The staging wrapper does not transmit the manifest's environment — the runner exports it into the local subshell around `eval`, and the generated command is an `ssh` command; the remote wrappers set three assignments for pg and one for redis, omitting `DB_CONNECTION`, host, port, Redis host/port and `CACHE_STORE`, and the migration preflight does the same | **ACCEPTED, and it is load-bearing exactly as stated.** Verified against the tree: production sets `DB_CONNECTION=central`, the `central` connection reads `DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE` (`dev:apps/api/config/database.php:105-133`, and its own comment says so), `pgsql` reads `DB_DATABASE` (`:87-99`), and Stancl's `central_connection` is `env('DB_CONNECTION','central')` (`dev:apps/api/config/tenancy.php:50`) — so without `DB_CONNECTION` the deployed value wins and `migrate --force` migrates the deployed central database while the freshly created `autoerp_staging_campaign` stays unused. **The fix is mechanical generation, not a longer hand list**: each lane gains a `staging_env:` overlay beside its `env:`; `env_merge()` decides precedence once, `env_quote()` escapes once, and `env_compose()` / `env_docker_flags()` / `env_value()` are compositions of the two. `staging_wrap()` now contains **no database name, no host, no port and no cache store** — adding a key to a lane's map is the whole change — and the `shell` lane's `docker exec`, which inherits nothing from the ssh session, gets the same map as `-e` flags. **The migration is two invocations** against AutoERP's real topology: `database/migrations/` (the central set, 39 files) into `${central_db}` and `database/migrations/tenant/` (586 files, the path `config/tenancy.php:195-197` hands `tenants:migrate`) into `${tenant_db}`, both with `DB_CONNECTION=pgsql` from the manifest so the DEFAULT connection points at exactly one database per run. The database **names** in checks 4 and 5 now come from the same map rather than from literals. **Exercised on the fake-`ssh` harness; the generated remote command lines are pasted verbatim in Task 4-3 Step 10 item 6** |
| **W4-B2** | EC-16a observes the queue before the row creates the job, and `ec16a_observe()` asserts nothing, succeeds on zero rows, decodes no `tenantId` and binds no isolated environment | **ACCEPTED on every clause.** The invocation moves: `ec16a_begin` before the row, **`ec16a_observe` after it and still inside the pause**, `ec16a_end` to resume — the sequence the prose already documented. The observer now runs a probe that decodes each pending `identity-revocation` job's serialized command, compares it against `RBAC_CAMPAIGN_TENANT`, prints one line per job with `tenant_match=yes|no` and a final `MATCHING_JOBS=n`, and **returns non-zero unless n ≥ 1**, failing the row with a message that says what is unproven. It receives the row's own `env`/`staging_env` and composes the same prefix the row ran with, so it reads the isolated `jobs` table. **Exercised both ways**: with a matching job, exit 0 and `MATCHING_JOBS=1`; with none, exit 1 and the RED line |
| **W4-B3** | `staging_replay: required` is never consumed — the planner is passed neither the flag nor the field — and the manual branch executes before `staging_wrap()`, writing the forbidden `execution_target:"manual"` | **ACCEPTED, both halves.** `plan()` now receives `STAGING_REPLAY` and filters rows on the field whenever no explicit `--row`/`--leg` is given; it plans **no leg** for a generated replay, which is safe **because it is an invariant, not a coincidence** — none of the nineteen rows references a leg, and `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` rule 6 now asserts that a `staging_replay: required` row carries no `leg:` home. Without that arm the two orphan legs (`R0`, `R1`, both `covers: []` and both `destructive: true`) would have run on every replay and created and mutated a campaign tenant. **`replay_selection_guard()`** compares the planned row set to the declared one before anything executes and exits 2 with `reason=staging_replay_selection_mismatch` on a difference. The manual branch now goes through `staging_wrap`, so a replay writes `staging:manual` and the four-string contract is satisfiable for the first time |
| **W4-M1** | The committed evidence template is still a one-row stub | **ACCEPTED.** `apps/api/tools/generate-evidence-rows.php` is **supplied in full** and its output was produced from this plan's own manifest on 2026-09-11: **58 lines**, one per register id, in manifest order. `Row` comes from Phase 0's `rbac-register-ids.json` when it carries a description and is left blank otherwise — the tool never invents the register's wording; `Lane`, `Home` and `Count / value asserted` come from the manifest. Its **`--check` mode is the coverage assertion** (exit 2 naming the drifted ids), and the same equality ships as **rule 8** of `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest`. Exercised both directions: clean document → exit 0, `evidence rows == manifest ids (58 rows)`; one line deleted → exit 2, `evidence rows drift from the manifest: EC-20b` |
| **W4-M2** | Campaign fix subjects use non-numeric patch suffixes | **ACCEPTED.** `Phase 0.7.4a`/`0.7.4b`/… become numeric ordinals from **`0.7.6`** upward (`0.7.1`, `0.7.2`, `0.7.3` and `0.7.5` are this wave's tasks and `0.7.4` is Task 4-4's base), reserved through `0.7.31` — twenty-six slots, above which it is a re-plan. The `0.7` lane allocation is untouched. The note records that the patch number is an identifier rather than a running order, the same way `Phase 0.4.10` works in the wave-2 lane |
| **W4-m1** | Shared-library ownership prose remains contradictory in two active descriptions | **ACCEPTED.** The Task 4-3 shape paragraph and the runner's own header comment now say what the library contains — the two-arg guard, the two `curl --fail` preflights, run-id generation and its four exports — and state that the **ledger accumulator**, the **unknown-argument arm** and the **exit-code contract** are the runner's own, with the reason. The "nine flags"/"ten flags" disagreement between the two inventories is removed by pointing both at the library header's enumeration instead of carrying a count |
| **W4-m2** | The onboarding-script ranges are stale | **ACCEPTED and re-counted against the 63-line file.** Fiscal fail-fast `:50-55`; `set +e` / run / capture / `set -e` `:56-59`; the two path echoes `:61-62`; the final exit `:63`. The library header's `:41-46` also becomes `:41-45` (`:46` is blank) and its "not extracted" note cites `:63` rather than `:58-63` |
| **W4-m3** | Rev-5 staging-preflight prose survived the rev-6 implementation | **ACCEPTED.** Task 4-5's table row 3 names **`php artisan rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}`** and its 0/1 exit, and row 4 says the preflight **creates** the two databases, verifies each holds zero `public` tables one statement after its `CREATE`, migrates them, and takes their names from the lane map — with the five `reason=` values it can emit |
| **W4-m4** | `legs[].covers` equality is verified manually but not guarded | **ACCEPTED.** Rule 3 now builds the referencing-row set per leg and asserts **`covers` equals it** for every leg, sorted, with a message showing both sides; the old arm (an orphan leg declares `covers: []`) is the degenerate case of the same assertion. Re-verified against the pasted manifest: equality holds for all **9** legs |
| **W4-m5** | Stale spec and producer labels | **ACCEPTED.** Spec **rev 9.2**, wave-1 pin **rev 6.4 `70384d7e0`** |
| *(found by exercising)* | **The plan record was TAB-separated, and TAB is IFS whitespace** | **FIXED.** `bash`'s `read` collapses a run of IFS-whitespace delimiters, so an **empty** field vanishes — and `env` is empty for the sqlite, vitest, posvitest, shell, playwright and manual lanes. On every one of those rows rev 6 read `rows` into `env`, `cmd` into `rows` and left `cmd` EMPTY, so the lane ran nothing and reported PASS. The separator is now **US (0x1f)**, which is not IFS whitespace; the loop, the `printf`s and `replay_selection_guard`'s `cut -d` all use it |
| *(found by exercising)* | **An `IFS=` command prefix is in scope for the command's process substitutions** | **FIXED.** `staging_wrap()` is called inside `IFS=$'\x1f' read -r target run < <(staging_wrap …)`, so the env helpers split the lane's env list on US instead of on space and emitted `DB_CONNECTION='pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 …'` — one assignment carrying four others as literal text, with `DB_DATABASE` never set and `redis-cli -n ` empty. Every helper now pins `local IFS=$' \t\n'`. The same pass introduced `env_merge`, which is what lets the central-migration prefix override `DB_DATABASE` without emitting the key twice |

**Linting and execution evidence for this revision.** All bash blocks re-extracted: `bash -n` clean and **`shellcheck -S warning` clean** (the two SC2086 sites and the one SC2163 site carry `disable` comments stating why the split or the list-export is intended). All php blocks re-extracted: `php -l` clean. The manifest was re-parsed with `symfony/yaml` from `apps/api/vendor` and still reports `rows=58 withdrawn=1 executable=57 legs=9 legrefs=22 over 22 rows`, plus `staging_replay_required=19`, **`covers` equality for 9 of 9 legs** and **13 of 13 lane env entries matching rule 7's shape**. The runner was executed against a fake `ssh` on a six-row manifest covering every lane arm; the ten invocations and their exit codes are in Task 4-3 Step 10 items 5–6.

## Change log rev 5 → rev 6

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r5** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r5.md`). Wave 4: **5 BLOCKER, 0 MAJOR, 4 minor**. All applied; nothing rejected.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest` cannot pass — `dirname(base_path())` is `<repo>/apps`, so every rule-2 path became `<repo>/apps/apps/api/…`; rule 3 asserts all manifest row ids number **22** while rule 1 requires **58**; and rule 2 performs only file-existence and executable checks where it promised `--list-tests`/`--filter` resolution. `php -l` rescues none of this and rev 5's M4-closure claim was false | **ACCEPTED on all three.** **(1)** A `repoRoot()` helper: `REPO_ROOT` when set, else `realpath(base_path('../..'))` with a `dirname(base_path(), 2)` fallback — and the result is **asserted to contain `apps/api`**, so a wrong root fails as itself rather than as *"a sibling wave renamed it"*. **(2)** Rule 3 collects `$referencingRows` alongside `$referenced` and asserts **22 distinct referencing rows**, with a separate **58** assertion for the whole id set. **(3)** The PHPUnit arm shells out through `Symfony\Component\Process\Process` in the lane's own `cwd` — `vendor/bin/phpunit --list-tests --filter <f> <file>` — and asserts the listing is **non-empty**, because PHPUnit exits **0** on an empty selection, which is precisely how a renamed method would have run nothing and reported green. Re-verified against the parsed manifest: 58 rows, 9 legs, 22 references across 22 distinct rows, 1 `WITHDRAWN` (EC-22), no undeclared references, both orphan legs (`R0`, `R1`) declaring `covers: []`, all 19 `staging_replay: required` rows carrying a replayable lane, and **34** PHPUnit homes carrying a `--filter` for the new arm to resolve | Task 4-2 Step 4 (the whole test body and the note above it) |
| **B2** | The disposable-tenant preflight is impossible against the command the plan supplies: `rbac:row-census` has **no** `--tenant` and **no** `--field`, runs under `tenants:run` against the **tenant** database, and `dev`'s central `Tenant` has no `is_disposable` fillable, cast or custom column — so the fake-SSH happy path validated mocked text | **ACCEPTED, and the marker is built rather than assumed.** **The ruling: a dedicated command, not an option on the census**, because conflating the central and tenant databases inside one command *is* the defect. New **Task 4-2 Step 5a** supplies: **(a)** a **central** migration `2026_09_12_090000_add_is_disposable_to_tenants_table.php` adding `boolean('is_disposable')->default(false)` and its index — `NOT NULL DEFAULT false` so the safe value is *"this is a real tenant"* and there is no *"unknown"* third state; **(b)** the `Tenant` model edit in **three** places — `$fillable`, `casts()` **and `getCustomColumns()`** — with the reason the third is fatal to omit (stancl's `HasDataColumn` routes an unregistered attribute into the JSON `data` column, so the real column would stay `false` while the model read `true`; `is_sensitive` is the precedent, `is_demo` is the anti-pattern); **(c)** two console commands, `tenants:mark-disposable {id} {--unset}` and `rbac:tenant-is-disposable {id}`, the latter reading the central **table** (not the model) and returning an **exit code** rather than a line to grep; and **(d)** `TenantDisposableMarkerTest`, one of whose assertions reads the column directly, which is what proves the custom-column registration. `staging_preflight()`'s check 3 now branches on `$?` and its failure message names the marking command. **Nothing in the runner marks a tenant** — a runner that could would defeat the check | New Task 4-2 Step 5a; Step 6's add list and its note; `staging_preflight()` check 3; File Structure (created + modified) |
| **B3** | The staging database lifecycle promised by the lane contract is absent — the preflight requires both databases to pre-exist and reads `pg_stat_user_tables`, which is a **statistics estimate**, not an exact row count; it never creates or drops either | **ACCEPTED.** Check 4 now **creates** both isolated databases (dropping a same-named leftover only under the new **`--drop-stale`** flag, never implicitly), asserts the fresh database has **zero `public` tables** — a tautology check that proves the `CREATE` landed rather than an estimate that can lie — and runs `migrate --force` against them. A **`campaign_cleanup` trap**, installed on `EXIT INT TERM` **before** anything is created, drops every database this run created and resumes any supervisor this run paused. `pg_stat_user_tables` is gone. The preflight's success line now says *"both databases created empty"* | `staging_preflight()` check 4; the new `campaign_cleanup()` trap; the parser's `--drop-stale`; the usage block |
| **B4** | EC-16a's queue-safe staging sequence is not integrated into the runner — `staging_preflight()` assigns `HORIZON_SUPERVISOR_PAUSE` and nothing reads it; the `pg` wrapper runs every row identically; the pause, observation and cleanup exist only as a separate manual snippet. So `--staging-replay` executes EC-16a with the queue **running**, and the *"local-only when unsupported"* branch is never written to its ledger | **ACCEPTED.** Three runner functions — `ec16a_begin`, `ec16a_observe`, `ec16a_end` — bracket the EC-16a row **inside the execution loop**. `begin` returns non-zero when `horizon:pause-supervisor` is unavailable, and the loop then writes a **ledger entry** with `result: SKIPPED`, `execution_target: local-only (declared)` and the reason — so the declared branch is auditable instead of invisible. When it is available: `horizon-before.txt`, the pause, the pending-job read **inside** the pause, the row, the resume, `horizon-after.txt`, and a `diff -q` that makes the before/after match a **RED** rather than an aspiration. The resume is also owned by the `campaign_cleanup` trap installed before the pause, so an interrupt between them cannot strand a supervisor on a shared host | The three `ec16a_*` functions; the execution loop's bracket; `campaign_cleanup()` |
| **B5** | Task 4-3's exact commit cannot be made: `RetiredKeyAbsenceTest.php` stays on `WAVE_4_CREATES` while the task creates it, leaving the guard red; and the add list names a nonexistent `tests/CampaignLibParityTest.sh` when the supplied file is `scripts/campaign-lib-parity-test.sh` | **ACCEPTED on both.** The phantom path is replaced by `apps/api/tests/Architecture/RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest.php` — the parity script was **already** in the block one line above, so rev 5 staged it twice under two names and `git add` on the missing path exits non-zero, meaning the commit never happens at all. The coverage test is staged **because** this commit must delete `RetiredKeyAbsenceTest.php`'s line from `WAVE_4_CREATES`; the one-line diff is printed, with the note that this is the exemption mechanism working as designed — it shrinks in the commit that earns the shrink. The rev-3 changelog row's *"ledger accumulator, exit-code contract"* wording is corrected in place | File Structure (created + modified rows); the `campaign-lib.sh` header; the rev-2→3 changelog row |

> - **Eleven flag/preflight contracts executed** against the extracted runner on a three-row/two-leg harness with a fake `ssh` (`CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN`) and a stubbed `curl`:
>   `--reuse --leg R2 --leg R3` → **2** `leg_repeated` · `--leg R99` → **2** `unknown_leg` · `--row EC-999` → **2** `unknown_row` · `--lanes nope` → **2** `unknown_lane` · `--reuse` → **2** `reuse_requires_leg` · `--row EC-3 --lanes shell` → **2** `empty_selection` · `--lanes pg` → **1 entry, no leg** · `--lanes playwright` → **2 entries, both legs** · `--lanes pg --leg R2` → **1 entry, `LEG R2`** · `--staging-replay` happy path → `preflight: staging isolation OK`, pg lane at `execution_target=staging:ssh` · and the five preflight failure arms `staging_ssh_unreachable` / `staging_tenant_absent` / `staging_tenant_not_disposable` / `staging_db_not_empty` / `staging_redis_db_collision`, each **exit 2**.
> - **`php -l`** clean on `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest`.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | Selector validation can silently certify an empty campaign: repeated `--leg` overwrites, an unknown `--leg`/`--row` selects nothing, and the script exits **0** with `red=0` | **ACCEPTED.** Three mechanisms. (a) `--leg` is counted and a second occurrence is `exit 2` `reason=leg_repeated`. (b) `validate_selectors()` runs **before anything executes** and checks `--leg`, `--row` and every `--lanes` value against the manifest's declared ids — `unknown_leg` / `unknown_row` / `unknown_lane`, each naming what **is** declared. (c) An `executed` counter and an **empty-selection guard** after the loop catch a combination of valid ids that intersects to nothing: `empty_selection`, *"a campaign that executed no entry is not a green campaign"*. **All six exercised**, including the two rev 4 provably failed: `--reuse --leg R2 --leg R3` → exit 2 `leg_repeated`; `--leg R99` → exit 2 `unknown_leg` | Task 4-3 Step 10 item 2 (the runner) and item 3 (the exercised table) |
| **B2** | `--lanes` unexpectedly runs all nine destructive browser legs — the filter tests `kind == "LANE"` only | **ACCEPTED.** The filter applies to **both** kinds when `--leg` is absent, so `--lanes pg,…` (which names no browser lane) runs no leg and never creates or mutates a campaign tenant. An operator who wants a leg names its lane (`--lanes playwright`) or the leg itself (`--leg R2`), and the explicit leg overrides the lane filter. **Exercised**: `--lanes pg` → 1 entry, no leg; `--lanes playwright` → 2 entries, both legs; `--lanes pg --leg R2` → 1 entry, `LEG R2` | Task 4-3 Step 10 item 2's execute loop and item 4 |
| **B3** | The staging safety contract is not part of the supplied runner — it checks three flags are non-empty and then does the generic HTTP preflight; the printed database check proves only that one database **name** exists | **ACCEPTED.** `staging_preflight()` is **in the runner**, called after `campaign_lib_preflight` and **before the plan is built**. Five checks: **ssh reachability** (`BatchMode`); the tenant is **in `tenants:list`**; the tenant is **marked disposable** via `rbac:row-census --field=is_disposable` — the check that makes "disposable" a marker on the row rather than a promise in a runbook; **both** isolated databases exist **and hold zero application rows** (`pg_database`, then `sum(n_live_tup)` over `pg_stat_user_tables` — existence is not emptiness); and the deployed `REDIS_DB` is not 14, plus a non-fatal `horizon:pause-supervisor` availability probe that makes EC-16a declare itself `local-only` when it is absent. Every remote call goes through `SSH_BIN="${CAMPAIGN_SSH_BIN:-ssh}"`, so **all six arms were exercised** with a fake `ssh`: healthy → `preflight: staging isolation OK` then `execution_target=staging:ssh`; unreachable → `staging_ssh_unreachable`; absent tenant → `staging_tenant_absent`; `is_disposable=false` → `staging_tenant_not_disposable`; 4 127 live rows → `staging_db_not_empty`; `REDIS_DB=14` → `staging_redis_db_collision` | Task 4-3 Step 10 item 2 (the function); Task 4-5 Step 4 (the five-check table) |
| **M1** | The staging shell path conflates the SSH host's root with the container's | **ACCEPTED.** `--container-root` (default `/var/www/html`) is a separate flag and the shell lane runs `docker exec -w <container-root>/apps/api`, so a command is relative to the container's workdir rather than absolutely rooted at the host's path | `staging_wrap()`'s `shell` arm; the isolation-precondition table |
| **M2** | The Horizon recovery sequence can leave a supervisor paused — no `trap`, and steps 2–3 are fallible `ssh` calls | **ACCEPTED.** `resume_identity_revocation()` is installed as `trap … EXIT INT TERM` **before** the pause, preserves the failing status, warns loudly if the resume itself fails, and writes `horizon-after.txt` once. Step 4's two explicit lines are **deleted** so the resume cannot run twice | Task 4-5 Step 4's EC-16a sequence |
| **M3** | The R8 MCP probe still contains a placeholder and `curl -o /dev/null` discards the denial reason | **ACCEPTED.** The narrowed-out route is **resolved**: `GET /api/v1/roles` (`viewer` holds `roles.view`, the token's subset excludes it, the route is gated `can:roles.view` at `dev:…/Identity/routes.php:59`). The probe keeps the body and asserts `error.code = FORBIDDEN` with `error.ability = "roles.view"`, and the leg asserts the **unscoped** counterpart returns 200 — which is what distinguishes token narrowing from any other 403 | Task 4-3 Step 8 |
| **M4** | The manifest guard is six prose rules with no implementation, and several manifest homes intentionally name files Wave 4 creates | **ACCEPTED.** `RbacCampaignManifestCoverageTest.php` ships as a body covering all eight rules, with a **closed `WAVE_4_CREATES` exemption** that Phase 0 Step 5's delta populates and that each creating commit **shrinks** — an `assertFileDoesNotExist` arm forces the deletion, so the exemption reaches empty and rule 2 becomes absolute. `rbac-register-ids.json` (Phase 0's reconciled list) makes rule 1 an equality. | **M5** | The shared-library extraction claim is inaccurate — the library contains neither the unknown-argument arm nor the exit-code contract — and `CampaignLibParityTest.sh` has no body | **ACCEPTED.** The header now says what the library **contains** (three variables and two functions) and what it does **not**, with the reason: the unknown-argument arm must print the **calling** script's usage block and the exit-code contract is a runner's final status — a library function can own neither. ## Change log rev 3 → rev 4

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r3** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r3.md`), wave-4 section: **5 BLOCKER / 4 MAJOR / 2 minor**, plus the cross-plan re-pin. **Every one is applied, and this round was EXECUTE-THEN-WRITE**: the manifest was written and parsed, and the runner was written and run, before either was described.

### The executed evidence, first

**The manifest was validated by BOTH parsers before it was pasted:**
```
$ python3 -c "import yaml; d=yaml.safe_load(open('rbac-campaign-manifest.yaml')); print(d['version'], len(d['lanes']), len(d['legs']), len(d['rows']))"
2 8 9 58

$ cd apps/api && M=<path> php -r 'require "vendor/autoload.php";
    $d = Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml::parseFile($_SERVER["M"]);
    printf("symfony/yaml OK — version=%s lanes=%d legs=%d rows=%d\n", …);'
symfony/yaml OK — version=2 lanes=8 legs=9 rows=58
```
and its invariants were asserted on the **parsed structure**: `rows=58`, `withdrawn=1` (EC-22), `executable=57`, `legs=9`, `legrefs=22 over 22 rows`, per-leg `R2:4 R3:3 R4:6 R5:2 R6:2 R7:1 R8:4`, `legs[].covers` equal to the rows that actually reference it for all nine, every row with ≥1 home, every `manual` home carrying a `result`, and 19 rows flagged `staging_replay: required`. **The YAML was then re-extracted from this plan file and re-parsed**, so what is pasted is what was validated.

**Both scripts were syntax-checked and their flag contracts executed:**
```
$ bash -n scripts/campaign-lib.sh && bash -n scripts/campaign-rbac.sh    # clean
$ shellcheck -S error scripts/campaign-rbac.sh                           # no errors
$ scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --reuse           -> reason=reuse_requires_leg          exit 2
$ scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --staging-replay  -> reason=staging_replay_requires_ssh exit 2
$ scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --lanes           -> reason=missing_value               exit 2
$ scripts/campaign-rbac.sh --nope            -> Unknown argument: --nope           exit 2
```
and **the manifest→plan step — the one B4 is about — was run against the pasted manifest**: `9 LEG` lines (each leg once, in declared order, R4 carrying six rows and R2 four), `63 LANE` lines (distinct `(lane, command)` pairs), `2 MANUAL`. `ONLY_ROW=EC-16a` yields one `LANE` line; `ONLY_LEG=R4` yields one `LEG` line **and nothing else**, which is the `--reuse` single-leg contract executing rather than being described.

|---|---|---|---|

The malformed flow mapping is gone — every lane is a block mapping. The verification row asserts all six numbers from the parsed file, and **drops `yq`**, which is not a repository dependency | Q-w4-2; Task 4-2 Step 4; verification checklist |
| **B3** | The campaign runner is an implementation stub — six steps of prose, no shell for `--leg`, dedup, attribution, accumulation, SSH or `execution_target` | **Both scripts supplied in full and exercised.** `campaign-lib.sh` is the extraction; `campaign-rbac.sh` is the runner, with the manifest read through `symfony/yaml` (not `yq` — it is not installed on staging and is not a dependency), the plan emitted as TSV so dedup happens once where the whole file is visible, per-entry `execution_target`, accumulation rather than short-circuit, and a staging-replay entry whose target is still `local` forced to `FAIL` | Task 4-3 Step 10 |

The "no duplicate rows" assertion becomes a `diff` of before/after census output, which exits non-zero on any difference. `apps/api/routes/console.php` joins Task 4-2's add list | Task 4-5 Step 2 |

Dedicated `autoerp_staging_campaign{,_central}` databases for the pg lanes; **forced `REDIS_DB=14`** on the remote command line so the deployed value cannot leak in; every lane labelled **mutating or read-only**; and a **three-check preflight** that aborts the replay — including one that refuses if the deployed application is itself configured on DB 14, which is the check that stops a data-loss incident | Task 4-5 Step 4 |

| **2** | The extraction inventory contradicts itself — the created-files row claims `campaign-lib.sh` extracts `--reuse` and a ledger accumulator; the "not touched" section says `campaign-onboarding.sh` is never edited while Task 4-3 edits it | **Both corrected against the 63-line script.** The created-files row now lists only what `dev:scripts/campaign-onboarding.sh` actually contains, by line range, and says explicitly that `--reuse` and the accumulator are **new**. The "Deliberately NOT touched" paragraph is narrowed to the onboarding campaign's **specs, config and journey/selector modules**, with a note that the runner **is** edited and is in the Modified table | File Structure (Created + NOT touched) |
| **3** | The leg-reference arithmetic is false — 22 across 22 rows, not "31 across twelve" | **Corrected to 22 across 22 rows**, asserted from the parsed manifest with the per-leg breakdown printed. The rev-2→rev-3 change-log line carrying "thirty-one" is annotated in place rather than rewritten, since it is provenance | Task 4-2 Step 4 closing note; rev-2 change log annotation |
| **4** | The staging queue test is under-specified and race-prone — a live worker may consume the row before the test sees it | **Supplied the full sequence.** Pause **only** `identity-revocation`'s supervisor (never global `horizon:pause`, which would stop every tenant's fiscal projections), run the forced-failure case against the isolated databases and the campaign tenant, read the pending window **inside** the pause correlating by the job's decoded `tenantId` rather than by recency, then resume and require `horizon-before.txt` and `horizon-after.txt` to match — a replay that leaves a supervisor paused is a **red**. Added the Phase-0 check that the deployed Horizon exposes `pause-supervisor` at all, with `local-only` as the declared fallback | Task 4-5 Step 4 |
